- **A bench is the only thing in this kingdom a person may have in a room without a box on anything, and nobody asks a man why he is on it and nobody asks a man why he got up off it.** About forty people a week go through the river-court door and about one in ten is there about a matter of their own, and Tomas Vale has stood in that room four times in five days and had not once sat down, and worked out in about four seconds that a bench is where a man waits to be served and he was not there to be served. **Being a reader and being a person in a room are different, and the door with no reason on it is open to both.**
- **A bell that is not sounding and not broken is waiting, and there are about nine hundred of them in this city and every one of them is the hour.** Nine bells in a walk of about a mile on the flat, and the count of them is a figure nobody in the city keeps. Three questions at nine bells — *do you strike it, who does, what is it for* — produced an answer at four and nothing at five, and the answer at all nine was the hour. **A bell they have heard every day of their lives is not a signal to them, it is the hour, and a man who puts a tenth one among them and expects a different ear is a man who has not listened to a yard: on the hundred and sixth a yard on the top terrace let a bell ring four times with nothing on the plate and went in again, and the man who struck it said out loud that he does not know whether he is to and that there is nobody above him to ask. That is what a schedule sounds like from underneath, and every one of the nine hundred in this city is that yard.** Which is the finding of Batch 0004 against the batch's own plan: **the difficulty was never the form and it is never the reach, it is that in this city a sound is an hour, and there is no box anywhere in this kingdom that can tell a bell telling you the time from a bell telling you a true thing.**
- **A caster's mark and a day, and not one word about anything, is the plainest instrument in this kingdom, and it can be read in about four minutes by a man who is not touching it, and nobody in a city of about nine thousand stopped him.** A craft literacy, not a perception. **And the finding that came with it: the same absence of a reason-box that has let him into every room he has walked into in twenty-seven days is what lets him stand in a gateway for half an hour, and there is nothing in that to be frightened of and it is not a door either.**
- **A claim in metal cannot be edited and cannot be read by anybody who was not in the reach. A sentence on paper is read for forty years and produced against you by a stranger. HE HAS ONE OF EACH, THE ONE HE CAN DO NOTHING WITH IS HIS, AND THE ONE ANYBODY CAN USE IS THE ONLY SENTENCE HE HAS EVER MANAGED TO GET ABOUT HIMSELF.** The instrument that would carry a true thing out of him in one evening and put it in front of every door in this city is the one he has been refusing since before he left Kells, **and it is the wrong shape for it, and both of those are true at once.**
- **The only thing nobody can take off him is the only thing he cannot use, and that is not a punishment; a maker's whole life is work that cannot be edited and he has finally made one, and there is no plate in a printer's room two streets off that cuts it and the price of it is that it is worth nothing to anybody else including him.** The trade, not a feeling, and it is Batch 0004's answer to Batch 0003's finding that there is no instrument for it.
- **THREE SETS OF TERMS, TWO PEOPLE IN A ROOM, NO PAPER, NO PLATE, NO CLERK, NO COPY, AND THE THIRD PERSON'S TERMS ARE A SHEET ON A PUBLIC WALL SHE HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED ABOUT.** The arrangement is three people protecting a sentence none of them can read, and the three sets of terms are his, hers, and a published standing instruction made five days earlier by a woman who does not know the arrangement exists. **It is made without her and it will hold whether she ever reads it or not, and if she ever wants in she is the fourth person to a thing that was already standing, and there is no form in this kingdom for that either.** A man who is given a room is a man who owes, so he pays cash by the hour, and a man who pays cash is not a man somebody is kind to. **Nobody signs anything, nobody is thanked, and he is not the clerk of it and did not know until the middle of the hour that he wanted to be.**
- **AND THE ARRANGEMENT'S HARDEST CLAUSE IS HIS OWN AND IT IS NOT THE ONE HE EXPECTED: he will not ask either of them for anything.** Not today, not in a month, and not in the shape of a thing he has been wanting since the ninety-first; and if either of them does a thing he will not ask what it was for, will not write it down, and will not say thank you. **She told him it was a man being frightened. It is a term and he had it since the hundred and thirteenth and he would not have it improved.**
- **AN ARRANGEMENT OF THREE IS NOT A NETWORK AND IS NOT SCALABLE AND CANNOT BE DONE TWICE BY ONE MAN, AND IT IS NOT A WARNING, AND NOBODY CAN BE ENTERED IN A COLUMN BECAUSE OF IT.** A no said in advance is not an answer to a claim, because there was no claim, so **three noes and not one agreement, and the three of them are not an answer to a matter and not a count and not a bell and not a signal. HE HAS GOT ONE OF THE THREE ANSWERS AND NOT TWO AND NOT THREE, AND THE ONE HE HAS IS HIS OWN, and a man can be one of the three answers and cannot be two.** That is a rule about counting and not a power.
- **THE ARRANGING IS THE WHOLE OF THE DAMAGE, AND IT IS THE SAME SENTENCE AS THE ASKING.** On the hundred and fifteenth a man with a pen behind his ear wrote down four sentences of his for fourpence and it moved nothing, and the damage was that he asked for it. On the hundred and twentieth he paid for a room and put three sets of terms into it, and the damage was the same word with one letter changed. **Every time anything has moved in a hundred and twenty-six days the cause has been a man with no standing organising people, and not one of them has been thanked for it and not one of them ever will be, and the reason is four inches long: the instant a man with no standing arranges a thing, the thing stops being about anything except him.**
- **A MARKET ON THE GROUND IS THE ONLY PLACE IN THIS KINGDOM WHERE A PERSON CAN SAY A TRUE THING AND NOT HAVE AN OFFICE HEAR ABOUT IT IN NINE MINUTES, and the reason is a hill: from the top of it you cannot see the man at the bottom of it.** A man in a market of about nine hundred on the ground, four inches, at ten feet, in the ordinary voice, in front of everybody — **and the whole of the volume's best civic instrument has been a fact about the shape of the ground and not about a person, and it cannot be carried up a hill.**
- **A thing in a head is the only instrument there is that a person cannot be made to give up, and Tamsin Rook cannot be given one either, and the two of them are the only two people in this kingdom holding something that cannot be produced.** **And it is not a partnership and it is not a friendship and she is not going to put a name on it, because a name is a thing that can be asked about, and she has had enough of things that can be asked about in eleven years on a road.** Her instrument did four things this month and one of them is a man on a bench and she does not know which and is not going to find out, and she priced it in a smith's shop on the fifty-first and has not gone up on it in seventy-two days.
- **NOBODY IN SENNET IS WICKED, AND NOBODY IN A MARKET IS WICKED, AND NOBODY IN A YARD IS WICKED, AND A MAN WHO COMES ROUND ON A DISTRICT ROUND AND KEEPS A MAN IN A ROOM WITH A FORM IN IT IS WICKED IN NOTHING.** That held for every batch of Volumes 01 and 02, for Volume 03 Batches 0001, 0002 and 0003, **and for every page of Batch 0004, including Hob Kersey, who is right about his pitch, and Nyle Prout, who is right about the four boxes, and the woman with the bag of pins, and the woman selling needles, and the man in a boatyard who has been asked about his bell four times this year and says it is a bell.**

#### The law Volume 03 Batch 0005 added, and none of it is a stage and none of it is a power

- **A warning is a sound, and a sound in a city of about nine thousand people is the hour, and that is the door the volume's climax had to get through and it is the reason he refused the instrument.** He had carried the objection in his own hand since the hundred and fourth and had it in a book three times, **and on the hundred and twenty-seventh he found out that none of the reasons he had written down in three counties and a city was the reason, and the reason was that a tenth bell in among about nine hundred bells that are the hour is one more hour, and a true thing in that position is a false one, and there is no form anywhere in this kingdom that can tell the time off a bell that is telling you a true thing.** He had been angry at a yard for twenty-one days and had been angry at his own reason.
- **There is nowhere in this city to put a bell that is not somebody's, and the four things a bell is fixed to are a gate, a board, a post and a yard, and all four have an owner with a document.** And **a bell over a gate is a gate's bell**: it is the sound a gate makes so that a man inside knows something outside has moved, and the man inside does not strike it and the man outside cannot, and there is no form on which either may.
- **A return about a thing in a ward has four boxes — the ward, the day, the thing, and the keeper — and the fourth one is not the ground, and the keeper's box is filled on about nine hundred a year and empty on one, and it is the same about nine hundred as the matters on the boards in the next room.** A keeper's bell is a bell with a living keeper's hand on it and there is not one in this city. **A man with no standing could have filled that box in and did not, because putting a name in it is not filling in a form, it is a keeper accepting a duty for a household that has not been asked, and there is no box on the whole of the paper anywhere that says the household was asked.** That is the fifth clause's seventh job and it is the reason there is no household in this city he can put a bell in front of.
- **A man ringing a bell he did not make says whatever the bell's claim is and cannot add to it, and the claim in a district's frame is the hour, and a claim in metal cannot be edited and the maker cannot take the time out of it.** He struck a bell once in a yard on the top terrace at about the seventh hour and wanted to put four sentences in it and could not, and about nine minutes later a form four floors up had a line in it. **The only instrument that cannot be edited is the one he used, and the edit was not his, and afterwards there is no form in this kingdom that can tell which of the two a man had done.**
- **A SIGNAL IS A REAL SIGNAL AND A WARRANT IS WHAT AN OFFICE DOES WITH A REAL SIGNAL, and the Office is right about the procedure and wrong about the standing, and a man with no standing has no standing to be wrong about.** The warrant is a copy of a bell, and **it is not a forgery, it is a copy, and a thing that comes out twice is a claim and a copy of a claim, and a copy is how a sentence stops belonging to the person who said it** — which he had worked out about a woman in a room and then found on his own paper. **And a copy of a Crown sheet is that office's from the moment it is made.**
- **A district's fourth box is a ground, and the ground on a matter about a sound is not three words: it is the sound somebody made in a yard, and it is true, and it can be produced against the man for forty years by a stranger he will never meet.** It is the truest thing a form has ever said about him and he cannot get at it, cross anything out of it, or argue with it, and **he is not sorry for the bell.**
- **A CHARGE IS NOT A HEARING. A charge is a sheet and a sheet can be produced by a stranger; a hearing is a room where one person's answer goes into a column and comes out as the answer of the whole matter, and a room cannot be produced by anybody because a room has to be there on the day.** About forty people a week go through that door and not one of them is a person with a column, and one of them in a hundred and thirty-five days will say a name four times inside a year in four towns, and the man the charge is about does not tell him what the charge says.
- **A notice comes off a board when it is done, and that is not a defeat and not a rescue and nobody came down four flights to take it off; and the man who served a warrant properly is not relieved of anything for serving it properly.**
- **THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS KINGDOM THAT LETS A DISTRICT KEEP A MAN, AND THERE IS NO FORM THAT LETS A COURT KEEP A MAN EITHER, AND A MATTER GOES WHERE A MATTER GOES AND THE PERSON NAMED IN IT GOES WITH IT.** That is the whole of how he walks out of a city of about nine thousand people, and **it is a hole in a set of forms about four days old, and nobody opened it for him, and he is not going to thank anybody for a hole.** A schedule is part of a paper, and a paper goes with a man, and a schedule in a man's hands in a street cannot be taken off him because there is no box on any paper in this kingdom that wants a man to give one back.
- **A MAP IS A MAP AND NOT A KEY AND HE CANNOT READ IT, AND THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS KINGDOM FOR READING ONE.** About a third of the way along a run there is a rectangle about two hands together with nothing in it, **not torn and not cut: the paper is smooth and uncreased there, the column rules that rule the panels either side stop short and do not cross it, the ink either side is a slightly different black, the sheet has been folded and unfolded about nine hundred times and that is the one place on it that has not been folded through, and the numbers either side of it go on without a break.** It is in the same place. **He does not know whether that is a thing done to every copy or a thing done to the plate, does not find out in a corridor, does not write it down, and does not join it to the empty fourth box on a form in a printer's room two streets off or to the keeper's field ruled past the end of a line in a county he has left, because three empties in three counties is a man arranging a thing.** This is the object Volume 04 opens on and it is not explained in this batch.
- **A THING IN A HEAD IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THAT CANNOT BE PRODUCED, AND THE ONLY PERSON WHO CAN COPY ONE OUT OF A HEAD IS THE MAN IT IS IN, AND HE HAS NOW COPIED ONE.** He wrote the eleven lines out in his own hand, for the third time in a hundred and thirty-four days, and kept it. **The first was on a step on the hundred and fifteenth and the second was at a rail on the hundred and nineteenth and both of those were a man refusing to put his mother in a column, and the third was not about her at all: the only way to get the truest thing he has ever read out of his own head is to make it into the instrument he has been refusing since before he left Kells, and the price of having it in his hands is that it is in his hands, and a man who has carried the only unproducible thing in this kingdom for a hundred and thirty-four days has spent it, on purpose, on paper, for himself.** A copy of eleven lines out of a head is not a copy of a court record, is not a document in the register, and is not a fourteenth.
- **A FORCED RELAY RUNS THE LINE TO EXHAUSTION, AND IT TAKES THE WORST OF IT OUT OF WHOEVER IS HOLDING, AND A MAN WHO DECIDES IS A KEEPER AND A MAN WHO IS A CHANNEL IS IRON.** She was on a sill and a floor and a wall and she is the one who reads vibration, and she lost more hearing and nothing is restored and will not be. **He was given the choice and he was not asked, because he could not have been asked: she had decided before she said the sentence and she said it afterwards, and the reason she offered rather than asked is not kindness, it is that those are two different men and the volume has been teaching him the difference for three volumes.** He did not cut anything, he did not decide anything, and he is not the man who cut it and is not going to be forgiven for being the man it came through. **He could not keep his feet for about four minutes and could not hold a straight edge afterwards.**
- **THE CROWN'S THIRD BELL WAS RUNG BY A PERSON IN THIS BATCH, AND IT IS A CLERK, AND HE IS RIGHT, AND HE WAS STRIKING SOMEBODY ELSE'S INSTRUCTION.** A man came down four flights with a printed sheet and a number at the top of it, turned the plate over, struck it four times, did not read the sheet, and went up. **He is not the man who decided that the bell rings at that hour and there is nobody above him to ask and he did not ask, and about two hundred people came out of that yard and made no noise and went in again.** This kingdom still has no name for that kind of bell on a form and no chapter may coin one.
- **A MAN WHO HAS PUT HIS HAND FLAT ON THE SAME CROWN THREE TIMES IS A MAN THINKING ABOUT IT, AND THE FOURTH TIME IS THE LAST TIME AND HE KNOWS IT IS THE LAST TIME WHILE HE IS DOING IT.** Four acts: the hundred and sixth, the hundred and twenty-second, the hundred and twenty-fifth, and the hundred and twenty-seventh, on the evening he had built a mount with nothing to hang in it. **He did not write down what the last one was about, because a man who writes down why he has stopped doing a thing is a man who has started it again.** The carrier is still not rung and the interval is counted in days from the seventy-seventh.
- **BEING BELIEVED BY SOMEBODY IN THIS KINGDOM IS WORTH EXACTLY NOTHING: there is no form on which belief is entered, a person four days' road away cannot enter a paragraph in a column, and there is no form on which a man may be believed at anybody's desk and act on it. It is not a consolation and it is not a defeat.** And **a man in a chair who has read the thing that is about you, has decided what it means on his own account, has written down that he has decided it on his own account, and has not answered the paragraph you sent, and has told you he has not answered it so that you cannot say afterwards that he was told what it meant by the man it is about, has done the best thing anybody has done for you in a hundred and thirty-five days.** He is not thanked and is not owed a visit and none is coming.
- **THE THING THAT PROTECTS A MAN IS OFTEN THE THING THAT STOPS HIM, and both of those are correct at once and neither of them is irony; it is a shape.** A claim in metal cannot be shown to anybody on a page, and that is why no room in this kingdom has ever been able to ask him one question, and it is also why he cannot put one up in a street where a few hundred people would hear it. **A man who cannot be found cannot hang a bell where a street can be reached by it.**

## 7. Volume 01 canon that still binds

- **Surveyor roles — keep distinct, three men, and they must never be merged.** (1) **Pever**, the delivering and certifying Crown surveyor, Chapter 1, present and accounted for; the delivery was two porters plus Pever an hour later with a clerk, so **do not reintroduce "the clerk who delivered it."** (2) **The missing docket surveyor**, whose service entry was withdrawn, named on paper as **Iven Sore**, never seen, no living witness to the withdrawal. (3) **Surveyor Rhea Collis**, of the northern district, Halden's inspection surveyor, "delayed on the north road," arrived Day 8, states she is not the officer who certified the carrier and has never held the L-4 entry, and whose line on the roll bears a date and **no withdrawal**. **Collis must not be identified as the missing docket surveyor.** None of the other Crown officers, the black-case commission observer, or the filing clerks are surveyors.
- **Iven Sore's withdrawn service entry is still GENUINE-OR-BORROWED and must not be settled.** He can support that he has never held a Crown survey commission in this ward and that the Measure holds no copy of the entry; he cannot support the rest; and **he will not say he did not sign it**, on the ground that a denial made in a recorded room is a sentence others can later use, in either direction against him. **He is in Kells and nothing puts him on the road.**
- **A Crown lock register records a master key signed out under the name "J. Vale." A later maintenance tag reads "L-4, third watch, J. V." The echo is a clue and not a proof. NO witness, name, or suspect is attached to "J. V." in prose and none may be attached retroactively.**
- **A service ticket in the partly cleared survey store says the clapper was removed and the carrier disconnected after a warning was filed, the route held for maintenance review, and NO KEEPER ANSWER RECORDED.** The ticket's second line — *answered in office, L-4 ward roll, per local practice*, with the mark **OFF. 411** — is read out again in Chapter 50 and **still has no person behind it.** **OFF. 411 is a mark on four papers in four hands and a district renewal desk. It is not a man, it has not been used in any chapter from 51 to 100, and no person in it is named.**
- **Tamsin Rook's handbell was recovered from the cooper's loft after a child opened the north-gate lockbox with a maintenance key. It is no wider than her palm across the crown and was physically altered to widen a Low Ward signal beyond its authorized route. She retains it.** The authorized run goes from the north gate down through the Low Ward to **the low drain gate at the end of the landing**, which is the last thing the route touches, and the bell may not repeat past that gate, where the flood bell takes up the local signal. **Use the drain gate whenever the boundary is named specifically**; "as far as the river landing" is a loose description of the same run and is not a different route.
- **Two different things, both on the page, and they are not the same event.** (1) The **watch-supervised reading of the courier bell's wear** was done in the Bellhouse yard on **Day 6** with a Crown guard present and Jerm holding the book, and it is done. (2) The **comparison Oren arranged in Chapter 3** — the courier handbell against the fixed Crown carrier — **was made at last in Chapter 50 on the thirty-third at the third hour of the morning, at the custody review, as a new custody event with its own receipt and witnesses.** It shows **a technique, a bench, a trade, a Crown pattern, and about a year between the two — a technique and not a person.** Both bells were altered at two removes from a Crown pattern: the handbell by a chisel about a sixteenth of an inch inside the crown with a **riffle file** mark at four points, the carrier by a coupling cut nearly through with a thin copper jumper across it. **This does not answer how an office gets into a bell, does not name the Crown clapper, and is not the mechanism.**
- **The RECKONING 619 chronology stands and is untouchable.** The bench-book for the year Reckoning 619 — about three hundred and eleven pages, and the only record in the world of what a bellwright's shop wrote in the year of the Hush — **was burned in the ash-pit in the yard in daylight on the twenty-eighth at about the fourth hour, in three goes, the page numbers and the hour entered by the copyist. No surviving copy exists or may be invented.** **No chapter from 41 to 100 adds a fact about the night of the Hush that a witness could not have, alters the Hush, Ewan Pike's death, the rope cut, the thin sound entered in Chapter 16, or the Crown's removal of independent keepers.** Oren's Day 11 statement that he has not read the gate book for that night and will not let any man of his watch read it aloud **is a record about a record, not about the night**, and it is not restated as a fact about the night.
- **Nell Fenn's alignment is SPENT.** The nine *not returned* lines and the six *accompanying* lines went to the district on the thirtieth as a checkable question, and the district's reply names the respondent as **a person of sixteen of the household of line twenty-two** and says it does not propose to ask why. **It must not be reported as unspent and is not available to anybody.**
- **The fifteen unanswered L-4 lines and the district's written promise of the thirty-second are still OWED and still UNASKED.** Tomas has two district offices he can walk into and a clerk's hall he can stand in front of, and **deliberately uses none of them for this.** **No chapter from 51 to 100 answers it and no state file may report it answered.**
- **The L-4 notice for the Low Ward was filed three days before the carrier rang, headed "dawn, seventh day from posting."** The inclusive counting rule is the market-court clerk's own addition on his public board in Chapter 6; **Chapter 6 states no elapsed time for how long the notice has hung, and no chapter invents one.**
- **Nell Fenn is sixteen. Her younger brother Jory is eight and her father Edan is also missing.** Chapter 5 fixes them at **Day −5 and Day −4** on Nell's witness-relative count, both preceding the Day −3 notice. **Do not let any character refer to a posted L-4 notice before Day −3, and do not call the earlier round the L-4 round.**

### 7a. The figures, recorded as the chapters give them

- **Four hundred and eleven** is the population of the Low Ward throughout Chapters 22–50 and is separate from the Crown's **two hundred and six** and the market court's **one hundred and forty and one** and the difference of **sixty-five** entered in the day-book as *whose office is counting*. **The 411 figure is not reconcilable with 206 + 141 and no character attempts to reconcile it. The forty-one and the fifty-three remain two unrelated records in two offices.**
- The ward's answer of the twenty-fifth is **three columns against the forty-one lines of the L-4 schedule**: one household in the first column, twenty-five in the second, seven in the third, and eight lines belonging to households not in the ward.
- The Day 28 four columns are **nine households that moved, twenty-one that stayed, eleven children hidden in eight places, four doors that refused.** Chapter 50's day-book gives the same four figures.
- **The six named people arrested on the twenty-eighth are Gib Hask, Mrs Wilde of 108, Wenna Cray, Ivo Prentice, Louisa Tace of 44, and Absalom Teage** — the last of them the man whose own return saved his son. **It was six persons on named grounds and not a broad sweep, and all six came off the district's amended return of the seventeenth in Oren's own hand.** **Absalom Teage then began asking one question at each door — *what did you do on the twenty-first, and who saw you do it* — and got to nineteen doors without being told anything by anybody.** No age for any of them.
- **Pell Rill's figures, corrected on the page in Chapter 79 by a district officer who is right, and the correction is not a solution:** about eleven thousand held lines, four hours a day, eight hundred hours, eleven pence an hour, about forty pounds a year, **about three hundred and sixty pounds a year across the nine districts that adopted the practice. There are nineteen under the county, the other ten spend it and spend more, and if all nineteen stopped it the figure would be about seven hundred and sixty pounds a year, which is about the price of a small bridge and is not money anybody finds in a place anybody looks.**
- **About forty-four thousand names in four counties against about eleven thousand lines a year.** It is being made about eleven thousand a year faster than anybody is unrolling it, and **there is nobody unrolling it, and there is no form for a list and there never has been.**
- **Ivor Salter's yard-book, thirty-one years.** Six households in Miremarket have a hand in it, **fourteen are dead**, and about nine hundred people in the town could each remember a thing he said at a gate, **and none of them can be paid.** The estate is about three hundred pounds; the six claims total **about fifty-one pounds**; the money is in a sealed bag about four months old on a table and **cannot move.**
- **Marden Vey, of Vey and Lock**, is owed about ninety pounds, about forty, about eleven and four, and about six and four by four families, and **was refused four times in four months by men with money in a town of two thousand, all of them right and none unkind, and he is going to lose a yard.** About nine men on the books, three boats in a season, four partners of whom two are his nephews and one has been in the yard since he was a boy. **He has read the approving return and thinks it is right and has said so.** No age for the four partners, the six households, or the merchant's clerk.

## 8. Boundaries — what must not be reached, checked through Chapter 170

**Each of these was checked in every batch of Volumes 01 and 02 and again in Volume 03 Batches 0001, 0002, 0003 and 0004, and holds. The ones marked with a chapter were re-checked against the page of that chapter and not against a summary.**

- **CHECKED AGAINST CHAPTERS 161 TO 170 AND UNCHANGED, AND THE RANGE OF THE WHOLE NOVEL'S FOUR BELL CLAIM IS NOW 51 TO 170.** **THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL WAS NOT STATED, PROPOSED, HINTED AT OR FORESHADOWED IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, AND A WORD-BY-WORD SWEEP OF `chapters/volume-04/` FOR *displac*, *administrative*, *invisible*, *registering*, *settlements*, *line of villages*, *forced answer* AND *copied answer* RETURNS NOTHING. THE CHAPTER 0145 SEED IS NOT CARRIED A THIRD TIME: no character in Chapters 161 to 170 talks about his father's line, his father's work, a line that came up through a wall, or a day a line stopped answering, and no character calls himself the second man in anything, and Wend Dreeve's refusal from Chapter 0160 stands unchanged and is not repeated. The place on nobody's map is plainly there for ordinary reasons a village gives and four of those reasons are on the page in mouths, and no chapter connects it to a paper, a column, a district's book, a household or a return. THE WORD *REGISTERED* IS NOT USED OF ANY PERSON IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** **NO CHAPTER OF THESE TEN ENTERS A NAME ANYWHERE, AND CHAPTER 0169 SAYS SO ON THE PAGE AS A DECISION ABOUT HIMSELF AND NOT ABOUT THE TOWN.** **THE SIX RESERVED MYSTERIES ARE ALL STILL OPEN AND NONE OF THEM IS NAMED AND NONE WAS ADVANCED: no mechanism is opened and no person is shown one, the word *clapper* is absent from Chapters 151 to 170 measured, and a store, a counter, a number and a chain are provenance and not mechanism.** **Tamsin Rook's hearing is not restored, not rescued, not asked about, not held over her and not made a spectacle, and she is not further reduced in this batch, and nobody asks her for a reason at any hour of the ten days including him, and her one entry in this batch is in her own hand and is about a day where nothing new happened.** **Dain is not named. The First Answer chamber is not opened, described or approached. No Crown office is defeated and no officer is relieved of anything and the warrant stands. No bell he made is unbarred. The Reckoning 619 bench-book and the Vale Bellhouse archive are not restored and no copy of either is invented. Salla Keene is not found and no chapter of this batch finds her by a document. Iven Sore is not in any chapter from 101 to 170. Mara Vale is not named, referred to or written to, and Oren Pike is not named, and there is no second letter from Kells, and the word *letter* does not appear in any of the ten chapters. There is no trio, *if there were three of me* is not written in any of them, and the leash is at eight uses with no ninth staged and the slate not named in any of them.** **NONE OF THE FOUR BELLS — the trial handbell, the millrace bridge bell, the market court call bell and the wheelwright's counter bell — IS NAMED AS A BELL IN ANY CHAPTER FROM 51 TO 170, and no custody event was staged for any of them in this batch, and the claim is about the bells and not about the trades.**
- **CHECKED AGAINST CHAPTERS 151 TO 160 AND UNCHANGED. HOW AN OFFICE GETS INTO A BELL IS PARTLY ANSWERED IN VOLUME 03 AND IS NOT ANSWERED, AND IT WAS NOT ADVANCED BY VOLUME 04 BATCH 0001 AND MAY NOT BE BY A LATER BATCH.** A smith named a coupling, a jumper and a bracket, said they are not the bell's own, said on the page that he does not know what they do and neither does the man at the counter, and said there is a book on the counter. **That is provenance and not mechanism, and it is the same shape as Chapter 0103 and Chapter 0109 and it advances nothing.** In Volume 02 it was said four times in Batch 0005 and every one of the four was a man saying he did not know it. **In Chapter 0109 a clerk at a counter in Sennet named the second kind of paper — a fitting, an instrument, or a box with a handle, issued out of a store against a district instruction with a number, which when fitted makes the answer for you in your own voice and is not a lie — and in Chapter 0103 a smith named the store and its four parts and refused a theory four seconds before Tomas could take one. THAT IS PROVENANCE AND NOT MECHANISM. Tomas has still not seen one, has still not seen a shelf, and says in Chapter 0110 that a description of a thing is not a thing and that he is not going to write down the name of a fifth thing he has not seen. THE CROWN CLAPPER IS NOW NAMED ONCE IN THE WHOLE NOVEL, IN ONE DOCUMENT, IN CHAPTER 0125, IN LIRA VALE'S OWN WORDS IN HER OWN TRANSCRIPT, AND NOWHERE ELSE. NO FITTING IN ANY CHAPTER FROM 101 TO 140 MAY BE CALLED ONE, NO PERSON IS SHOWN ONE, NO FITTING IS DESCRIBED, AND NO MECHANISM IS OPENED. AND THE WORD ITSELF APPEARS NOWHERE IN CHAPTERS 131 TO 140: it was drafted five times and removed five times before the files were saved, and the object is described instead as nothing in it that can strike.**
- **The Crown clapper, the higher official and any person in OFF. 411 are UNNAMED. OFF. 411 is not named or used in any chapter from 51 to 150, and the sweep across Chapters 141 to 150 returns nothing.**
- **The name-transfer mechanism is UNTOUCHED and is not described in any chapter.** A chapter says a number is a filing reference and not a man, **which is a statement about a number and not a mechanism. No copied signature is explained, no transferred name is explained, and no child is found by a document.**
- **The Silent Muster is now PROVED, in Chapter 0107, by a document and a date and not by a bell, and it is one clause of eleven in a printed standing instruction, and it names no enemy.** What it did NOT do is set out at §11 and is binding: no signature on the sheet, only a store number; six clauses unread and unguessed; no account of what happens to a child once taken; and no mechanism opened. **The word *muster* is on the page exactly once in the whole novel, in the name of the operation as Chapter 0107 gives it. THE WORDS *Renewal* APPEAR IN THE HEADING OF THAT INSTRUCTION AND NOWHERE ELSE IN CHAPTERS 101 TO 130, AND THE ONE FURTHER OCCURRENCE IN THE WHOLE OF VOLUME 03 IS CHAPTER 0145:11, WHERE A DISTRICT'S TRENCH IS DUG *FOR A RENEWAL THAT HAS BEEN DONE*. That is a public works programme in lower case and it is not the operation, and no character connects the two, and the clause is not touched and the trench is not touched. A state file that says Batch 0005 uses the word nowhere was wrong and is corrected here. The one chapter that carries the eleven clauses and clause seven is Chapter 0107 and it is the only one.**
- **Iven Sore's withdrawn service entry is untouched, still genuine-or-borrowed, and is named in Chapter 100 only as a question this volume did not settle. He is not in any chapter from 101 to 150 and nothing in any of Volume 03's five batches puts him on a road.**
- **The Day 24 request is not re-opened** and the refusal is not treated as a wound.
- **The Reckoning 619 bench-book is not restored and no surviving copy is invented.**
- **No Master Key, charter, commission or Crown licence is brought into existence as a solution.** The only instrument of that kind in Batch 0005 is **a form for papers**, and its authority is a copy of a Crown sheet being Crown property from the moment it is made, **which is a fact about copying and not a charter. THE SAME IS TRUE OF CHAPTERS 101 TO 110, AND THE ONE NEW OBJECT IS A PRINTED SLIP AND A FORM FROM THE QUIET OFFICE'S PUBLIC COUNTER, WHICH HE CARRIES AND CANNOT USE, AND A SLIP AND A FORM ARE NOT A CHARTER.**
- **LIRA VALE'S FATE IS NOT ANSWERED AND IS NOT APPROACHED. THE RECORD OF HER MATTER IS ON THE PAGE IN CHAPTERS 0124 AND 0125, IT IS ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED WORDS PLUS ELEVEN LINES IN HER OWN WORDS, AND IT DOES NOT SAY WHETHER SHE WAS IMPRISONED, KILLED, OR USED AS AN ANCHOR — NOT IN HER WORDS, NOT IN A CLERK'S MOUTH, NOT IN A MARGIN, NOT IN A FORM, AND NOT BY ANY CHARACTER INFERRED ALOUD. NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 03 MAY MAKE IT SAY IT, AND CHAPTERS 141 TO 150 DO NOT: **the absence is not referred to at all in this batch, and the eleven lines are quoted once, in Chapter 0148, as a copy in his own hand, and the copy stops in the middle of a subject the way a record stops, and neither the chapter nor any character in it says one word about what became of her and no character reasons it out loud.** She is Volume 05's finding and Volume 08's, and `outline/ending.md` keeps her load-bearing for both.**
- **WHY THE OLD NETWORK ACCEPTS A CROWN CLAPPER IS NOT STATED IN ANY CHAPTER OF BATCH 0005, AND NO CHARACTER MAY STATE IT AS A CONCLUSION. IT IS NOT NAMED AS A QUESTION IN ANY OF CHAPTERS 141 TO 150.** IT IS SEEDED EXACTLY ONCE, IN CHAPTER 0145, BY A MAN STACKING SACKS IN A YARD WHO IS NOT A KEEPER AND WHO WAS NOT THINKING ABOUT IT: *when I was a boy there was a line that came up through the bottom of that wall and it answered, and there was a day it stopped answering and my father went looking for it and he never told anybody what he found* and *the ones above him stopped too, and when the ones above you stop, the ones above them go quiet, and the last one out is always a building with a bell on it that has to go at the hour.* **THAT IS A MAN TALKING ABOUT HIS FATHER AND NOT A FINDING. The chapter says so on the page, and Tomas writes down that he did not ask why a route that answers is a route that does not have to be argued with, and that he has not come away with it and is not going to go and look for it. IT IS NOT CONCLUDED, IT IS NOT NAMED, AND IT IS THE OPENING PRESSURE OF VOLUME 04.**
- **Tamsin Rook's hearing is permanent and IS WORSE AT THE END OF THIS VOLUME THAN AT THE START OF IT.** She is not rescued, no chapter has her discovered she has been exposed, nobody holds it over her, nobody treats it as a casualty of his decision, **and no person asks her for a reason at any hour in the volume, INCLUDING IN CHAPTER 0146, WHICH IS THE CHAPTER SHE LOSES IT IN, AND INCLUDING HIM** — including a district officer who read a book of refusals for four years and could have, a clerk who stood six feet from her for two hours and did not speak once, a market keeper, a man with onions, two clerks, a merchant, and the man who said the thing into the record. **She rang nothing, and the bell is in her bag and it is hers.**
- **THE DAY 21 CANON CHANGE stands and is used rather than revised.** Her deafness is public **by her own act**, on the ground that a thing said once by the person it belongs to is a record and a thing found out in a lane is a rumour. What is not changed: she has rung nothing, the bell is hers, the route is hers, nothing has been rescued for her. What is new and live: **her deafness is on the ward's record, and any Crown or private party who wants her bell can now price it correctly without her permission.**
- **The Volume 01 midpoint reversal is CLOSED and must not be re-announced, re-explained or re-discovered, and is explained to no new listener.** The chapters that carry it as an already-known fact are **30, 33, 42 and 50**. **In Chapter 0107 Tomas refers to it in one clause — a district form dated about a year after the notice was pinned up in my ward, and about a week after my bell rang — and does not explain it to anybody, and that is the whole of the traffic and it is correct.**
- **No new final enemy is introduced and no Crown office is defeated. No coronation, no cure, no trial won, no bell he made ever unbarred.**
- **Nell Fenn's count remains spent. The fifteen L-4 lines and the district's written promise of the thirty-second remain owed and unasked.**
- **THE ROUND DOES NOT COME BACK.** Miremarket's round came in on the sixtieth at about the eighth hour of the morning, was out again on the sixty-third, and closed at the end of the sixty-sixth with four pitches left on it. **A review of Batch 0003 found a chapter putting it out again on the seventy-first, eleven days after it shut, and that is the state error this rule exists to stop. No chapter may put a man with a book on a stool at a door in that town. A cart and a man and a day is the only instrument in this kingdom for a line that cannot be shut on paper, and a chapter that needs one stages the event itself.**
- **The loose leaf in Amos Trewin's stock book was opened once, on the fifty-second, in front of Trewin, and it did not confirm itself. THE ONE PERMITTED OPENING IS SPENT. No chapter opens it again and none confirms it.** **It is a leaf of unknown hand. Older state files called it *Mara's leaf*; the prose never makes that attribution and the page does not support it.**
- **Mara Vale's trail is not mentioned in any chapter of Volume 02, and the volume's seventh mystery item is not paid.** See §11. **IN BATCH 0004 SHE MAKES A CHOICE ON THE HUNDRED AND EIGHTEENTH, IN A PUBLIC ROOM, IN THE OPEN, AND TOMAS DOES NOT READ IT — see §6b.**
- **The five dates in a drawer are unaccounted for, are not explained away, and are not called a forgery or a conspiracy.** They have been touched four times since Day 82 and **they were not touched at all in Batch 0004.** See §11.

## 9. Counts discipline — the figures that must never be restated as each other

- **THE REGISTER IS THIRTEEN AT THE END OF CHAPTER 0150 AND BATCH 0005 CREATED NO FOURTEENTH AND REMOVED NONE, and for the fifth time running that is not a loss. Four things came into his hands in this batch and not one of them is a fourteenth: a schedule folded in three, which is a district's and part of a paper and a map and not a document; a leaf in his own book with eleven lines on it, which is his and is a copy out of a head and is not a copy of a court record; a letter from Kells, which is a letter and is somebody's and he has not counted it; and a form on a table in an empty room that he read and took nothing out of.**

- **THE REGISTER IS THIRTEEN AT THE END OF CHAPTER 0170 AND VOLUME 04 BATCH 0002 CREATED NO FOURTEENTH AND REMOVED NONE, for the seventh time running, and that is not a loss. WHAT CAME TO HIM IN THE TEN DAYS IS WRITTEN OUT IN CHAPTER 0170'S DAY-BOOK AND NONE OF IT IS A DOCUMENT: a wheel is a reason for a stranger to stop and two of them stop about three times as many; a new road on the top of a bank and the man who used to come past underneath it; four boxes and one more that a man with a barrow cannot get a hand into; a man with a shovel doing the worse of two jobs so that the better one can never be pointed at; and three words for one place and not one of them matched to a sheet he cannot read. NOTHING WAS ASKED FOR, PRODUCED, GIVEN OR TAKEN ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS AND EVERY DAY-BOOK SAYS SO. THE ONE EXCEPTION IS RECORDED AS AN EXCEPTION AND NOT AS A FOURTEENTH: in Chapter 0164 the schedule came out of his coat, was held flat on a kerbstone in the open for about nine seconds, and went back in, and A DRAWING IS NOT A DOCUMENT AND THE REGISTER HAS CARRIED IT AS A MAP AND NOT A DOCUMENT SINCE DAY 134. NO EIGHTH REFUSAL WAS STAGED: THE BATCH STAGES TWO EXPLICIT REFUSALS OF A QUESTION (a man with a dog and a gun, and a district's road surveyor refusing to read a small print on a stake) AND TWO QUESTIONS WRITTEN DOWN AS NOT ASKED (a district's entry in a street, and the schedule), and the standing seven refused-or-untaken papers from Volume 03 are untouched and none is restated or re-taken.**

- **THE LEASH IS AT EIGHT USES AT THE END OF CHAPTER 0170 AND NO NINTH USE IS STAGED IN CHAPTERS 161 TO 170. THE WORD *SLATE* APPEARS NOWHERE IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS, NOT EVEN IN TAMSIN ROOK'S OWN DAY-BOOK IN CHAPTER 0169, WHERE THE PRICE SIDE OF THE BOARD IS REFERRED TO WITHOUT NAMING THE OBJECT, and that is a tightening of Batch 0001's own practice and not a change of state. THE FOUR STANDING NON-USES AT CHAPTERS 0127, 0130, 0134 AND 0143 ARE UNCHANGED AND NO NON-USE IS WRITTEN DOWN IN ANY OF THESE TEN DAY-BOOKS.**

- **THE *ABOUT NINE* FIGURE FOR VOLUME 04 BATCH 0002 IS FORTY ACROSS 26,790 WORDS, WHICH IS 0.15 PER HUNDRED WORDS, against 0.25 in Volume 04 Batch 0001, 0.28 in Volume 03 Batch 0005, 0.35 in Volume 03 Batch 0004 and 0.29 in Volume 03 Batch 0003. THE FIGURE IS MEASURED AND NOT CLAIMED AS AN IMPROVEMENT.** The batch's nines are all told apart by which room they are in and none is added to any other: nine panels on a sheet, about nine hundred names in a book, about nine hundred bells, about nine hundred entries, about nine hundred rooms, about nine matters, nine refusals, nine stakes in ten days, about nine people a year right about a bridge and about nine going round it, about nine years of timber in a yard, about nine years of a ruled column, about nine minutes, **nine days out of ten and the tenth hour which are a carter's rounds and a page reference and not a nine of anything**, nine miles, and **NO FIFTH NINE IS WRITTEN.**

- **THE TWO NEW FIGURES OF BATCH 0002 THAT LOOK LIKE A SIXTH QUANTITY AND ARE NOT: three hundred and eleven fences and two hundred and six fences, which are two numbers about two different pieces of ground told by the same mouth on two days, and six hundred and forty yards and six hundred and fifty-one yards, which are two true numbers about one length of bypass and the true figure is between them. NEITHER PAIR MAY BE ADDED, DIVIDED OR CARRIED INTO A LATER BATCH AS A RATE.**

- **THE REGISTER IS THIRTEEN AT THE END OF CHAPTER 0160 AND VOLUME 04 BATCH 0001 CREATED NO FOURTEENTH AND REMOVED NONE, for the sixth time running, and that is not a loss. FOUR THINGS CAME TO HIM IN THE TEN DAYS AND CHAPTER 0160 WRITES THEM OUT: four names for a road, a word on a stake, a burr under a cut, and a man who was born at the end of it. NOTHING WAS ASKED FOR, PRODUCED, GIVEN OR TAKEN ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS AND EVERY DAY-BOOK SAYS SO. NO SEVENTH REFUSAL WAS STAGED: THE BATCH STAGES TWO EXPLICIT REFUSALS OF A QUESTION AND ONE QUESTION WRITTEN DOWN AS UNASKED, AND THE SEVEN REFUSED-OR-UNTAKEN PAPERS STANDING FROM VOLUME 03 ARE UNTOUCHED AND NONE IS RESTATED OR RE-TAKEN.**

- **The document register is THIRTEEN and the thirteen are unchanged. Batch 0004 created no fourteenth and removed none, and for the fourth time running that is not a loss.** Batch 0005 created no fourteenth and removed five, which are in a district's keeping on a slip with five numbers. **The physical count of sheets in a coat is FOURTEEN, because Chapter 87 has Bryn Pethick write a sheet on the hall's own paper and give it across, and that sheet is not in the register. Both are correct of two different objects and the disagreement is inherited, recorded and not repaired.** **The five receipts of Chapter 95 are not a fifteenth document** — a receipt for property taken off a man is not a document he carries as evidence, and the chapter keeps the two figures apart in one sentence. **A fourteenth thing exists and is not a document:** the nine lines in a coat, which nobody may certify and no clerk can enter and which is on nobody's list.
- **Ten things and a slate is his own count of what was on the table in the room over the draper's on the sixty-second**, and Chapter 76 says so in his own mouth. **It is not thirteen.** The Fourmile four are one line in a book in a coat and not four.
- **The chapter's own numeral for the deputy's sheet of the forty-eighth — *the fifth sheet he has* — is the chapter's figure, is left as written, and no chapter may restate it as a different number or assert a total that contradicts the thirteen.**
- **THE LEASH IS AT EIGHT USES AT THE END OF CHAPTER 0160 AND NO NINTH USE IS STAGED IN CHAPTERS 141 TO 160. THE SLATE IS NAMED NOWHERE IN VOLUME 04 EXCEPT IN TAMSIN ROOK'S OWN DAY-BOOK IN CHAPTER 0156, WHERE SHE WRITES THAT ITS PRICE SIDE IS THE OTHER WAY UP, AND THREE NON-USES THAT WERE DRAFTED INTO THE DAY-BOOKS OF CHAPTERS 0157, 0158 AND 0159 WERE REMOVED BEFORE THE FILES WERE SAVED, because a non-use written down in a day-book is a fifth non-use staged. THE FOUR STANDING NON-USES ARE CHAPTERS 0127, 0130, 0134 AND 0143 AND THEY ARE UNCHANGED.**

- **THE LEASH IS AT EIGHT USES AT THE END OF CHAPTER 0150 AND NO NINTH USE IS STAGED IN CHAPTERS 141 TO 150.** One non-use is staged in this batch, in Chapter 0143, where the slate is under his arm for the whole of an hour in a room he paid for with two people in it and one of them has just refused him, and he does not put a question on it **and he does not write the non-use down.** The standing non-uses are **Chapters 0127, 0130, 0134 and 0143.**

- **The leash is at EIGHT uses, five worked and three of which cost the man who asked, and nobody has been refused a second time. The count is settled and is not to be re-counted differently. IT IS STILL EIGHT AT THE END OF CHAPTER 0120: NO NINTH USE OF THE SLATE IS STAGED IN CHAPTERS 101 TO 120.** In Chapter 0106 he does not put a question on the slate; he asks her nothing, she tells him nothing, and each of them notes the other's not-asking in his own or her own book. **THAT IS A NON-USE AND IT IS NOT A NINTH USE AND NO LATER BATCH MAY COUNT IT AS ONE.** The eight: Mercy Slee's **writing** the slate on the thirty-first; the **flat fork, the thirty-fourth**; the **shop, the thirty-eighth**; the **ninth door, the forty-third**; the **market square, the fifty-third**; the **room, the sixty-second, about the seventh hour of the morning**, the first that cost anybody anything; the **counting room, the seventy-fifth, about the second hour of the afternoon**; and **Chapter 97, the eighty-third, about the fourth hour, at the bottom of a stair** — where she refuses in one word and gives a reason that is hers and is the first time in eleven years she has said why she wants a thing. **Non-uses stand and NONE of them is a use of any number: Chapter 78, where he had it ready and did not use it; Chapter 93, where he has the slate and the chalk and does not put a question on it; Chapter 96, where he does not ask her to come up the stair and does not offer; Chapter 0116, Day 102, where a woman offers him her sentence in a market in front of about nine people and he says no out loud in front of all nine; and Chapter 0120, Day 106, where he does not put a question on the slate and writes down once that he is not going to write it down again, because she said in her own book on the hundred and second that the third time she writes it she will be counting, and a woman who counts is a woman waiting to be owed.**
- **THERE ARE NOW FOUR NINES IN THIS BATCH'S WORLD AND NOT THREE, AND THE FOURTH IS THE COURT'S, AND CHAPTER 0126 WRITES ALL FOUR OUT FLAT IN A MAN'S DAY-BOOK ON THE PURPOSE OF KEEPING THEM APART. **About nine hundred rooms** on the top terrace, **about nine hundred bells** in the city, **about nine hundred entries** on nine sheets in a store's book, and **about nine hundred matters a year** into a room with a lamp in it. **They are told apart by which room they are in, none of them is a quantity of anything, and no later batch may add any of them to any other or write a fifth.**
- **The Volume 03 figures that must never be restated as each other:** **about nine hundred rooms** on the top terrace, **about nine hundred bells** in the city, and **about nine hundred entries** on nine sheets in a store's book are **THREE DIFFERENT NINES**, written out in a row on the page in Chapter 0115 and **never to be added together**; a number that can only be told apart by which sheet it is on is a filing reference. **About two hundred and forty things** in a district column and **nineteen bells** in the first column of the same sheet; **about eleven thousand lines a year** in a district, **about nine hundred** into one hall, **about two hundred and forty** onto one sheet; **eleven clauses** in a standing instruction and **clause seven**; **nine days** from the date of a notice to a hearing, counted once and checked twice; **four in ten** lines not attended in a village of about two hundred over nine years, and **four in ten** in a city of nine thousand — **the same arithmetic in two places and one man, and it is the only arithmetic in this business**; **about four hundred people** round a trestle, **about two hundred** who said their own names, and **about two hundred** who come out of doors for four strokes in a yard; **about nine hundred people** in a market, which is a market in Volume 02 and a different market in Volume 03 and must never be the same one; **about nine** words in the fourth column of a store's list and **about ninety** and **nine hundred rows** over nine sheets; **nine** years at a rail and in a yard, **six** years at a plate, **eleven** years sweeping a yard, **eleven** years on a road — **and a man's years at a trade are not an age.** **None of these is a fifth figure and none of them may be added together.**
- **Four rows of figures that look alike and are not:** the **fifty-three** wards marked on the Measure's road map; the **forty-one** L-4 schedule lines; the **four hundred and eleven** of the Low Ward; and the **four** columns. None of them is a fifth.
- **THE THREE-ANSWER ARITHMETIC IS A RULE ABOUT COUNTING AND NOT A STAGE AND MAY NOT BE WRITTEN AS A POWER.** A private handbell may carry one answer; a local public signal normally needs three independent answers, and **a man can be one of them and cannot be two.** **Nothing in Chapters 111 to 130 moves him and no chapter of any batch may manufacture the movement the batch is about.** The nine words on the flyleaf from Day 62 remain a comparison. His own plain carrier is in a sealed yard in Kells. The Crown carrier is rung in no chapter because it may not be. Tamsin's handbell may be rung at most once in the whole volume, on her own terms, and never for him, and is not rung in this batch.
- **THE CERTIFIED PLAIN CARRIER'S INTERVAL IS COUNTED IN DAYS FROM THE SEVENTY-SEVENTH AND NOT IN CHAPTER NUMBERS. Day 107 is thirty days and Day 116 is thirty-nine days, and every one of the ten chapters of Batch 0003 states the correct figure for its own day: thirty, thirty-one, thirty-two, thirty-three, thirty-four, thirty-five, thirty-six, thirty-seven, thirty-eight, thirty-nine. **CHAPTER NUMBERS AND DAY NUMBERS ARE TWO DIFFERENT NUMBERINGS AND A STATE FILE MAY NOT CONVERT ONE INTO THE OTHER: Chapter 120 is Day 106, Chapter 130 is Day 116, Chapter 140 is Day 126, and a file that writes a chapter number as a day is repeating the error §10c records. The rule is simple and it is this: the interval is written as a day of the volume, and as a day, thirty, thirty-one, thirty-two, thirty-three, thirty-four, thirty-five, thirty-six, thirty-seven, thirty-eight, thirty-nine across Chapters 121 to 130, then, across Chapters 131 to 140, FORTY, FORTY-ONE, FORTY-TWO, FORTY-THREE, FORTY-FOUR, FORTY-FIVE, FORTY-SEVEN, FORTY-EIGHT AND FORTY-NINE, **and then, across Chapters 141 to 150, FIFTY, FIFTY-ONE, FIFTY-TWO, FIFTY-THREE, FIFTY-FOUR, FIFTY-FIVE, FIFTY-SIX, FIFTY-SEVEN, FIFTY-EIGHT AND FIFTY-NINE.** Chapter 0136 states the figure in the words *not been struck* rather than *untouched* and the number is right.

**THE TWO COUNTERS ON THAT CARRIER ARE SEPARATE AND NO LATER WRITER MAY MERGE THEM. THE STRIKE COUNTER: not rung and not struck since the seventy-seventh, fifty days at Day 127 and fifty-nine days at Day 136, and that is the figure every chapter of Batch 0005 states. THE HAND COUNTER: a hand laid flat on the crown four times, on the hundred and sixth, the hundred and twenty-second, the hundred and twenty-fifth and the hundred and twenty-seventh, and the fourth is the last one and he knows it is the last one while he is doing it. THE INHERITED STATE FILES CARRY A SINGLE PHRASE THAT SAYS *NOT TOUCHED SINCE THE SEVENTY-SEVENTH* AND ALSO RECORD THE HAND ON THE CROWN, AND BOTH ARE CORRECT OF TWO DIFFERENT ACTS; THE BATCH HAS WRITTEN THE FIGURE IN THE WORDS *NOT RUNG* IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS AND THE FOURTH HAND IS ON THE PAGE IN 0141 AND IN 0150, AND THE DISTINCTION IS RECORDED HERE SO THAT IT CANNOT BE LOST.**
- **THE CERTIFIED PLAIN CARRIER'S FIGURES, WHICH NO CHAPTER OF ANY BATCH MAY CHANGE:** **fourteen pounds two ounces**, **one pound fourteen shillings**, bought at Bramden on the fifty-second, **no wax, no dressed groove, only the caster's mark and a day**, **RUNG ON FOUR OCCASIONS IN ITS LIFE** — Ketch striking it twice on a leather before signing, which is **one occasion and two blows**; a sill on the fifty-second; Bramden market square on the fifty-third; Miremarket on the seventy-seventh. **"Twice" is wrong and no chapter may say it.** **It has not been touched since the seventy-seventh: twenty days at Day 97, twenty-nine days at Day 106.** On Day 106 it is unrolled on a step, his hand is put flat on the crown, and it is put back **unstruck**. **A STATE FILE MAY NOT WRITE *DAY 120* FOR THIS INTERVAL. CHAPTER NUMBERS AND DAY NUMBERS ARE DIFFERENT NUMBERINGS AND THE CARRIER FIGURE IS COUNTED IN DAYS FROM THE SEVENTY-SEVENTH; DAY 120 WOULD BE FORTY-THREE DAYS AND NO SUCH DAY EXISTS IN THIS VOLUME.**
- **THE MATTER OF LIRA VALE, AND ITS ARITHMETIC. The district court of Netherby sits in Sennet by the arrangement of eleven districts up the river from it, in the third room along the north corridor. The matter is 4, 1 8 2, the district is Netherby, the day is the eleventh, and the same number and the same day are the line in a store's book on the top terrace, entered as a repair, with *coupling* in the fourth column. The court sat four days. The record is about four hundred words and there is a folded sheet of eleven lines in her own words at the back of the bundle, in a different paper from the rest and in a fair hand that is not hers. **HE DOES NOT PUT THE INTERVAL BETWEEN THE MATTER'S DAY AND HIS OWN ANYWHERE IN THE MANUSCRIPT, AND NO STATE FILE MAY COMPUTE IT.** The record gives a day and a year and the boards give a year each and he counts neither, and the reason is on the page: a number he has not checked is a sentence.
- **THE FIFTH CLAUSE'S JOB-COUNT IS SIX AT THE END OF CHAPTER 0142 AND SEVEN AT THE END OF CHAPTER 0148, AND THE WRITING-TIME PAGES SAID SEVEN, THEN SIX, THEN SEVEN, WHICH CANNOT BE TRUE OF ONE CLAUSE. The count was five by Day 110 (Chapter 0124), and Chapter 0142's keeper's box and Chapter 0144's yard are the same clause-function two days apart, so the sixth job is paid on Day 128 and restated on Day 130, and the seventh is paid on Day 134 in Chapter 0148, where it is the only one that gave him a thing instead of costing him one. Chapter 0142's two numerals are corrected from seven to six. The chapter does not say that 0142 and 0144 are one job, and that is recorded as the chapter's ambiguity rather than settled. NO LATER WRITER MAY USE A FIGURE OTHER THAN FIVE AT DAY 110, SIX AT DAYS 128 AND 130, AND SEVEN FROM DAY 134 ONWARD.** One consequence inside Chapter 0148 is recorded rather than repaired: its seventh job both gave him a schedule he cannot be asked to give back and cost him the only thing he wanted, and the chapter prices it both ways in two different paragraphs, and both are the chapter's.

- **THE LEASH IS AT EIGHT USES AND NO NINTH USE IS STAGED IN CHAPTERS 121 TO 140.** Chapter 0127 is Batch 0003's central non-use: he has a thing in his head he wants, the slate is under his arm all day, and he does not put a question on it, and the count is eight, and he writes the non-use down once and writes under it that he is not going to write it down again. **A NON-USE IS NOT A USE AND NO LATER BATCH MAY COUNT IT AS ONE. Chapter 0130 is a second one: he does not tell a man in a market that he is the man the man was talking about on the hundred and fifth, and he does not thank him, and nobody asked either of them for anything. Chapter 0134 is a THIRD and it is Batch 0004's only non-use: the slate is under his arm for the whole of an hour in a room he paid for, with two people in it, one of whom has just refused him in writing, and he does not put a question on it — AND HE DOES NOT WRITE THE NON-USE DOWN, and the chapter says why in his own hand, because she said in her own book on the hundred and second that the third time she writes the not-asking she would be counting, and a woman who counts is a woman waiting to be owed, and a man who keeps a count of what he does not ask is a man waiting to be owed too. THE STANDING NON-USES ARE CHAPTERS 0127, 0130 AND 0134.**
- **The hearing, and the arithmetic that fixes it.** The notice at the foot of the board and at the top of the street is dated **nine days** before the day of the hearing — **counted once and checked twice, and the count is the only thing on that board a man can check and he checked it.** **Day 96 is seven days off; Day 101 is two days off; Day 102 is tomorrow; the hearing is Day 103 at the second hour, at the upper room of the Quiet Office in Sennet. IT HAPPENED OFF THE PAGE. No chapter of any batch describes what was said in that room, and the hearing's outcome is not a matter of record anywhere in the manuscript except four hundred words of the office's own account of the shape of it.**
## 10. Canon corrections and the repair record, compressed

**Kept so that no later phase believes a state file that a chapter does not support. None of these is a change of plot and none may be re-litigated without the chapter in front of you.**

- **Batches 0001–0005 of Volume 01 recorded NO canon correction.** The Reckoning 619 chronology, Ewan Pike's death, the rope cut, the thin sound and the Crown's removal of independent keepers are untouched throughout. **The deliberate spending of Nell Fenn's alignment on the thirtieth is a new event and not a correction.** The Tamsin Rook deafness change of Day 21 is a deliberate canon change and is used, not revised.
- **Volume 02 recorded NO canon correction in any of its five batches.** **Volume 03 Batch 0001 recorded NO canon correction either, and it corrected one inherited error of the state layer rather than of the prose, which is recorded at §4: the loose leaf in Amos Trewin's stock book is a leaf of unknown hand, and Chapter 0105 states on the page that the copying wax is not the evidence and that the wax inference was wrong twice.**
- **Volume 01 close: the volume and its outline disagreed at nine places and the outline was corrected in every one of them — the prose is right in all nine.** The points were the placement of Crake's seizure order, Chapter 38's market lane as a *false* alarm, the attribution of the saved child at house 114, the attribution of the surviving register fragment, the ringing-ears figure in the cost, the route fragment at the departure, the placement of the next-volume question, and the bolts, the wax and the village in the final image. The record is `reviews/volume-01/volume-audit.md`.
- **Chapter 76 was repaired by Batch 0005 and the repair is recorded because it touched a chapter that batch does not own:** it said of the certified plain carrier that *it has never been rung*, which is false, and it now says *I have not rung it since the fifty-third*. The sentence after it, *I am not going to ring it in this county at all*, is untouched.
- **Chapter 100 was repaired in this pass, in this file's own terms, and the repairs are recorded rather than made quietly.** The market square of the seventy-seventh was described as being *in a town four days behind this one*, which is false — **the Day-77 market was in Miremarket, the town he is standing in as he leaves, and chapters 91, 93, 95, 96, 97 and 99 all say so. It now reads *in this town*.** The count, *twice myself*, was correct throughout and is not the count of occasions in the bell's life, which is four.
- **The largest class of defect this project produces is a state file claiming a repair the prose does not contain, and the second largest is a state file restating a fact as a different figure.** Nine defects of the first kind were found and fixed inside Batch 0004 before the files were saved, a second pass found twenty more, and a review pass found seven. All are set out in `state/batch-0009-summary.md`. **A defect count is deliberately not given here: a figure in a state file is a claim about the page and has to be counted in the page.**

## 10a. THE POST-BATCH REPAIR PASS ON VOLUME 03 BATCH 0001 — every item, and none of it is a change of plot

**This pass ran against the reviewer's findings for Batch 0011 and against the finished chapters. It changed no event, no motive, no refusal, no document, no figure that a later chapter may lean on, and it moved no day. Every item below is either a contradiction between two sentences of the same book, a state file asserting something a chapter does not support, or a mechanical repetition. The plot of Chapters 101 to 110 is unchanged and the batch is not to be restarted.**

- **THE DAY-BOOK COLUMN RITUAL, which contradicted itself in seven of ten chapters, is now consistent in ten of ten.** The closing stage sentence in each chapter now names the columns the entry actually fills. Chapters 0101, 0102, 0104 and 0105 fill the first and second and leave the third and the fourth; **Chapters 0106, 0107, 0108, 0109 and 0110 fill the first, second and third and leave the fourth**; **and Chapter 0103, which had no stage sentence at all, now has one.** The labelled blocks in each entry were the ground truth and the stage sentences were corrected to them, not the other way round.
- **THE BELL'S LEAD TIME IS NOW ONE THING AND IT IS THE FOUR DAYS.** Chapter 0101 established that the sheet under the first bell is dated four days forward of the ringing, and Chapter 0102 had a nine-year board-keeper say flatly that the bell told them *the day before the day*. **He no longer says it.** He now says *before the day*, and then says the true thing, which is that **he has kept the book nine years and has never once written down how many days ahead the bell goes, and there is no column for it, and he has wanted to rule one about nine times and has not, because a column a man rules for himself is a thing he has to be right about.** That converts the contradiction into the volume's own method and it is a better beat than the one it replaces. **Chapter 0101's own sheet is dated *the ninety-first day of the month* — it had said *the eleventh*, which was a day already forty days gone, and the chapter's own arithmetic said four days off. NO CHAPTER MAY NOW STATE A SINGLE FIGURE FOR HOW FAR AHEAD THE BELL GOES EXCEPT FOUR DAYS, AND THE BELL GOES FOUR DAYS AHEAD.**
- **THE SECOND WATER BELL IN CHAPTER 0104 IS NOT MARTA VELL'S BELL.** The chapter had asserted in bold that it was, and denied it two lines later, and three state files had canonized the assertion. **The prose now reads *a water bell with Marta Vell's fault is in that column*, which is what the scene is about, and the same form, the same empty box and the same rule past the end of the line are what the next sentence compares.** `outline/volume-03.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md` and `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` have all been corrected to the same reading, and `state/continuity.md` §5a now says in terms that any later file calling it her bell is repeating a repaired error. **The dramatic beat is untouched and is in fact restored by this: two empties in a column in three counties is what he refuses to join, and it cannot be that if it is one bell.**
- **CHAPTER 0108 NO LONGER MISREMEMBERS CHAPTER 0107.** One sentence had a man in a market *four days behind* him put *a clerk's name* in *nine hundred* people's mouths and tell him the price *at a trough*. It was **the day before, in the same square, with about four hundred people round a trestle, and it was Garvin Lyle's own name, and there is no trough anywhere in the batch.** The sentence is corrected and the same error was corrected in `state/continuity.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and `state/batch-0011-summary.md`, all three of which had propagated it.
- **ONE SQUARE HAS ONE POPULATION.** Chapter 0108 opened with *about two hundred people* at the board and closed at the edge of *a market of about two thousand people*. Both are true of different quantities and the chapter now says so in the opening sentence: **a market of about two thousand, with about two hundred at the board.** The Day-94 row in §5a and the Chapter 0108 entry in `state/chapter-summaries.md` had a third figure of about nine hundred and have been corrected. **THE MARKET OF ABOUT NINE HUNDRED ON THE MAINTAINED ROAD AND THE MARKET OF ABOUT TWO THOUSAND ARE TWO DIFFERENT MARKETS AND MAY NOT BE ADDED, AND THE CROWD AT THE TRESTLE ON DAY 93 AND THE CROWD AT THE BOARD ON DAY 94 ARE THE SAME TOWN ON CONSECUTIVE DAYS.**
- **THE ELAPSED-TIME FIGURE IS NOW ONE FIGURE PER CHAPTER.** He left Kells on the thirty-third, so Day 87 is fifty-four days, Day 92 is fifty-nine and Day 96 is sixty-three. The batch had **six weeks** in eighteen places and **eleven weeks** in eight, which cannot both be right and neither of them is. **Chapters 0101 to 0106 now read seven or eight weeks and Chapters 0107 to 0110 read nine weeks**, with two exceptions that are a different measurement and are marked: **four district officers in about seven weeks** (Chapter 0108, first met around Day 45) and **a smell he had been away from for seven weeks** (Chapter 0103, pre-dating the road). `state/continuity.md` §5a, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/current.md` and the Batch 0002 prompt all carry the same figures. **There is no *six weeks* and no *eleven weeks* anywhere in Chapters 101 to 110 and no later batch may use either.**
- **A VOLUME 02 LEAK IN CHAPTER 0101 IS GONE.** *I carried it eleven days* was a Volume 02 figure. At Day 87 the sentence in the second column of the fortieth has been carried forty-seven days, and the chapter now says forty-seven twice, once as a date and once as a duration. **The Day 40 to Day 51 interval of eleven days in Chapter 0110 is a closed retrospective and is correct and stays.**
- **THE STORE IS THREE MILES AWAY, NOT NINE DAYS.** Chapter 0103's day-book had said the store Doss Harrow named was *nine days up the road* on a day when the smithy is three miles short of Ashmoor and he walks through Ashmoor at the seventh hour. The chapter now reads **three miles up this road, and I will be in Ashmoor at about the seventh hour, and I could walk to it, and I am not going to, and I want that in the book in that order and not improved in the morning.**
- **THE DISTANCE CHAIN IS NOW CLOSED AND SELF-CHECKING.** Sennet is four days up the road on Day 91, three on Day 92 and two on Day 93. **Chapter 0107 said the market of about two thousand was *four days further on* than the market he had been in on the ninety-first, which put a four-day town on a day two days from the capital. It is now two days further on, which is what the same chapter's own closing line said all along.** Day 94 is the last day in the market and Day 95 is his arrival in Sennet.
- **THE LEASH IS AT EIGHT AND THE VOLUME OUTLINE NO LONGER SAYS OTHERWISE.** `outline/volume-03.md` recorded a ninth use in Chapter 0106, in the batch's own *what the batch paid* list, and the word *leash* appears in no Volume 03 chapter. The record now says the opposite, in the same terms §9 already used: **the leash is NOT used a ninth time, the non-use is on the page, and the count stays at eight.** The batch card for 0106 has been corrected to match its own outcome.
- **THE FOURTH COLUMN IS TWO LINES.** `outline/volume-03.md` said *one line* in one place and *two lines* in another, and Chapters 0106 and 0110 both say two. It is two, and the outline now says two in both places.
- **A DEAD PATH IS GONE.** `state/current.md` and `state/volume-index.md` both pointed at `workspace/volume-03/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` and `workspace/volume-03/batch-0001/`, **which have never existed in git and never will.** The cards are `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0001.md` and the prose is `chapters/volume-03/`. Both files now say so, and the index row is marked as the one row in the table that is not a phase directory.
- **THE DAY-BOOK NO LONGER RE-COPIES THE SCENE IT IS WRITING ABOUT.** The panel was 27 to 60 per cent of each chapter and the worst case, Chapter 0110, carried a ninety-word verbatim run between its second block and its own closing entry. **The longest verbatim run between any two panels in the batch is now nine words, and most of those are a printed heading or a signature sentence that the man is shown writing down twice on purpose.** About 260 words came out of Chapter 0110, 30 out of Chapter 0107 and 30 out of Chapter 0108, and in every case the *finding* stayed and the restatement went.
- **THE *ABOUT* TIC IS DOWN BY A THIRD.** *About nine* stood at 114 across 28,403 words and is now 73 across 28,243. **What remains is load-bearing and must stay: about nine hundred rooms, about nine hundred bells, about nine hundred reasons, and about nine thousand people in Sennet.** The hedge was dropped where the figure is a measurement he chose (nine feet, nine flights, nine minutes) or a count that is exact (nine sheets, nine lines, nine of them), and it was kept where the man is estimating.
- **WHAT WAS CHECKED AFTER THE PASS AND CAME BACK CLEAN:** no weekday and no month named; the register is thirteen in every chapter that states it; no age supplied for anybody; **every daytime hour still bare and the ninth hour of the evening the only half-day, because not one clock figure was touched**; no day renumbered and no day repeated; one chapter to a day, Days 87 to 96; **zero prose lines of five words or more repeated across Chapters 101 to 110.**

## 11. The two Volume 02 payoffs — BOTH ARE NOW PAID, in Volume 03 Batch 0001, and the record of how

**These were recorded as unmet at the Volume 02 close because they were unmet. They are paid, in Chapters 0105 and 0107, in that order, and they are paid in this volume's first batch. The record of what was paid, and of what each payment deliberately did not do, is here so that a later writer inherits a shape and not an idea. The obligation itself is discharged and `state/current.md` and `state/open-threads.md` say so in those words and not one word more.**

- **Mara Vale's trail is visible, is confirmed to be hers, and refuses to answer — PAID in Chapter 0105 and continued in Chapter 0110.** A leaf of paper about the size of a day-book page, folded once, pinned at an angle under a drawing-pin among nine Crown sheets and a handbill about a horse, on a public board in a market of about nine hundred on Day 91. **Confirmed by comparison of the hand — the t she does not cross, the space she leaves after a full stop, the downstroke on the g that stops and starts — and NOT by copying wax, and the chapter says on the page that there is no wax on it and that a mark the size of a thumbnail is not a hand.** The five words are *Ask the board. Not me.* The second leaf, on the Office's own board on the top terrace in Sennet on Day 96, is *Read the store list. Not the room*, with a date and a store number on the back.
  **And the chapter states four things it will not do, and all four are binding on every later batch: (1) her name is not said aloud in Chapter 0105, and four people were within about nine feet of the board; (2) he does not take either leaf off either board, and the reason is on the page in his own hand — a paper off a public board in a Crown town is the same act with a different name as five sheets off a table in Miremarket; (3) he does not go into any building to ask after her, and the three reasons are written out in full, and the third and worst one is that a man who goes and asks so that he can say he did not go is doing the thing for himself; (4) nothing about her is resolved — not where she is, not whether she is alive, not what she wants, and no age is supplied for her by the narrator.** The relationship is now **started and refused**, which is a different thing from unstarted, and nothing in the prose has to be undone.
- **The Silent Muster is proved, by a document and a date and not by a bell, to be one clause of a larger Renewal operation — PAID in Chapter 0107.** A printed Crown sheet with a number at the head of it and a date under it, headed *Standing instruction of the district, in the matter of the coming Renewal, for the use of all officers and clerks in the district, and for the information of such persons as may lawfully be informed.* **Eleven clauses. Clause seven: *that in the course of the Renewal, such persons as are entered on no roll in the district, being children, be taken and held to the uses of the district, and their names be brought onto the roll.*** A district's own form in a second county, nine days after the date of the instruction, in a clerk's own hand, **refers to clause seven by its number and not by its words**, because a number is shorter than a sentence and a clerk is in a hurry. A third form in a third county, in a hand about a year younger, doing the same, dated about a year after the L-4 notice was pinned in Kells and about a week after his bell rang.
  **And the chapter holds all four fences: (1) the sheet carries no signature and only a store number; (2) six of the eleven clauses are about things Tomas has never heard of, he says so out loud in a market to about four hundred people, and he does not guess at one of them and does not go looking for them in the six; (3) it is a clause and not a plot — it does not say who struck it, who holds the names afterwards, or what happens to a child once taken, and he says he wanted that sentence for ninety-three days and it is not in it; (4) it names no enemy and opens no mechanism.** **He got the direction of one of the two dated sheets wrong in public and a man in the room corrected him and was right, and a fair copy of a Crown sheet is Crown property from the moment it is made, and the officer of a county who said so gave his name off his own mouth and then stood nine feet off the trestle and took nothing.**

## 10b. VOLUME 03 BATCH 0002 — the batch record, and every fix made before the files were saved

**The batch ran Day 97 to Day 106, one chapter to a day, and it changed no event, no motive, no refusal, no document, no custody state, no figure a later chapter may lean on, and no day. This section records what was drafted wrong and corrected in the prose, in the direction the error has now gone more than a dozen times — the state layer and not the chapters — so that Batch 0003 inherits the clean shape and knows where the traps are.**

- **Chapter 0120 drafted an age for a new person, three times, and all three were removed.** A carter's boy on the second cart was drafted as *a boy of fifteen* in his mouth, again in the same man's mouth later, and a third time in the day-book, and he now has **no age anywhere in the chapter.** A new person enters with no age, and the only age in a hundred and twenty chapters that belongs to a child is a boy of eight in Volume 01.
- **Chapter 0120 put a contested count into Tamsin Rook's mouth** — *that is twice* — and it is removed.
