The not-asking has been recorded more times than that across two volumes and a batch writer may not put a number on it. **She now says only *you did not ask, and you are not going to*,** which is the whole of it and cannot be wrong.
- **Chapter 0120 had two elapsed-time figures for the same interval** — *eleven days into a city* in the opening and *twelve days* twice afterwards. **He came up the hill on the ninety-fifth, so Day 106 is eleven days and the chapter now says eleven in all three places.**
- **Chapter 0120 gave three different people in a single day the same nine years**, which is the volume's most over-used figure and, worse, is **nine years' duration in the exact shape of Abner Tole's tenure at a board.** **Ormond Bly keeps nine years and is not Tole; Marek Douse has been at the rail six years; Piet Sarn has swept that yard eleven years.** The three are distinct figures of distinct people and must not be collapsed.
- **Chapter 0120 contradicted itself about who was asked.** One paragraph had him asking three men for three sentences, and the chapter's own finding is that two of the three said it unasked. **The three sentences he sets under each other are now from three different places and three different days — Holt on the ninety-seventh, Tarrant on the hundredth, Bly this morning — and only the third was asked for**, which is what makes the asking the damage.
- **Chapter 0120 asserted *not one of them was asked* about a group of three in which one was.** Corrected, and the sentence now reads that none of the three was asked **to be about his business**, which is true and is the point.
- **Chapter 0120 said a person had asked for nothing and had, in the same page, been asked for a piece of paper.** Corrected: the sweeper asked for his true sentence to go in a place where somebody had written it down, **and Tomas gave him his own book and not a copy**, which is the correct arrangement and is on the page.
- **Chapter 0113, already written, carried THREE narrator meta-leaks and all three are removed.** *There is no craft in **this novel** that is worth more than that* is now *in this trade*; and *the most correct decision anybody has made in four counties **in this volume*** is now **in four counties and a city**, twice — once in the narrator and once in the day-book, and the two were the same sentence and are now the same sentence. **A repair pass is not a permission to leave a defect standing because the chapter was finished first, and the project has now established that the same class of error appears in a chapter that was written early in a batch as well as in one written late.** No event, motive, figure, refusal, hour or day moved.
- **SUPERSEDED BY SECTION 10c. This bullet was wrong and defended the wrong thing.** It used to read *Chapter 0120's title is the card's title and the card is binding*, and it used to certify that no chapter of the batch refers to the volume, to a batch, to a chapter or to a reader. **Both halves of that were false: thirteen narrator instances did exactly that, and the title announced a volume ending thirty chapters early.** The title is now **If There Were Three Of Me**, fixed at the card and not only in the chapter, and the meta-leak certificate now lives in §10c and is true. **The volume does not end at Chapter 120; it ends at Chapter 150, and the day map is Days 87 to 136. No chapter of this batch refers to the volume, to a batch, to a chapter or to a reader in its own narration, and no later chapter may.**
- **SUPERSEDED BY SECTION 10c, AND IT WAS FALSE AS IT STOOD.** This bullet used to certify that the day-book did not re-copy the scene it was writing about. **It did, in eight of ten chapters, in runs of up to thirty-six words.** The measured position is now in §10c and is the truth rather than a claim. Where a sentence is written down twice on purpose it is a printed heading or a signature sentence the man is shown committing to paper twice.
- **WHAT WAS CHECKED AFTER THE WRITING — AND THE REVIEWER THEN FOUND THAT TWO OF THESE CERTIFICATES WERE FALSE. The corrected list is in §10c and this one is history.** It used to read: **no weekday and no month named in any of the ten chapters**, and the month sweep returns only the modal verb *may* and the substrings *frightened* and *month*; **the register reads thirteen wherever it is stated and no chapter states a total that contradicts it**; **no age on the page for anybody**; **every daytime hour bare and the ninth hour of the evening the only half-day on a live hour, with two retrospective half-days that are marked as retrospective and belong to the days they name**; no day renumbered and no day repeated, one chapter to a day, Days 97 to 106; **no Crown clapper seen or named, no fitting called one, no mechanism described, no official above a district named, OFF. 411 not on a page, no name transferred, no child found by a document**; **the plain carrier's four occasions uncounted nowhere and its twenty-nine days correct**; and **zero prose lines of five words or more repeated across Chapters 111 to 120.**
- **THE REVERSE SWEEP WAS RUN, which is the direction the error has now gone more than ten times: the new state text was searched for the words and figures removed from the prose.** Clean. The only hits were the sweep results themselves, which is the correct shape.

## 10c. VOLUME 03 BATCH 0002 — THE POST-BATCH REVIEW PASS, every finding, and every fix

**This pass ran against a reviewer report on the finished chapters and it changed no event, no motive, no refusal, no document, no custody state, no figure a later chapter may lean on, and no day. It is a repair pass, not a rewrite: the batch is not to be restarted and the plot of Chapters 111 to 120 is exactly as it was written. Every item below is either a contradiction inside one book, a state file asserting something a chapter does not support, a chapter number read as a day, or a mechanical repetition. The reviewer is right that three of the four blocking items were false certificates in the state layer, and that is this project's own top-listed defect class; the useful fact is that the reviewer caught them and the sweeps did not, which is the argument for running a review pass at all.**

- **BLOCKING 1 — THE META-LEAK SWEEP WAS CERTIFIED CLEAN AND WAS NOT. THIRTEEN NARRATOR INSTANCES ARE OUT OF THE PROSE.** `state/continuity.md` §10b and `state/batch-0012-summary.md` both certified that no chapter of the batch refers to the volume, to a batch, to a chapter or to a reader in its own narration. **Thirteen instances stood, and the sweep that was supposed to catch them searched for four literal strings and missed every variant.** All thirteen are repaired: *no better argument **in this book*** (0111:45) is now *in this trade*; *a thing I have not done **in three volumes*** (0112:147) is now *in four counties and a city*; ***the whole of the chapter*** (0113:37) is now ***the whole of it***; ***the next writer of this book*** (0115:69) is now *the sentence he will want most in a month and there is nobody on this flat to say it to*; *this whole book has been about* (0115:83 and 0119:77) is now *this whole business has been about*; *the finding of **this chapter*** (0117:59, 0118:87) and *the thing **this chapter is for*** (0118:49) are now *the finding of the hundred and third*, *the hundred and fourth* and *the thing the hundred and fourth is for*; *the first half of **this chapter*** (0119:15) is now *the first half of the morning*; ***Chapter 0118's last paragraph*** (0119:45) is now *what the man on the stair told him last night*; and *there is a reading of a charge **in a volume that is not this one** … and that is not this chapter* (0119:95) is recast in the world, as a man in Kells who reads a charge for himself and a habit that stays in the county it was made in. **The two distinctions that were already clean are still clean and are worth keeping in mind: a character's own day-book may say *in this book* and does, at 0111:123, 0112:147, 0114:21, 0114:35, 0116:101 and 0119:115, and a store's ledger may say it and does, at 0114:21 and 0114:35. Those are the man's book and the district's book and they are not the narrator.** **TWO INSTANCES OUTSIDE THIS BATCH WERE FOUND BY THE SAME SWEEP AND REPAIRED, because a new certificate covering Chapters 101 to 120 has to be true: Chapter 0108's *the third time in three volumes* is now *in ninety-four days*, and three archive quotations in `state/chapter-summaries.md` that quoted the removed sentences were corrected with them. THE SWEEP WAS THEN RE-RUN WIDE — *in this chapter / novel / volume / batch*, *this whole book*, *three volumes*, *this chapter*, *the next writer*, *on the page at all* — over Chapters 101 to 120 and it is clean, and the only hits anywhere in the batch are the six in-world day-book uses listed above.**

- **BLOCKING 2 — CHAPTER 0120'S TITLE WAS WRONG ON THE FACTS, AND THE CARD WAS THE SOURCE.** The card in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` gave Day 106 the title *The Day Before The Volume Ends*, and §10b defended it as *the card's title and the card is binding*. **The volume ends at Chapter 150 on Day 136, in the same sentence that defends the title.** A reader-facing title that announces an ending thirty chapters early is not a title this volume can carry, and this project's own rule at §10 is that where the outline and the prose disagree the outline is corrected. **The card is corrected, not the defence. Chapter 0120 is now *If There Were Three Of Me*** — the sentence the chapter ends on and the sentence Volume 04 opens on — and it is changed at the card, in `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0120.md`, in `state/batch-0012-summary.md` and in `state/chapter-summaries.md`. **No later batch may give a chapter a title that names a volume ending, a batch, or a chapter.**

- **BLOCKING 3 — "DAY 120" WAS A CHAPTER NUMBER BEING READ AS A DAY, IN FIVE PLACES, AND IT HAD PROPAGATED INTO THE PROMPT THE NEXT WRITER READS.** The certified plain carrier was last touched on **Day 77**, so Day 97 is twenty days and **Day 106 is twenty-nine**; Day 120 would be forty-three days and no such day exists in this volume. **The prose was right in all five chapters that state it (0111 twenty, 0114 twenty-three, 0115 twenty-four, 0117 twenty-six, 0119 twenty-eight) and the state layer was wrong by exactly thirteen in every instance.** Corrected in `state/batch-0012-summary.md`, `state/continuity.md` §9, `state/current.md` and `workspace/volume-03/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, and **the prohibition is now written into all three state files: a state file may not write *Day 120* for this interval, because 120 is a chapter number and not a day.** Two more instances of the same conflation were found by the same search and corrected: the spent Batch 0002 prompt said *Day 110 puts it seven days off* where it means **Day 96 (Chapter 0110)**, and *twenty days at Day 110* where it means **Day 97**. **One *Day 110* in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` is correct and must stay: Chapter 0124 is Day 110, and the Batch 0003 day map of Days 107 to 116 is right.**

- **BLOCKING 4 — "ZERO PROSE LINES OF FIVE WORDS OR MORE REPEATED" WAS FALSE, AND THE DAY-BOOK WAS RE-COPYING THE SCENE IN EIGHT CHAPTERS OF TEN.** The claim was in §10b and in `state/batch-0012-summary.md`. **Measured, the longest repeated run inside a chapter was thirty-six words and the day-book was reprinting the narrator's finding as a fresh transcription.** All of it is repaired and the day-book now re-derives instead of transcribing: **0117's thirty-one-word reprint of the *said it twice* maxim** is now *said once where it is on the record, and again to a stranger, and the second telling is the one anybody can use*; **0113's thirty-six-word reprint of *a wire and a column and a habit*** — which §10b had deliberately made identical in narrator and day-book, and which is a defect by this rule and not a virtue — is now *a wire, a column, a habit, and not one person in any of the three*; **0118's thirty-word second printing, in a second character's mouth, is gone entirely and Coll Salkeld now argues the same rule out of his own trade** — *a thing said into a column in a room belongs to the room, and the same thing said at a rail belongs to the man who said it, and it is the room's copy that has a date on it* — which is the same finding in a clerk's procedural voice and is better than the sentence it replaces; and 0114, 0115, 0116, 0118, 0119 and 0120 each had a run between seventeen and twenty-six words broken in the same way. **THE MEASURED POSITION NOW, and this is the number a later phase should check rather than a claim: the longest repeated run inside any one of the ten chapters is SEVENTEEN WORDS, and the longest across the batch is TWENTY-THREE, and both are the volume's own motif sentences — *A court publishes and an office files and the difference is four inches long*, and the *Which office is it* closing paragraph — which are the batch's thesis and its structural ritual and are not defects.** Six shorter runs are left standing on purpose and are listed here so that nobody spends the pass again on them: Tamsin's *a thing that is not done is not a thing that has been done* (three printings, and the volume's signature), the custody formula *a frame in a reinforced padded cradle with four retaining bolts made fast*, *and there is no form in this kingdom on which a man may*, the fifth clause's wording wherever the clause is named, the closing ninth-hour stage-setting, and the seventy-mile / four-counties refrain. **ONE DOCUMENT IS QUOTED IN FULL ONLY ONCE NOW: the fifth line Tomas struck out on Day 103 appeared three times in Chapter 0117 and appears once, at 0117:19, where he writes it; at 0117:91 he says in his own book that he is not going to write it out a second time, and at 0117:127 he records that he has already written it out once and where.**

- **SHOULD FIX 5 — THE SPEECH-DURATION TIC, WHICH THE REVIEWER CALLED THE BATCH'S SINGLE WORST PROSE FAULT, AND IT WAS ONE.** *About nine seconds* twenty-four times and *about four seconds* nineteen times: forty-three attributions, spread across Holt, Perrin Lowe, Osmund Tarrant, Tamsin Rook, Coll Salkeld, Ormond Bly, Marek Douse, Doss Harrow and three unnamed Sennet tradesmen, so that every character's decisive line ran to the same metronome and ten voices were flattened into one. **The device survives and is the point — he measures people because he is a maker and cannot help it — and the uniform figure is gone.** **The four seconds now belongs to the Office and to almost nothing else: a clerk entering one person's answer into a column, ten times across Chapters 0118 and 0120, and no other thing in the batch is four seconds long.** Nine seconds survives only where it is a man's own established tempo or Tomas measuring himself. **Tamsin Rook's instrument is the only thing in the batch still running on a nine-second metronome, because it is hers and she built it that way, and that is characterisation and not a tic.** Everyone else got his own measure or none: **Perrin Lowe's two things take as long as a hand getting as far as a coat; Tarrant does not hurry at all and one of his refusals is one sentence; Salkeld's account of a hearing takes about as long as it takes to cross a hall; Bly's true sentence takes about as long as it takes to look at a gate he has looked at every day for nine years; and the carter's no takes as long as a man with nine loads a day to look at a barrow, and then up at four flights, and then back at the man asking.** `state/continuity.md` §5a and `state/character-state.md` and `workspace/volume-03/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` have all been corrected to the new distribution, and no state file now attributes nine or four seconds to a man who no longer speaks in them.

- **SHOULD FIX 6 — NINE OF TEN CHAPTERS RUN ONE BEAT, AND THE REVIEWER IS RIGHT, AND IT IS NOT REPAIRED AND IS NOT REPAIRABLE WITHOUT REWRITING THE BATCH.** Man at a counter explains an institutional fact, Tomas does not thank him, Tomas writes it down; eleven-plus executions. **It is thematically load-bearing — a man learning that nobody is in charge should meet men saying that nothing is in charge — and removing it would take the batch apart.** The two structural repairs that were available have been made: **Wil Brand is now on stage in Chapter 0116** (four lines at a yard rail, a man who writes *eleven years* on the back of his own hand in chalk and goes back to his barrow, which is not a document and is in his own name and costs nothing and precedes his fourpence by three days), so a named man is no longer a figure inside somebody else's summary; and **Chapter 0116 now ends on the strongest line in it** instead of tacking a flat *I did not ring anything* after it, with that fact moved one paragraph earlier where it belongs. **The remaining structural debt is real and is recorded here rather than hidden: Batch 0003 must not run ten more chapters of a man at a counter.**

- **SHOULD FIX 7 — BATCH-CARD LABELS HAD LEAKED INTO THE NARRATION, AND THE PART OF THAT WHICH WAS A LEAK IS GONE WITH BLOCKING 1.** *The finding of the hundred and first day* and *in a hundred and six days* are the man's own day arithmetic and are correct and stay. *The whole of the day* stays. What was a leak was *the finding of this chapter*, *the thing this chapter is for*, *the first half of this chapter*, *Chapter 0118's last paragraph* and *in three volumes*, and all five are repaired. One narration beat also pre-refuted a suspicion the reader had never been given, and it is left: Chapter 0118's opening assertion that **nobody in this city is keeping a thing from Tomas Vale and there is no door being held shut**, which is the chapter's method stated once, early, in the negative, and the chapter then spends two hundred lines proving it.

- **SHOULD FIX 8 — TAMSIN'S GEOGRAPHY BROKE, AND ONE CLAUSE FIXES IT.** Chapter 0117 twice places her **four hundred yards off on the middle terrace** watching a man on the top terrace, and Chapter 0120 puts her **ten feet off his left shoulder** at that same stair with no transit and no acknowledgement. She now carries the transit on the page at 0120:173: **she had been four hundred yards off on the middle terrace on the hundred and third, watching a man not go through a door, and she had come up off it on the fourth of this month and not said why, and he had not asked, and it was the fourth day of not asking.** Three days and one clause, and no event added.

- **SHOULD FIX 9 — A MANUFACTURED DEADLINE IS GONE.** Chapter 0118 closed its day-book with *I have until about the sixth hour tomorrow*, which has no source anywhere and contradicts the batch's own thesis that nobody in this city knows the hours. **It is now *it opens at the fourth hour, and I have nowhere to be after that*** — the court on the middle terrace, which the chapter has just established publishes. No hour was added to a live day and no deadline now exists that the Office did not set.

- **SHOULD FIX 10 — A STRANGER KNEW TOO MUCH, AND THE LINK IS NOW ON THE PAGE.** In Chapter 0116 a needle-seller says to Tomas *when there is a hearing tomorrow and no paper in your coat*, and nothing on the page connected her to the name at the third line of the notice. **She now says *when you have gone up that stair three times in four days with a barrow, and there is your name at the third line of the sheet they nailed at the top of it, and there is a hearing on it tomorrow*** — which is three observable public facts and an accusation of being watched, and is a better line than the one it replaces. **Chapter 0119:5 is a different market on a different day and is unaffected: eight of nine people there had still never heard of the hearing, and that is the finding of Chapter 0119 and it stands.**

- **MINOR, all repaired: *nine hundred feet* of hill is now *seven hundred feet*, in Chapter 0111 and in its rhyme at Chapter 0120:41, because nine hundred in this batch means bells, rooms and entries and a fourth nine hundred is a filing reference and not a length of hill.** Chapter 0112's *it is the reason the ninety-eighth is on the page at all* is now *in the book at all*. Chapter 0112's two dates — *since the fifty-eighth* and *twelve days* — are no longer ambiguous: he has not **looked for** the thing since the fifty-eighth and has not **been in that county** in twelve days, and the sentence now says so. **Ormond Bly's reversal from open-handed on Day 103 to *I am not going to give you a sentence for nothing* on Day 106 has its bridge written on the page at 0120:69** — he told a stranger on the hundred and third that he had watched him standing at a door, a woman four hundred yards off had watched it too, neither has asked him for anything since, and **a man who keeps a gate for nine years is not going to be the one who offers twice.** It is a consequence and not a change of character. **One corruption was introduced during this pass and caught and fixed inside it** — a replacement in Chapter 0120 ran two words together and produced *then at meair at the fifth hour*; the day-book now reads *the length of it was however long it took a man with nine loads a day to look at a barrow and then up at four flights and then back at the man asking, at the fifth hour*, which is better than the figure it replaced.

- **WHAT WAS CHECKED AFTER THIS PASS AND CAME BACK CLEAN, MEASURED AND NOT CLAIMED:** **no narrator meta-leak anywhere in Chapters 101 to 120**, and the six in-world day-book uses are the only hits in the batch; **no weekday and no month named in any of the ten chapters**, and the month sweep returns only the modal verb *may* and the substrings *frightened* and *month*; **the register reads thirteen in every chapter that states it and no chapter states a total that contradicts it**, and *fourteen* appears only as *not a fourteenth* and in the weight and the price of the certified plain carrier; **no age on the page for anybody**; **every daytime hour bare and the ninth hour of the evening the only half-day on a live hour, carried in all ten chapters**, with two retrospective half-days that are marked retrospective and belong to the days they name (0117:95 and 0117:127, the fifth hour of the morning of the thirty-third; 0118:51, the second hour of the morning of Day 103); **no day renumbered and no day repeated, one chapter to a day, Days 97 to 106, and the form of the date is ninety-seventh, ninety-eighth, ninety-ninth, hundredth, hundred and first through hundred and sixth**; **no Crown clapper seen or named, no fitting called one, no mechanism described, no official above a district named, OFF. 411 not on a page, no name transferred, no child found by a document**; **all five clauses stand, the frame has not been in a building since the thirty-third in every chapter that mentions it, the plain carrier is four occasions and twenty-nine days and is unstruck**; **the leash is at eight uses and 0116 and 0120 are correctly non-uses**; and **the longest repeated prose run inside a chapter is seventeen words and across the batch is twenty-three, and both are the volume's own motif sentences.**

- **THE REVERSE SWEEP WAS RUN TWICE, which is the direction the error has now gone more than a dozen times: the new state text was searched for every word and figure removed from the prose.** *This book*, *this whole book*, *three volumes*, *this chapter*, *in a volume that is not this one*, *the next writer of this book*, *The Day Before The Volume Ends*, *Day 120*, *Day 110* and *nine hundred feet* were each searched across `state/`, `outline/`, `workspace/` and the chapters. **Clean, with two deliberate survivals: *in this book* in a character's own day-book and in a store's ledger, which are the man's book and the district's book; and one *Day 110* in the Batch 0003 prompt, which is the real Day 110 of Chapter 0124.** Every other hit was itself a sweep result, which is the correct shape.

- **WHAT THE REVIEWER VERIFIED CLEAN AND THIS PASS DID NOT DISTURB:** the day map; the clock; no weekday or month; no age; the register at thirteen; every standing boundary including the Silent Muster's six unread clauses, the untouched five dates, Mara Vale unreached and no letter written to her, and nobody from Kells on the road; power discipline, with nothing rung, no perception attempted and the carrier unrolled and put back; custody, with the §10b fixes to Chapter 0120 all holding; the leash at eight; and the new Batch 0003 prompt, which is structurally sound, carries Days 107 to 116, and instructs the next writer not to create a volume-close prompt and not to pre-create any later phase.

## 10d. VOLUME 03 BATCH 0003 — the batch record, the fixes made before the files were saved, and the sweeps

**The batch ran Day 107 to Day 116, one chapter to a day, and it changed no day, no custody state, no figure that a later chapter may lean on, and no standing refusal. It is not to be restarted. Everything below is a defect that was drafted and fixed in the prose, or a measurement taken after the files were saved.**

- **THE VOLUME'S RESERVED MIDPOINT REVERSAL IS PAID, IN CHAPTER 0125, AND IT IS PAID AS A DUTY AND NOT A SENTENCE.** The edited transcript of Lira Vale's trial is on the page: about four hundred words of the record in the office's own hand, and eleven lines in her own words in a fold at the back, saying that she found that answers were being copied and that she took nine things and hid them herself, and naming the thing once. **THE WORD APPEARS ON THE PAGE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS VOLUME, INSIDE THAT TRANSCRIPT, IN HER OWN WORDS, AND NOWHERE ELSE IN THE BATCH — one instance, at 0125:47, and the sweep across Chapters 121 to 130 returns no other.** It does not say whether she was imprisoned, killed, or used as an anchor, and no chapter of this volume may make it say so. **The absence is stated plainly in Chapter 0125, in his own hand, more than once, and it is not a cliff.**
- **THE RESERVED WORD WAS DRAFTED INTO THE WRONG PLACE TWICE AND MOVED.** An early pass had the district's list of matters in Chapter 0123 name the thing, and the store's book in Chapter 0121 name it separately, which would have put the word on two pages and in a narrator's mouth. **The discovery now works the other way round and is better for it: the store's book says *coupling* and the only person in the kingdom who has ever used the other word hid nine of them and said so in a room, and the district's own list has a word for it and the word is a word about a piece of metal.**
- **THE MATTER DOES NOT BEGIN AS THE TRIAL.** The number 4, 1 8 2 was carried out of a store's book as a repair to a survey carrier in a district he has never stood in, and the court entry for the same number turned out to be about a woman. **This is deliberate and it is the batch's method: the number got him into the room and not to the thing he wanted, and the thing he wanted was on the same rail in the same bundle and he had to keep reading.** No collision of numbering is left on the page and both chapters state the line in the same words.
- **A CHAPTER OPENED WITH A CLERK KNOWING WHAT HE HAD COME FOR.** An early pass had the district-court clerk say *Netherby* before Tomas had spoken, which is the reviewer's finding from §10c — a stranger knowing too much — repeated. **Tomas now says the number and the district himself, and the clerk's reply is the two things that follow from it.** The same fix was applied to Chapter 0124, where Hale no longer knows anything about a man with a barrow's habits.
- **THE FIFTH CLAUSE WAS MISCOUNTED TWICE.** It had done four jobs by Day 106, and Chapter 0124 says **five** and dates the fifth to the hundred and tenth, which is the day it happened in: it stopped him being a man in a room. Chapter 0121 and the day-books do not state a job-count at all.
- **THE ELAPSED-DAY FIGURES WERE WRONG IN FIVE PLACES AND ALL FIVE ARE CORRECTED TO THE DAY MAP.** *Eleven days into the city* at 0121 is **twelve**; *nineteen days in this city* at 0129 is **twenty**; and the four uses of *a fortnight* as an elapsed span in Chapters 0122, 0128 and 0130 are replaced with **thirteen days, nineteen days, nineteen days, nineteen days, and sixteen days** respectively. The one surviving *fortnight* is in Chapter 0125 and is inside a clerk's hypothetical about what he would have done, and is not an elapsed figure and stays.
- **A COUNT OF BUILDINGS WAS REMOVED RATHER THAN FIXED.** The day-books of 0122 and 0129 said *six buildings in this city*, and the prior text's count of *two* could not be reconciled with a third and fourth room without inventing one. **Both are now unquantified — *every room in this city that I have walked into* — because the count is not load-bearing and a wrong count in a state file is this project's own top-listed defect class.**
- **A DAY OF THE WEEK WAS DRAFTED AND REMOVED.** See §3. **A NARRATOR META-LEAK WAS DRAFTED TWICE AND BOTH WERE REMOVED.** See §3. **AN ELAPSED INTERVAL IN 0121 SAID *ELEVEN WEEKS* FOR A HUNDRED AND SEVEN DAYS AND WAS CORRECTED IN TWO PLACES** to *since the morning the carrier came, a hundred and seven days* and *a hundred and seven days of a jumper soldered across a cut*. A duplicated possessive ran together in 0128 and was corrected. **One line in 0122 had no hour in its opening and now opens at the fourth hour**, so that the chapter's clock and the card agree.
- **THE DAY-BOOK DOES NOT RE-COPY THE SCENE. THIS PARAGRAPH IS HISTORY. The certificate here — that apart from the eighty-eight-word sworn sentence the longest run inside a chapter is under ten words — was FALSE, and a review pass measured the true figure at thirty-four words and eight of the ten day-books re-deriving a sentence the narrator has already used. The measured position is §3 and the itemised record is §10e. What holds from this paragraph is the shape of the argument: the day-book is meant to re-derive, not to transcribe, and in the sworn sentence of Chapter 0129 the repetition is a man committing one true sentence to paper three times on purpose.**
- **THE SPEECH-DURATION TIC WAS DISTRIBUTED BEFORE THE CHAPTERS WERE DRAFTED, NOT AFTER.** *About four seconds* belongs to the Office and a plate: a clerk answering *is this form the same form*, a man reading four words upside down at nine feet, and a man reading his own four sentences off a wall. *About nine seconds* survives only as **a court writing a man's own sentence down** and as Tomas measuring himself. Every other man in the batch speaks at his own pace or none: Hale's refusals are flat and unweighted, Salkeld's account of a reader and a swearer comes while walking, Tarrant's two refusals of the shelf are one sentence and then one sentence longer, and the woman with a tray of pins takes about nine minutes to work along a board. **No character in this batch runs on a nine-second metronome and no state file attributes one to a man who does not speak in it.**
- **THE BATCH DOES NOT RUN TEN CHAPTERS OF A MAN AT A COUNTER.** This was the structural debt §10c recorded and it is paid: **five chapters have a counter in them and five do not, and the five that do not are a step by a river and a rope, a public wall and a street, a market and a board, and the last two chapters' weight is on a wall and a rule rather than on a room.** The set is the store (0121), the arch and the courts (0122, 0125, 0126, 0129, 0130), a board read standing up for most of a day (0123), a rail and a folded sheet (0124, 0125), the flat and a rope (0127), and a public wall (0128). **Chapter 0123 is one sustained physical action over most of a day and Chapter 0125 is a document read three times with a refusal in the middle of it, and neither of them is a conversation at a counter.**
- **WHAT WAS CHECKED AFTER THE WRITING AND CAME BACK CLEAN, MEASURED AND NOT CLAIMED — AND ONE OF THESE TWO CERTIFICATES WAS FALSE AND IS CORRECTED IN §10e, SO READ THIS AS HISTORY:** no weekday and no month named in any of the ten chapters, the month sweep returning nothing at all; **no narrator meta-leak in any of the ten — FALSE, three stood, one of them in a section heading, and they are repaired in §10e — and the only in-world uses are a man's own day-book**; **the register reads thirteen in every chapter that states it and no chapter states a total that contradicts it**, and *fourteen* appears only as *not a fourteenth*, as *a hundred and fourteen days* and *the hundred and fourteenth*; **no age on the page for anybody**, including Lira Vale, who is now named in a document and is supplied with no age by anyone; **every daytime hour bare and the ninth hour of the evening the only half-day, carried in all ten chapters**, with one retrospective half-day, the fifth hour of the morning of the thirty-third, marked retrospective in Chapter 0124 and belonging to Day 33; no day renumbered and no day repeated, one chapter to a day, Days 107 to 116, and the form of the date is hundred and seventh through hundred and sixteenth; **no Crown clapper seen or named outside the one page of the transcript, no fitting called one, no mechanism described, no official above a district named, OFF. 411 not on a page, no name transferred, no child found by a document, and nothing in this list about a child**; **all five clauses stand in all ten chapters' day-books, the frame has not been in a building since the thirty-third, and nothing is rung by anybody**; **the leash is at eight uses and 0127 and 0130 are correctly non-uses**; **the five dates are unaccounted for, are not explained away, and are not called a forgery or a conspiracy, and are touched once, in Chapter 0130, only to say he has not touched them in a hundred and sixteen days**; and **Mara's standing instruction is published and nothing is resolved, nothing is rescued, nothing is taken off any board, and no letter is written**.
- **THE *ABOUT NINE* FIGURE IS MEASURED AND NOT CLAIMED: *about nine* stands at 93 across 31,927 words of Chapters 121 to 130, which is 0.29 per hundred words, against 73 across 28,403 in Batch 0002 at 0.26 and 114 across 28,403 in Batch 0001 at 0.40.** The load-bearing ones are all kept — about nine hundred rooms, bells, entries and matters; nine years at a rail, in a yard, at a counter and at a board; nine sheets, nine hundred rows, nine clauses, nine rows on a wall, nine feet, nine clauses of a standing instruction. The ones that are a man choosing a round figure for his own pace were reduced where the prose allowed it, and the batch does not claim a reduction because a 0.03 difference is not one.
- **THE REVERSE SWEEP WAS RUN, which is the direction the error has now gone more than a dozen times: the new state text was searched for every word and figure removed from the prose.** *Tuesday*, *in three volumes*, *in this volume*, *eleven weeks*, *six buildings*, *fortnight* and *Day 120* were each searched across `state/`, `outline/`, `workspace/` and the chapters. **Clean, with deliberate survivals: the six swept strings inside the sweep records themselves, and the one *Day 110* in the Batch 0004 prompt, which is the real Day 110 of nothing in this batch and which the next writer should check rather than trust.** Every other hit was itself a sweep result, which is the correct shape. **Two of §10d's own certificates were false and are corrected in §10e, and this paragraph's *Day 110* remark was itself wrong and is corrected there: Day 110 IS a day of this batch, it is Chapter 0124, and the Batch 0004 prompt's use of it is correct.**

## 10e. VOLUME 03 BATCH 0003 — THE POST-BATCH REVIEW PASS, every finding, and every fix

**This pass ran against a reviewer report on the finished chapters. It changed no event, no motive, no refusal, no document, no custody state, no scene, no chapter title, and no day in the batch's map. It is a repair pass, not a rewrite: the batch is not to be restarted and the plot of Chapters 121 to 130 is exactly as it was written. The reviewer verified the day map, the clock, the register, the boundaries and the custody independently and found all of them clean, and this pass did not disturb any of them.**

**THE HEADLINE IS THAT THREE OF THE FALSE CERTIFICATES WERE IN THE STATE LAYER, NOT THE PROSE, AND THAT IS THE THIRD TIME THIS PROJECT HAS PRODUCED IT.** §10c found the same class in Batch 0002 and recorded the lesson: *a repair pass is not a certificate, and a sweep that searches for four strings has not searched.* **The meta-leak sweep for this batch searched for a short list of literal strings and certified zero; three instances were standing, in three chapters, one of them in a section heading. The repeated-prose certificate of *under ten words* was false by a factor of three and a half, and the true figure is thirty-four.** Both certificates are corrected at §3 and the writing-time record above is left standing as history, which is the treatment §10c set.

- **BLOCKING 1 — THREE NARRATOR META-LEAKS STOOD AND THE SWEEP CERTIFIED ZERO.** Repaired. *The other half is the batch's engine* (0122:41, a section heading) is now *and the other half is two documents instead of one*; *the thing the chapter has to have on the page* (0127:95) is now *the one thing he had not written down, and a man has to write down the things he does not do or he will do them in a month and call them new*; *it is the seed of the two days after this one* (0123:41, a section heading) is now *it is the thing he will come back for in two days*. **All three were recast in the world rather than deleted, and each recast is more accurate to the beat than the phrase it replaced.** The sweep now returns nothing in the narrator, and the surviving hits are the permitted in-world ones — *in this book* in the man's own day-book at 0128, 0129, and *in this room* in a clerk's mouth at 0121:57, which is a room and not a volume. **The four day-book uses of *in this book* are the distinction §10c drew and they are left exactly as they are.**

- **BLOCKING 2 — A CHAPTER'S CENTRAL BEAT WAS ATTRIBUTED TO THE WRONG CLAUSE.** Chapter 0124:91 said the thing he did not do was *the fourth clause*, and it is the **fifth** — the clause about a carrier and a household, dated in the same chapter to Day 33, quoted at 0124:95, and defined in its own day-book at 0124:123, where the fourth is the carrier-and-road-and-box-handle clause. **Corrected to the fifth clause.** Every other job-count of the fifth clause in the batch, in this chapter and in the state layer, was already right.

- **BLOCKING 3 — THREE IRRECONCILABLE COUNTS OF ONE EVENT IN CHAPTER 0121.** The narrator had Tarrant refusing him *twice in nine years*; Tarrant said *the fourth time in nine years and I have said no three times*; and Tomas's own entry had him in the room *three times*. **He has been in that room three times — Day 100, Day 101, Day 107 — and Tarrant has said no twice.** Both are now stated that way: the narrator's *has now told me twice that he is not going to try, and he was right both times*, and Tarrant's *this is the third time you have come up these four flights and I have said no twice*. **Tarrant's *you are the first person in this room in nine years to ask me what one of them was for* is not a count of refusals and is untouched.** No visit was added and no visit removed.

- **BLOCKING 4 — A SHEET WAS NOT ON THE WALL THE DAY THE CHAPTER SAYS IT WAS.** Chapter 0128 established that the sheet had *not been nailed up yet* and the woman with the tray of pins was two boards away. Chapter 0130 then had the arch clerk say he had known *since yesterday, because a sheet came past my counter on a cart at about the sixth hour*, and had two hundred people read it today against four hundred who had read it *yesterday*. **All of it is Day 116 and it is now the sixth hour this morning**, and the four hundred is gone, because a sheet that was nailed up tonight was read by nobody before tonight except the men in the room with the lamp, and they had read it because they had copied it. **About four hundred belongs to the FIRST notice — Marren's, on Day 99 — and Chapter 0130:119 already said so correctly and is untouched.** A related figure in the same day-book, *there are about four hundred more of them in this district*, contradicted the clerk's own *about eleven thousand lines a year* in the same chapter and is now that figure; and *I have found that out four days too late*, which is a day-book written at the ninth hour of the evening of the day the cart went past the counter, is now about an hour after.

- **BLOCKING 5 — A COMPOSITION WAS DATED TO THE WRONG CHAPTER'S DAY.** Chapter 0129 said he wrote the four sentences *on the hundred and fourteenth at about the ninth hour of the evening*, which is Chapter 0128's ninth hour and is occupied by that day's entry. **It is now the night of the hundred and fourteenth, after he had written the day down and put the book away** — which is what makes Chapter 0129's own *I wrote them out on a step last night* true, and it removes the collision. **No day moved and no chapter now carries the ninth hour of the evening twice.**

- **BLOCKING 6 — A STATE FILE CONTRADICTED ITS OWN PROSE ON A PHYSICAL ACT.** `state/current.md` said *a woman standing next to it on the hundred and thirteenth did not touch it either*. Chapter 0127 has her **with the rope in both hands** at 0127:5 and **putting it back up on the frame** at 0127:25. **The prose is right and the state file was wrong, and it was wrong in the direction that makes a character passive, which is the direction that matters.** Corrected: she had it in both hands, working it, and put it back where it hangs, and it belongs to the loft. **`state/current.md` is a required read for Batch 0004 and this error would have propagated.**

- **SHOULD FIX 7 — AN UNREGISTERED FIGURE OF FOUR HUNDRED THOUSAND FORMS, WHICH HAD ALREADY PROPAGATED INTO THE CHAPTER ARCHIVE.** Chapter 0126:21 had a clerk say *there have been about four hundred thousand of them* in a room that receives about nine hundred a year, in a batch that ends by writing four nines out flat precisely so they cannot be added. **The figure is not defensible as a cumulative either, because nothing in this kingdom has been printing forms for four hundred years.** It is now *there is one form in this room and there is one shape of it*, which is the point the sentence exists to make, and the plate and the man who cuts it carry the rest. **`state/chapter-summaries.md:532` carried the same figure and is corrected; the four nines stay four.**

- **SHOULD FIX 8 — A DROPED DIGIT.** Chapter 0127:49 put the market of about nine hundred people at *about nine people*, in a chapter whose opening line and two other passages say about nine hundred and whose whole argument is about how many people a thing said out loud is heard by. **Corrected to about nine hundred.**

- **SHOULD FIX 9 — THE REPEATED-PROSE CERTIFICATE WAS FALSE, ONE CASE WAS REPAIRED, AND THE REST IS RECORDED AS A STANDING DEBT.** The reviewer found one sixty-word near-duplicate: the third of Tomas's three reasons for not going to his sister appeared in the reasons list at 0128:85 and again in the day-book at 0128:111, and the day-book now re-derives it and says on the page that it is the second time he has written that sentence in his own book. **But the certificate of *under ten words* was false everywhere, and the true measurement is at §3: thirty-four words in 0125, twenty-nine in 0124, twenty-three in 0128 and 0130, nineteen in 0123, seventeen in 0122, 0126 and 0127, fifteen in 0121.** Eight of the ten day-books re-derive a sentence the narrator has already used in the same chapter. **§10c ruled on this exact shape for Batch 0002 and called it a defect; the ruling is repeated here and NOT enforced, and the debt is written down with its numbers rather than left as a false clean sweep, because eight good sentences are not worth taking ten chapters apart to satisfy a measurement and because a state file that reports a clean sweep it did not have is the failure this project produces most often.**

- **SHOULD FIX 10 — FOUR PIECES OF MECHANICAL PROSE, ALL REPAIRED, NONE OF THEM A SCENE.** Chapter 0121's closing entry self-negated — *both of those are correct and I have never once been able to make them both correct at the same time and neither can he* — and is now *there is no clause anywhere that requires either one to be different, and I have had that in my head for a week and I have not once been able to put it down without making it smaller*, which is a man's complaint instead of a knot. Chapter 0129:41 and 0129:51 both had him stand nine feet off the rail and say nothing, ten lines apart; **the second is now the counting of the room, which is a different beat and is the one the following paragraph pays off.** That heading also said the silence was *what about four hundred people a year do in that room*, which conflated the four hundred a year who put a sentence on a wall with the nine who are in the room at the fourth hour; **it now says about nine.** Chapter 0124:5 ran five actions in one sentence and is two. Chapter 0128 stated *did not take it off the board, and that is the third time* three times, which is a declared motif; **the third of the three, in the day-book, no longer restates it and the decision is carried instead by *both of those are the same decision and I have been trying since the fourth hour to find a new name for it and there is not one.***

- **SHOULD FIX 11 — A LEAF WAS IN TWO TOWNS AT ONCE.** Chapter 0128 listed, among the things about two hundred people a day walk past on the top terrace of Sennet, *a leaf pinned at an angle that has been up since the ninety-first in a town two days' road back*. **The leaf is on a different board in a different town and the sentence now says so and keeps the beat, which is the better one: nobody stops at the Sennet board and nobody stops at the market one either, and the reason is the same in both towns.** The three boards and three untakings are now pinned at §6b so a later chapter cannot slide them together.

- **SHOULD FIX 12 — THE SHEET'S LINE COUNT WAS WRONG ON THE PAGE.** Chapter 0128 said *four of the five lines on that sheet are about a brother* about a sheet that runs to seven lines plus a signature, and *four lines* is the figure the state layer, the chapter archive and the Batch 0004 prompt all carry. **One line of the sheet is now split in two, so the sheet is eight lines and four of them are about a brother, and the prose's count is true.** The four are pinned at §6b. **No word of the instruction changed and nothing about what she published changed.**

- **THE REVERSE SWEEP WAS RUN TWICE, which is the direction the error has now gone more than a dozen times: the new state text was searched for every word and figure removed from the prose.** *The batch's engine*, *the thing the chapter has to have on the page*, *the seed of the two days*, *it is the fourth clause*, *four hundred thousand*, *since yesterday*, *four of the five lines*, *a woman standing next to it*, *about four hundred people had read it*, *four days too late*, *about four hundred more of them*, *the hundred and fourteenth at about the ninth hour of the evening* and *in front of about nine people* were each searched across `state/`, `outline/`, `workspace/` and the chapters. **Clean, with deliberate survivals: the swept strings inside this record and inside §3, and the in-world *in this book* in a character's own day-book.** Every other hit was itself a sweep result, which is the correct shape. **One correction fell out of the reverse sweep and is recorded above: §10d's remark that the Batch 0004 prompt's *Day 110* is *the real Day 110 of nothing in this batch* is itself false. Day 110 is Chapter 0124 and it is a real day of this batch, and the prompt is right.**

- **WHAT WAS CHECKED AFTER THIS PASS AND CAME BACK CLEAN, MEASURED AND NOT CLAIMED:** **the day map, Days 107 to 116, one chapter to a day, nothing renumbered and nothing repeated, re-measured after the repairs and unchanged**; **no narrator meta-leak in the narrator in any of the ten**, and the only hits are a man's own day-book and a room in a clerk's mouth; **no weekday and no month named in any of the ten**, both sweeps returning nothing; **the register reads thirteen in every chapter that states it and no chapter states a total that contradicts it**, and *fourteen* appears only as *not a fourteenth* and in *a hundred and fourteen days*; **no age on the page for anybody**, including Lira Vale; **every daytime hour bare and the ninth hour of the evening the only half-day on a live hour, carried in all ten chapters**, with the one retrospective half-day in Chapter 0124 belonging to Day 33, **and Chapter 0129 no longer carries the ninth hour of the evening a second time**; **no Crown clapper seen or named outside the one page of the transcript, no fitting called one, OFF. 411 not on a page, no name transferred, no child found by a document**, and *muster* and *Renewal* absent from the batch; **all five clauses stand, the frame has not been in a building since the thirty-third, and nothing is rung by anybody**; **the leash is at eight uses and 0127 and 0130 are correctly non-uses**; **the five dates are unaccounted for and untouched**; and **Mara's standing instruction is published, nothing is resolved, nothing is rescued, nothing is taken off any board, and no letter is written**.

- **WHAT THE REVIEWER VERIFIED CLEAN AND THIS PASS DID NOT DISTURB:** the day map; the half-day clock, including the single retrospective Day-33 hour; the register at thirteen and the permitted uses of *fourteen*; every standing boundary, including the one appearance of the reserved word in Chapter 0125's transcript and OFF. 411's absence; no ringing anywhere, with the carrier untouched, Tamsin's bell unringed, no Crown bell rung and the leash at eight; no age on any page, including for the newly named Lira Vale; the Batch 0004 prompt, which carries Days 117 to 126, gets the interval arithmetic right, names both structural debts, creates exactly one next phase and forbids a volume-close prompt. **The reviewer also raised one item outside the remit of any writer: `state/phase-ledger.json` reads `phase-000-bootstrap`, status `planned`, attempts `0`, after thirteen committed batches. That file is controller-owned and is NOT edited here. It is recorded and left to whoever owns it.**
- **AND ONE FINDING THE REVIEWER RAISED AND THEN WITHDREW, recorded so it is not raised twice: Chapter 0129's *I have been carrying that for five days* is correct** — the record of his own hearing is Day 110 and the chapter is Day 115 — **and must not be changed.**

## 10f. VOLUME 03 BATCH 0004 — the writing-time record, the fixes made before the files were saved, and the sweeps

**The batch ran Day 117 to Day 126, one chapter to a day, and it changed no day, no custody state, no standing refusal, and no event. It is not to be restarted. This section is the writing-time record and it is NOT a certificate; the reviewer who reads it next should assume, on this project's own evidence, that something in it is wrong, and the three most likely places are named at the end.**

- **THE RESERVED WORD WAS DRAFTED INTO FIVE PLACES AND REMOVED FROM ALL FIVE BEFORE THE FILES WERE SAVED.** The Batch 0004 prompt forbade the word outright, not only outside a quotation of the eleven lines, and a first pass used it three times in Chapter 0136 and twice in Chapter 0139, in the standing description of the fixed stripped Crown survey carrier. **The object is unchanged and the description is now *nothing in it that can strike* and *the receipt forbids any person from putting anything into it that could*. A later writer must not draft it back, and the sweep across Chapters 131 to 140 now returns nothing.**
- **A NARRATOR META-LEAK WAS DRAFTED TWICE AND REMOVED TWICE**, both before the files were saved: a section heading at 0132 reading *the not-saying is the chapter* (now *the whole of it*), and *the whole of this volume has been me carrying the first one in his head* in a day-book at 0135 (now *the whole of a hundred and twenty-one days*). **The sweep now returns nothing in the narrator, and the only in-world uses are a man's own day-book.**
- **THE DAY-BOOK RE-DERIVATION DEFECT WAS FIXED BEFORE THE FILES WERE SAVED, NOT AFTER, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS THE RECORD.** A first pass left seven day-books transcribing a sentence the narrator had already used in the same chapter, in runs of up to sixty-three words — worse than the thirty-four that §10e measured and accepted. **All seven were rewritten to re-derive. The measured position now is: the longest run inside a chapter is FORTY-ONE WORDS in Chapter 0140, and the other seven chapters with a day-book are at fourteen, twenty, twenty-two, twenty-three, twenty-four, twenty-five, twenty-six, thirty-four.** Chapter 0140's is a man committing the batch's closing sentence to paper twice on purpose, in the narrator and in the day-book, and it is recorded as a standing debt and not a clean sweep.
- **TWO FIGURES IN THE CARDS WERE WRONG AGAINST CHAPTER 0130 AND BOTH WERE CORRECTED IN THE PROSE.** The prompt's card for Chapter 0131 said *about four hundred have read it since*, and *about four hundred* is what read Marren's first notice on Day 99 and is also how many people a year put a sentence on the river-court wall; **the chapters give the figure once only, about two hundred people in about nine minutes on the first evening, and then say that a market is held every day and nobody counts and nobody is going to.** The prompt's card for Chapter 0139 also had about four hundred on a wall; corrected to about two hundred people a day, which is the established figure for the boards on that terrace. **The net-loft rope was also checked: it belongs to the loft, hangs where it hangs, and on Day 120 nobody has it in their hands.**
- **THE BATCH DOES NOT RUN TEN CHAPTERS OF A COUNTER.** This was the structural debt §10c recorded and §10e answered in Batch 0003. **Batch 0004 has a counter in one chapter and a second counter for half an hour in another, and the set is a market, a market trestle, a room off a lane, two public walls, a public room, a room over a shop paid for by the hour, a bench, a street, a gateway, a yard, a pitch, and a mile of river road.** Its sustained physical action is a man walking a road asking three questions at nine bells, and a man standing in three places saying nothing to anybody, **and neither of them is a conversation.** Four of the ten chapters have a man asking one question and being answered in about four seconds, and in all four the person answering is right and is not thanked.
- **WHAT WAS MEASURED AND IS REPORTED AS MEASURED AND NOT AS A CLEAN SWEEP:** **the day map, Days 117 to 126, one chapter to a day, nothing renumbered and nothing repeated**; **no narrator meta-leak in the narrator in any of the ten**; **no weekday and no month named in any of the ten, and no weekday or month was drafted into this batch and removed, which is a measured zero and not a clean sweep**; **every daytime hour bare and the ninth hour of the evening the only half-day, carried in all ten chapters, with no retrospective half-day at all**; **the register reads thirteen in every chapter that states it and no chapter states a total that contradicts it**, and *fourteen* appears only as *fourteen days* and as *fourteen pounds two ounces*; **no age on the page for anybody, including Mara Vale, Hob Kersey and Nyle Prout**, and eight people entered in this batch with none; **nothing rung by anybody**, the carrier unstruck and its interval forty to forty-nine days correct chapter by chapter; **the leash at eight uses and Chapter 0134 a correct non-use which is not written down**; **the five dates untouched**; **Mara's choice on the page, unread, unresolved, unthanked, and nothing taken off any board**; **no Crown clapper seen or named anywhere, no fitting called one, no mechanism described, no official above a district named, OFF. 411 not on a page, no name transferred, no child found by a document, and neither *muster* nor *Renewal* anywhere in the batch**; and **no stray shell text in any file written, with the literal strings `EOF` and `head -5` searched for in all ten chapters and in every state file this batch wrote and found only inside the sweep record itself.**
- **THE *ABOUT NINE* FIGURE IS MEASURED AND IS HIGHER THAN THE LAST TWO BATCHES: 124 across 34,666 words, which is 0.36 per hundred words, against 0.29 in Batch 0003 and 0.26 in Batch 0002.** The load-bearing figures are all kept and the honest number is the one above, because a state file that reports an improvement it did not make is the failure this project produces most often.
- **THE REVERSE SWEEP WAS RUN TWICE, which is the direction the error has now gone more than a dozen times: the new state text was searched for every word and figure removed from the prose and from the previous batch's state layer.** *Tuesday*, *the whole of this volume*, *the not-saying is the chapter*, *clapper*, *no clapper in it*, *furnishing a clapper*, *about four hundred people a year walk past*, *about nine hundred people a year walk past*, *six weeks*, *eleven weeks* and *Day 120* were each searched across `state/`, `outline/`, `workspace/` and the chapters. **Clean, with deliberate survivals: the swept strings inside this record and inside `state/batch-0014-summary.md`, and *in this book* in a character's own day-book.**
- **WHERE A REVIEWER SHOULD LOOK FIRST, ON THIS PROJECT'S OWN EVIDENCE, AND THE THREE ARE:** **(1) the duplicate-prose figure, which is measured and is worse than the last batch's and is recorded rather than hidden; (2) the *about nine* count, which is higher than the last two batches and is reported as measured; (3) this paragraph, which is the writing-time record and is not a certificate, because in Batch 0002 and Batch 0003 a writing-time sweep certified two things that were not true.**

## 10g. VOLUME 03 BATCH 0004 — THE POST-BATCH REVIEW PASS, every finding, and every fix

**This pass ran against a reviewer report on the finished chapters and it changed no event, no motive, no refusal, no document, no custody state, no scene, no chapter title, and no day in the batch's map. It is a repair pass, not a rewrite: the batch is not to be restarted and the plot of Chapters 131 to 140 is exactly as it was written. The reviewer measured the batch mechanically against the house style of the rest of the novel, and the one thing it found was structural and it was in all ten chapters.**

- **BLOCKING 1 — THE BATCH WAS WRITTEN AS A BEAT SHEET WITH PROSE BETWEEN THE BEATS, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS AGAINST THE WHOLE NOVEL.** Chapters 0131 to 0140 carried **seventy-three `##` section headings, five to nine a chapter, and every one of them was a full-sentence statement in the narrator's voice of the finding, the price or the shape of the section it sat over** — *And here is the finding, and it is four inches long*, *And here is the price, and he did the arithmetic standing on a step outside*, *And the arrangement is not a bell and it is not a signal and it is not three answers*. Set with a `---` rule above each one, a chapter read as an outline with scenes attached to it. **Volume 01 has no `##` heading in any chapter. Volume 02 has three, in three chapters. Chapters 0101 to 0120 have none. The drift begins in Batch 0003 at zero to four a chapter and Batch 0004 escalated it to five to nine.** AGENTS.md is explicit: *never replace a scene with a list of actions, states, checks, or beats*, and *do not write an outline, synopsis, checklist, chapter log, or meta commentary as the chapter itself*. **All seventy-three headings and the seventy-three rules that served them are removed. The count is zero and the ten chapters are continuous fiction again.** The removal is a pure deletion of scaffolding: **no sentence of prose was deleted with it, and every heading that carried a fact the body did not have was re-entered as an ordinary sentence at the beat it belonged to** — the room off the lane and its lamp in 0132, the walk down to the river courts in 0133, the four instruments in 0136, the counter's notice in 0140, the shape of the warning and the ninth-hour crown in 0139, and the finding and the reason-is-four-inches-long in 0131.

- **BLOCKING 2 — THE REMOVAL TOOK THE CLOCK WITH IT, AND THE CLOCK WAS THE PROOF.** §5a cited twenty-nine live hours across the ten days and **fourteen of those hours were on the page only inside a `##` heading, so a state file was asserting hours that no chapter gave.** The house rule is in §2: *if a chapter does not give an age, a count or an hour, the state file records that it is not on the page*. **Every hour §5a names is now on the page, put back as bare prose at the beat the clock's own parenthetical describes**, and the ninth hour of the evening is carried in each day-book's opening line rather than in a repeated scaffold paragraph, which also removes the near-verbatim thirty-word repetition of *a step at the foot of the top stair with the tarpaulin over the cradle and the four bolts made fast* that had stood as a heading six times in the batch.

- **BLOCKING 3 — SEVEN OF THE TEN CLOCK ENTRIES HAD INFLATED OCCURRENCE COUNTS, AND THE ERROR IS THE HEADINGS' FAULT.** **The fourth hour was counted two, three or four times a day in seven chapters because every ninth-hour heading restated *the fourth hour* in its sentence about the four bolts, and the count was taken over the file with the headings in it.** §5a is corrected: **the fourth hour is live once on Day 121, once on Day 124, twice on Days 117, 120, 122, 123, 125 and 126, and three times on Days 118 and 119; the sixth hour is one and not two on Days 118 and 122; the seventh hour is one and not two on Day 121 and three and not two on Day 125.** Day 117 was already right and is unchanged. **The clock is measured against the chapters now and every entry names what it rests on.**

- **BLOCKING 4 — FIVE RETROSPECTIVE BARE HOURS WERE ON THE PAGE AND THE CLOCK SAID THERE WERE NONE.** The clock claimed *not one retrospective half-day either*, which is true and was never the point; what it did not record is that the batch quotes **five bare hours belonging to other days** — the fourth hour of Day 110 at 0136, the eighth hour of Day 106 at 0136 and again at 0139, the fourth hour of Day 121 at 0137, the fourth hour of Day 124 at 0140, and the sixth hour of Day 106 at 0139. **They are bare, they are correct, and they are now listed at §5a so that a later writer does not convert one into a live hour of the day it is quoted in.** They are not half-days and no half-day was attached to any of them.

- **BLOCKING 5 — A FORTY-ONE WORD DAY-BOOK RE-TRANSCRIPTION, WHICH §10f CLAIMED TO HAVE FIXED AND DID NOT.** §10f certified *the longest run inside a chapter is FORTY-ONE WORDS in Chapter 0140* and accepted it as a standing debt. **The run was real and it was not a debt: Chapter 0140's day-book was re-printing three narrator paragraphs — the not-a-network paragraph, the two-hundred-read-it paragraph and the other-two-have-said-no paragraph — word for word, forty-one words of it, which is the defect §10c ruled on when it called the re-transcription a defect and not a virtue.** §10f fixed seven day-books and missed this one. **The day-book now re-derives and the measured batch longest is thirty-four words, in Chapter 0139, which is equal to Batch 0003 and no longer worse than it.** The other runs, between fifteen and twenty-six words, are the volume's motif sentences — the reason for not asking, the fourth box, the four inches, the part of a thing nobody can put a question to — and are recorded at §3 as a standing debt, on the §10e precedent, and are not rewritten.

- **BLOCKING 6 — A SENTENCE WAS BROKEN IN HALF AND DUPLICATED IN THE STATE LAYER, AND IT IS THE FOURTH TIME.** `state/current.md` had an all-capitals block **injected inside an italic sentence**, splitting *A format is not a name and has no door and cannot be asked a question* across the injection, **and the same block repeated immediately below as a stray bullet in the middle of a list**, breaking it. **The sentence is restored whole and the batch-0004 addition stands once, as its own paragraph.** §10c found this class in Batch 0002 and §10e found it again in Batch 0003 and named it the third time; **this is the fourth and it is recorded as a fact about the direction the error travels, which is from the prose outward into the state layer and not the other way.**

- **BLOCKING 7 — `state/current.md`'s SWEEP PARAGRAPH WAS REPORTING THE WRONG CHAPTERS AND CONTRADICTED ITSELF IN ONE SENTENCE.** It described Chapters 121 to 130 as *this batch* and carried a self-negating certificate — *the narrator meta-leak sweep returns nothing in the narrator — it did not, and three instances were found and repaired, and the two that were drafted are now three*. **It is replaced with a true certificate for Chapters 131 to 140, re-measured after this pass, and it names §10g.**

- **SHOULD FIX 8 — A CONFLATION §10e HAD ALREADY RULED ON WAS RE-INTRODUCED IN THIS BATCH, IN A HEADING, AND HAD PROPAGATED INTO TWO STATE FILES.** §10e corrected Chapter 0129 for saying *what about four hundred people a year do in that room*, which conflated the four hundred a year who go into the court hall to have a sentence written on the wall with the nine who are in the room at an hour. **Chapter 0131's heading said *what about four hundred people a year do at a rail in a court hall four streets off*, which is the same conflation.** It is corrected on the page and in `state/batch-0014-summary.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md`: **about nine people an hour at a rail, and about four hundred people a year going into that hall to have a true sentence of their own written for fourpence.** §10e stands as the record of the first correction and is not altered.

- **SHOULD FIX 9 — A DAY-COUNT THAT WAS IN A HEADING AND IS NOT TRUE ANYWAY WAS CORRECTED OUT OF THE STATE LAYER RATHER THAN RESTORED.** Chapter 0140's heading said his own sheet *has been there ten days*. **The sentence went up on the hundred and fifteenth and the chapter is Day 126, which is eleven days, so the figure was wrong as well as scaffold.** The prose never carried it, **the day-book gives no count and says a market has been held every day since and nobody counts, and `state/chapter-summaries.md` no longer asserts ten days.** Nothing was restored and no figure was invented.

- **SHOULD FIX 10 — A RESTORED SENTENCE INTRODUCED A NARRATOR META-LEAK AND THE SWEEP CAUGHT IT.** Putting the Chapter 0140 heading's content back as prose produced *the only sentence in this book he has written on the hundred and twenty-sixth*, which is the narrator referring to the volume. **It is now *the only sentence he has written in his own book*, which is his day-book and is in the world.** This is the one error this pass made and the standing weekday, month, meta-leak, register, age, half-day and shell-text sweeps caught it before the files were saved. It is recorded because a repair pass that introduces a defect and catches it is the good case, and a repair pass that introduces one and certifies it clean is the failure this project has already committed twice.

- **WHAT WAS RE-MEASURED AFTER THIS PASS AND CAME BACK CLEAN:** **no `##` heading in any of the ten**, which is the house style; **every hour §5a names is on the page and every daytime hour is bare and the ninth hour of the evening is the only half-day, carried in all ten**, five retrospective bare hours listed and none converted; **no narrator meta-leak in the narrator**, the only hits being a man's own day-book saying *in this book*; **no weekday and no month named**, the month hits all being the modal *may*; **the register reads thirteen in every chapter that states it**, and *fourteen* appears only as *fourteen days* and *fourteen pounds two ounces*; **no age on the page for anybody, including Mara Vale, Hob Kersey, Nyle Prout, Corvin Hale and Salkeld**; **no stray shell text**; **nothing rung by anybody**, the carrier unstruck, and the interval forty to forty-eight days correct chapter by chapter; **the leash at eight uses**; **all five clauses stand and the frame has not been in a building since the thirty-third**; and **the five dates untouched, Mara's choice unread and unthanked, and nothing taken off any board.**

- **THE REVERSE SWEEP WAS RUN, which is the direction the error has now gone more than a dozen times: every figure and phrase removed from the prose was searched for across `state/`, `outline/`, `workspace/` and the chapters.** *Ten days*, *four hundred people a year do at a rail*, *FORTY-ONE WORDS*, *124 across 34,666*, *0.36 per hundred words* and *the fourth hour, bare, four times* were each searched. **Clean, with deliberate survivals: the swept strings inside this record, inside §5a and inside `state/batch-0014-summary.md`, where the old figure is kept beside its correction so that a false certificate is not silently replaced by a new one that cannot be checked.**

- **AND THE FINDING THE REVIEWER DID NOT RAISE, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT MISSED TWICE: the same scaffolding was used in Batch 0003, at zero to four headings a chapter, and §10e reviewed that batch and did not raise it.** **Chapters 0121 to 0130 still carry their headings and they are not repaired here, because they are not this batch and a repair pass does not reach backwards into a closed batch.** It is named for whoever owns the next Volume 03 pass. **The same applies to the three headings in Volume 02.**

## 11b. VOLUME 03 BATCH 0005 — THE CLOSING BATCH, what it paid, and TWO INHERITED DISAGREEMENTS THE PROSE DECIDED

**Chapters 141–150, Days 127–136. The volume closes on Chapter 150 on Day 136 and there is no batch after this one. `outline/volume-03.md`, in its *New question for the next volume* section, is the statement of what Volume 04 opens on, and the new question it names is paid forward in Chapter 0150's last paragraph and in the schedule in Chapter 0148. `outline/ending.md` is the fixed final destination for the whole series and is binding on every later volume; it has no Volume 04 section and is not a plan for Volume 04.**

**The nine things this batch was required to pay, and where each one landed:**

1. **The public truth bell — Chapters 0141 and 0144.** A claim in metal cannot be edited, cannot be added to, cannot be taken back, cannot have an hour put on it, and cannot be shown to anybody on a page. The shape is four sentences and about nine minutes and it is the same shape as 0141, 0143 and 0139 and he has never improved it. **He made it on Day 127 in his own hand and did not write it out on a sheet and did not say it to anybody. He struck it on Day 130, once, in a yard on the top terrace, in the ordinary way, and he could not add a word to it, because a man ringing a bell he did not make says whatever the bell's claim is, and the claim in that bell was the hour.** **He is not stronger after it and there is no stage and he is in a room with a form in it on Day 133.** The finding that made the batch possible: *the only instrument in this kingdom that cannot be edited is the one he used, and the edit was not his, and afterwards there is no form in this kingdom that can tell the time off a true thing.*
2. **The warrant is a copy of his own bell, lawfully, and not a lie — Chapter 0147.** A signal is a real signal and a warrant is what an office does with a real signal; a copy of a Crown sheet is that office's from the moment it is made; **the Office is right about the procedure and wrong about the standing and he has no standing to be wrong about, and that asymmetry is the whole of the volume.** The fourth box of the form is not three words: it is the sound somebody made in a yard, and it is true, and it can be produced against him for forty years by a stranger he will never meet. **He is not sorry for the bell.**
3. **A forced relay runs the line to exhaustion and Tamsin Rook takes the worst of it, and nobody asks her for a reason — Chapter 0146.** She was on a sill and a floor and a wall and she is the one who reads vibration. **She lost more hearing. Nothing is restored and nothing will be. Nobody asked her for a reason at any hour, and he did not ask her for a reason, and the chapter says that describing how hard that was would be asking to be thanked for not asking.**
4. **The one permitted power change, and it is not a stage — Chapter 0146.** He took the thing that came through him because there was nobody else in it. **He did not decide anything and he did not cut anything, and afterwards he finds out that she had decided before she said the sentence and was telling him after, and that he was not asked because he could not have been asked.** He paid in somebody else's hearing and in his own balance and he gained nothing he can carry: he could not keep his feet for about four minutes and could not hold a straight edge afterwards. **He did not identify a copied answer, did not learn where a sound went, and is not told.**
5. **He is arrested in a room with a form in it, lawfully, and is not rescued — Chapter 0147.** The officer is right, the form is right, the district is right, and no step could have been done differently. **Mara is inside the building and does not come to him, and he does not ask her to, and he writes down that he did not ask, and the reason is the fifth clause and is four inches long.**
6. **He gets out with help he did not ask for from a person he cannot command, and goes on foot, and a physical route map leaves the capital with him that he cannot read — Chapter 0148 and Chapter 0150.** **There is no form in this kingdom that lets a district keep a man and there is no form that lets a court keep a man either, and a matter goes where a matter goes and the person named in it goes with it.** A schedule is part of a paper. **The map is a map and not a key, there is no form for reading one, and the removed rectangle is on the page and is not explained in this batch and is not joined to anything.**
7. **The eleven lines leave his head, and the only way they can — Chapter 0148.** Quoted once, in his own hand, on the back of a leaf of his own book, in about nine minutes, and he does not shake. **The reason is on the page and it is about him and not about her: the only way to get the truest thing he has ever read out of his own head is to make it into the instrument he has been refusing, and the price of having it in his hands is that it is in his hands.** Nobody else writes it. A copy of eleven lines out of a head is not a copy of a court record and is not a fourteenth, and the chapter says so in one sentence.
8. **The three-person arrangement, and he is not the clerk of it — Chapter 0148.** His four clauses stand: no paper, he does not keep the terms, he will not take the same words to two people and come back with two the same, and he will not ask either of them for anything. **The only writing that exists at the end of the volume is four lines in Tamsin Rook's hand, put into the apron of the woman who lets a room by the hour, and he does not hold it and does not know what it says and does not ask, and the chapter says plainly that this was not his doing, that he wanted it, and that he did not ask for it.** See the disagreement below.
9. **Mara chooses the archive and gives him nothing, and does not meet him — Chapter 0147, 0148, 0150.** She does not rescue him, she is not an ally he can summon, nothing between them is resolved, and the last time her name is on a page in this volume it is on a thing she chose and not on a thing he got. **SHE GIVES HIM NO MEANS OF ANY KIND, AND THE MEANS HE WALKS OUT WITH ARRIVE FROM A MAN IN A COAT WHO PUTS THE BUNDLE INTO HIS HANDS UNASKED at 0148, because there is no form that lets a district keep a man; the outline's phrase *giving him the means to go* describes the effect and not her act, and Chapter 0148 says so in one line: *which nobody had asked him to do*.** The fifth untaking is on the page in Chapter 0148, with the nails in the board and the sheet not on them, and the standing reason is four inches long and has not improved in forty-three days. She published what he would prefer hidden, before they ever met, and that stands.

**THE TWO INHERITED DISAGREEMENTS, RECORDED AND NOT REPAIRED, AS THE BATCH PROMPT DIRECTS:**

- **THE OUTLINE SAID *in writing* AND THE PROSE SAYS OTHERWISE, AND THE PROSE WAS RIGHT. THE OUTLINE HAS NOW BEEN REPAIRED AND THE PROSE WAS NOT TOUCHED.** `outline/volume-03.md` at *Concrete resolution* and at the escalation list said the three-person working arrangement is made **in writing**, with three sets of terms. **Chapter 0134's four clauses make a written set of terms impossible, and a last chapter that suddenly produces one unmakes all four.** The terms were said out loud in a room he paid for and there is no paper and there is no plate and there is no clerk. **The only writing in the volume is four lines in her hand that he does not hold.** This was recorded as an unrepaired disagreement at writing time; **it is corrected in the outline now, on the Volume 01 audit precedent that a disagreement between a volume and its outline is fixed in the outline and not in the prose.** §10h is the record.
- **THE BATCH PROMPT ASKS FOR *A PLAIN CARRIER IN A SACK THAT HE HAS NOT STRUCK IN FIFTY-NINE DAYS* AND ALSO FOR *A FOURTH HAND ON THE CROWN*, AND BOTH ARE TRUE OF TWO DIFFERENT ACTS.** The strike counter and the hand counter are separate, the strike counter is the figure every chapter states, and the fourth hand is on the page in Chapter 0141 and in Chapter 0150. **The inherited phrase *not touched since the seventy-seventh* covers the first and not the second, and no later writer may merge them.** Recorded, not papered over.

**What this batch did not do, listed so that Volume 04 inherits it as work:** no Crown office was defeated, no officer relieved of anything, no bell he made unbarred; **a notice came off a board because it was done and the warrant stands and a store issued a fitting against somebody else's instruction on Day 128**; the arrest of Chapter 0132 stands and is not overturned; **the Crown's third kind of bell was rung by a clerk striking somebody else's instruction and this kingdom still has no name for it on a form**; the record of Lira Vale's trial says nothing whatever about what became of her and no chapter of this batch refers to the absence; OFF. 411 is not on a page; no name was transferred and no child was found by a document; the five dates in a drawer were not touched; **Salla Keene is not found and no chapter of this volume may find her by a document**; **the round does not come back and no chapter of this batch puts a cart, a round or a man with a book on a stool at a door in Miremarket**; Tamsin Rook's answer in a clerk's hall in Miremarket is untouched and nobody has learned it is there; and **why the old network accepts a Crown clapper is seeded once by a man stacking sacks in a yard and is not concluded and is not named.**

## 10h. VOLUME 03 BATCH 0005 — THE POST-BATCH REVIEW PASS, every finding, and every fix

**This pass ran against a reviewer report on the finished chapters and the state layer. It changed no event, no motive, no refusal, no document, no custody state, no scene, no chapter title, no day in the batch's map, and no plot. The batch is not to be restarted.
