The reviewer's overall verdict is preserved in `logs/batch-0005.review.log`: the batch's spine is strong and the volume closes honestly, and its sweeps came back clean on weekday and month names, the hour clock, the carrier counters, the register at thirteen, the five clauses, the do-not-age list, no System panel, no new final enemy, Dain not named, no Crown office defeated, no bell of his unbarred, and Tamsin's bell not rung.**

- **BLOCKING 1 — THE PHASE CLOSED THE VOLUME WITHOUT ITS HANDOFF PROMPT.** The writing commit added **zero `PROMPT.md` files**, and `outline/volume-04.md` does not exist, so the volume was declared complete in two files while the next phase had no prompt and no volume plan. **REPAIRED: `workspace/volume-03/audit/PROMPT.md` now exists and is the one and only next-phase prompt — the Volume 03 close-and-audit phase, in the shape of `workspace/volume-01/audit/PROMPT.md`, which creates `outline/volume-04.md` and `outline/batches/volume-04-batch-0001.md` only after the audit passes, per `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`.** It is the only prompt written and no other phase directory was created. **The runner selects the first `PROMPT.md` under `workspace/` whose directory holds no `.done` or `.blocked` marker, in sorted order, and `volume-03/audit` sorts before `volume-03/batch-0005`, so the audit is what the next dispatch picks up.** The runner only writes `workspace/continuation/next/PROMPT.md` when there is no other incomplete phase, so that file is not the handoff and was not touched.

- **BLOCKING 2 — A BELL-HEADCOUNT CONTRADICTION ON THE VOLUME'S CENTRAL EVENT, RESTATED FOUR TIMES WITH THE WRONG NUMBER.** Chapter 0144 establishes the yard at **about two hundred people on the sixth hour** and **about forty at the seventh hour**, and he strikes the bell at the seventh hour; lines 41 and 69 of the same chapter already say about forty. But 0144:59, 0144:77, 0147:83, 0147:85, 0150:25, 0150:47 and 0150:65 all put **about nine hundred people** in the hearing crowd — the volume's motif number for bells, rooms, entries and matters, and the count of the market on the middle terrace, which is a separate and long-established figure and was NOT touched. **ALL SEVEN ARE NOW ABOUT FORTY PEOPLE IN THAT YARD, with the rest of a city of about nine thousand not finding out for about nine minutes that there had been anything in it at all.** Chapter 0144 line 71's fifth-clause paragraph said *a yard in front of about two hundred people* about the seventh-hour stroke and is now about forty. **The repair makes the chapter agree with itself and keeps the motif number where it belongs, and it strengthens the batch's own argument: a single stroke that does not carry far inside a yard is a smaller and sadder instrument than nine hundred doors, which is what 0150's cheapest-and-most-expensive paragraph needs in order to work.**
- **BLOCKING 2b — THE SAME ERROR WAS ALSO IN CHAPTER 0146, WHICH THE REVIEW DID NOT RAISE.** A clerk strikes the same gate bell four times **at about the seventh hour**, and the chapter says **about two hundred people** came out of that yard, which cannot be true of the seventh hour in a yard that holds two hundred at the sixth and forty at the seventh. **Corrected in all three places (0146:57, 0146:65, 0146:73) to about forty.** Chapter 0141's sixth-hour yard of about two hundred on Day 106 is correct and stands, and 0145's village of about two hundred is a different two hundred and stands.

- **BLOCKING 3 — INTERVAL ARITHMETIC, IN FOUR PLACES, AND ONE PLACE WAS RIGHT.** **0147:83 and three places in 0150 said the bell went on *the evening of the day before yesterday*; the bell is the hundred and thirtieth, so from Day 133 it is three days and from Day 136 it is six, and both are now *the evening of the hundred and thirtieth*.** The phrase is used correctly once already, at 0146:7, where Day 132 looks back at Day 130, so this is drift and not a convention. **0148:111 and :123 said the standing reason *has not improved in forty-six days*; it was written out flat on Day 91, so it is thirty-eight days on Day 129 (0143, already correct) and forty-three days on Day 134 — both are now forty-three.** **0150:33 said *I have had sixty days to find out that it is one sentence* about the arranging; the asking was written down on Day 116 and Chapter 0150 is Day 136, so it is twenty days, and it is now twenty.**

- **BLOCKING 4 — THE DAYS-IN-SENNET COUNTER STOPPED UPDATING, IN FOUR PLACES ACROSS CHAPTERS AND STATE.** Arrival is Day 95. **0141:32 (Day 127) and 0144:35 (Day 130) are correct; 0149:75 said *thirty-five days* on Day 135 and is now forty; 0150:17 said *thirty-five days of it* on Day 136 and is now forty-one; `state/current.md` and `state/character-state.md` both said thirty days and both now say forty-one.** The same two state files also said the unanswerable hearing was *thirty days* behind him; **it is Day 103 at the second hour, fixed by the notice on the board and counted twice on the page, so from Day 136 it is thirty-three days, and both files now say thirty-three.** `state/character-state.md` now carries the four permitted values — thirty-two, thirty-five, forty, forty-one — so that a later writer cannot invent a fifth.

- **BLOCKING 5 — CHAPTER 0150 PUT THE WRONG BELL IN THE WRONG PLACE, AND BROKE ITS OWN COUNT.** 0150:25 said *a tenth one went in among them*, merging the district gate bell he struck on the top terrace into the **nine bells in a mile of river road on the flat**, which is a different set of bells and which he had already counted once before in Chapter 0141. The same sentence turned 0150:21's *I am the only man in this kingdom who has it twice* into three. **Corrected: the nine are the nine, the gate bell *is not one of them, is not in a mile of river road and is not a tenth of anything*, and the count of nine in a mile stands at two.** The passage now says where the bell is.

- **BLOCKING 6 — A STAGING GAP IN CHAPTER 0146, AND A HEDGE THAT DESCRIBED HANDS THAT WERE NEVER PUT DOWN.** 0146:19 left him **in the road** and 0146:25 had both hands on the sill of the shuttered shop by the sixth hour, with the move un-narrated, and the hedge *my hands had been on the sill for about four minutes without my putting them there* claimed a passivity the scene could not support. **REPAIRED: he now goes in off the road, gets his boots up on the boards about four feet off her, and puts both hands flat on the frame of the opening, and says in his own hand that he has had a hundred and thirty-two days to decide whether he did that for a reason and has not got one.** The hedge is now *I had put them there myself at ten feet off her because there was nothing else in that room to put a hand on, and I am not going to write down that I did not choose to.* **The forced relay, the nine seconds, what it cost her, and every event of the chapter are unchanged.**

- **BLOCKING 7 — CHAPTER 0146'S TITLE SENTENCE STATED THE OPPOSITE OF ITS OWN SCENE.** The title and the day-book said *I was given the choice and I am not the person who gets to make it*, and the scene has her offer a binary he picks from. **The title is canon and is not changed. The sentence is now true in the scene it describes: she handed him the carrying the way a clerk hands a man a form to sign, the sentence on the front of it was written before either of them spoke, and what was not on offer was whether it runs, how far, or what it is for.** The same sentence is corrected where it recurs in 0150:51 and in `state/character-state.md`. **The decider/channel distinction is untouched and is still the volume's most important line.**

- **BLOCKING 8 — `state/character-state.md` ASSERTED THE OPPOSITE OF THE BATCH, TWICE, IN ADJACENT PARAGRAPHS.** The new Tamsin paragraph was inserted and the old one kept, so the file said **NOBODY ASKS HER FOR A REASON AT ANY HOUR IN ANY CHAPTER OF VOLUME 03** and, two lines later, **AT ANY HOUR IN CHAPTERS 121 TO 130**, and then kept *Her hearing is the subject of no sentence in this batch except one line on the hundred and fifteenth* — which Chapter 0146 flatly contradicts, since that chapter is entirely about her hearing. **The duplicate is removed and the surviving paragraph now states the truth: nobody asks her for a reason at any hour in any chapter of Volume 03, including the chapter she loses it in and including him; the one line in Batch 0003 where her hearing is the subject of a sentence is on the hundred and fifteenth and is about the price of a wall; and in Batch 0005 the subject of her hearing is Chapter 0146 and nothing else, with Chapter 0150 pricing it once and refusing to describe it.** Her bell's paragraph was also stale — it said she did not ring it *in either of the last two batches* and stopped at Chapter 0137 — and now records that **it was not rung in any batch after the first, that Chapter 0143 is a third time it was nearest her hand and not taken out, that Chapter 0146 is a fourth and it is the chapter she loses hearing in, and that it may be rung at most once more in the whole series and never for him.**

- **BLOCKING 9 — `state/character-state.md` SAID NO LETTER FROM KELLS CAME AND THAT A KELLS MAN'S WORK WAS NOT THIS BATCH'S. BOTH WERE FALSE.** The line said *NO LETTER FROM KELLS IS PERMITTED IN THIS BATCH AND NONE CAME — THE PERMITTED LETTER WAS SPENT ON DAY 105* and *Oren Pike's reading of a charge is Batch 0004's work and not this batch's*. **Oren Pike's letter is Chapter 0149, on Day 135, four sides, and reading a charge is the volume's closing human beat and `outline/volume-03.md` requires it at 0149.** The line is rewritten: one letter came on the hundred and thirty-fifth, it is in the inside pocket of the coat, it is not a fourteenth document, **the permitted-letter allowance is now spent twice, on Day 105 and Day 135, and no second letter may be staged in Volume 04 without the volume that earns it.** A sentence was added recording that **Nell Fenn is named in no chapter of Volume 03 and appears in none**, and that the outline has been corrected to the Kells beat the volume delivered.

- **BLOCKING 10 — A DROPPED WORD IN A STATE FILE.** `state/current.md` read **ONE LETTER FROM CAME IN THIS BATCH**. **It now reads ONE LETTER FROM KELLS CAME IN THIS BATCH.**

- **SHOULD FIX 11 — `NOVEL_SPEC.md` DECLARED FIFTY CHAPTERS MORE THAN IT DID IN TWO OF ITS OWN CLAUSES.** The same file said *nobody asks her for a reason at any hour in any of the one hundred and forty chapters* and *has gained no power in Chapters 11–140*. **Both are now one hundred and fifty and Chapters 11–150.**

- **SHOULD FIX 12 — `NOVEL_SPEC.md` SAID MARA *GAVE HIM THE MEANS TO GO*, WHICH THE PROSE SAYS SHE DID NOT DO.** The volume outline states it correctly and Chapter 0148 puts the bundle into his hands at the line *which nobody had asked him to do*. **The compression is corrected: she chooses the archive, gives him nothing, and is not met, and the means arrive from the officer in the coat because there is no form that lets a district keep a man.**

- **SHOULD FIX 13 — A CROSS-REFERENCE THAT DOES NOT RESOLVE, IN TWO FILES.** `NOVEL_SPEC.md` and `state/current.md` both called `outline/ending.md` *the binding statement of what Volume 04 opens on*. **`outline/ending.md` has no Volume 04 section — it goes from the final external conflict to Chapters 501–510.** Both files now say that `outline/ending.md` is the fixed final destination for the whole series and is binding on every later volume, and that `outline/volume-03.md`'s *New question for the next volume* is the statement of what Volume 04 opens on. `state/continuity.md` §11b carried the same bad reference and is corrected.

- **SHOULD FIX 14 — THE OUTLINE NAMED A RELATIONSHIP BEAT THE VOLUME NEVER DELIVERED, AND NO STATE FILE RECORDED THE SUBSTITUTION.** `outline/volume-03.md` at *Relationship and emotional progression* names **Tomas and Nell Fenn** as a volume-level beat: *she is sixteen and in Kells and the volume's best civic act is a thing she does without him, and it is a thing he never hears about until a letter arrives eleven days late.* **She has zero appearances by name anywhere in `chapters/volume-03/`, all fifty chapters searched, and no letter in the volume is eleven days late.** On the Volume 01 audit precedent this is corrected **in the outline and not in the prose**: the beat is now the Kells beat the volume actually delivered, **Oren Pike in a chair in a real building four days' road away, who read the first charge against the Office's warrant, would not let Tomas explain what it meant, and told him so in writing so that he cannot afterwards say he was told.** The outline already carried the Oren beat in the same list, so the fix removes a false entry and keeps the true one.

- **SHOULD FIX 15 — THE VOLUME'S FINAL-CHAPTER IMAGE CARRIED THE WRONG CROWD.** `outline/volume-03.md`'s *Final chapter image* said *about nine hundred of them have been told nothing at all about what to decide*, which is the line Chapter 0150 no longer says. **It now says about forty people in a yard on the top terrace, and no more.**

- **AND THE FINDING THE REVIEWER DID NOT RAISE, RECORDED SO IT IS NOT MISSED TWICE: the batch's own figures were checked chapter by chapter against the day map and two more interval errors were found and repaired.** **0148 called the three-person arrangement *made in a room over that shop on the hundred and twentieth* and *a hundred and twenty days*, and that is CORRECT — the arrangement is Chapter 0134, Day 120, and Chapter 0143 on Day 129 is a second meeting about three independent answers and not the arrangement.** It was checked before being changed and was left alone, and the same applies to the carrier's fifty to fifty-nine days, the hand-on-the-crown count of four, the hundred-and-twenty-ninth to hundred-and-thirty-sixth volume-day counters, and the twenty-three-days-behind figure that was correct on Day 126 and is thirty-three days on Day 136. **A repair pass that changes a correct figure because it looks like a wrong one is the failure this project has already committed twice; the check is recorded because it was done.**

- **AND THE FALSE CERTIFICATE, WHICH IS THE SAME FAILURE §10b AND §10f RECORD TWICE ALREADY.** `state/batch-0015-summary.md` certified *the longest verbatim run inside any one of the ten chapters is TWENTY-SIX WORDS, in Chapter 0150, and the other nine are between eight and thirteen*. **Re-measured, the batch longest is FIFTY-ONE WORDS, in Chapter 0147**, and eight of the ten chapters run between seventeen and thirty-nine. **The certificate is corrected in that file with the table, the method and the reason.** What the long runs are matters as much as the number: **they are the volume's own motif and ritual sentences, which §10b already recorded as standing, and the day-book re-deriving the day's finding in the same words, which §10c and §10g both ruled is *a defect and not a virtue*.** Almost every chapter of this batch has the second kind. **IT IS NOT REPAIRED HERE, and the reason is stated so the omission is not read as a pass: a repair pass does not reach into a batch the reviewer has just called strong, the runs are the book's ritual rather than a new error, and the rewrite would touch roughly thirty passages across ten chapters and risk the prose the reviewer praised. It is named in `workspace/volume-03/audit/PROMPT.md` Part 2 with the §10g rule — break the run in the day-book so that it re-derives, never by cutting a sentence of prose, and never by touching a motif or ritual sentence.** This is the third recorded instance of a state file certifying a prose measurement that the prose does not support, and the direction is the same every time: **the certificate is written after the sweep is described rather than from the sweep's output.**

- **AND SIX MORE FINDINGS THE REVIEW DID NOT RAISE, ALL OF THEM THE PROJECT'S OWN STANDING META-LEAK RULE, AND ALL SIX REPAIRED.** `state/continuity.md` §10b ruled that a character's own day-book and a store's ledger may say *in this book* because they are the man's book and the district's book and are not the narrator, and that **the narrator may not.** The sweep found six instances in this batch that sit in the **narrator's bolded prose and not in a day-book**: *in this book* at 0141:83, 0145:65 and 0148:31, **now *in my own book***; *in this chapter* at 0145:77, **now *in the hundred and thirty-first***, on the §10b shape; and ***three volumes*** at 0146:75 and 0150:51, **now *in four counties and a city***, which is the exact replacement §10b used for the identical phrase at 0112:147. **The whole of Chapters 101 to 150 is swept and clean of the disallowed forms. The remaining *in this book* instances in the volume are inside a day-book or a ledger and are allowed, and *in this trade* is in the world and is allowed.** This is the **sixth** time this project has found this class, and the direction is the interesting part: **every other error of this kind travels from the prose outward into the state layer, and this one travels inward, from an allowed in-world use in a day-book into the narrator's mouth two chapters later.** It is recorded so the next pass checks for it deliberately.

- **WHAT WAS RE-MEASURED AFTER THIS PASS AND CAME BACK CLEAN:** **no `##` heading in any of the ten chapters**, which is the house style and is the Batch 0004 defect not repeated; **every daytime hour bare and the ninth hour of the evening the only half-day, carried in all ten day-books**; **no weekday and no month named in any of the ten**; **the carrier's interval reads fifty days on Day 127 through fifty-nine on Day 136, one per chapter, counted in days from the seventy-seventh**; **the register reads thirteen in every chapter that states it**, and the seven papers refused or untaken are each refused on the page; **all five clauses stand and the frame has not been in a building since the thirty-third in all ten day-books**; **no age on the page for any of the six new people of this batch**; **no narrator meta-leak in any of Chapters 101 to 150**; **no stray shell text**; **nothing rung by anybody, the carrier unstruck at fifty-nine days, his own bell barred in a sealed yard in Kells, and Tamsin's bell in her bag**; and **the only about nine hundred people left in the batch is the market on the middle terrace, which is a long-established figure and is not a crowd at a bell.**



## 10i. THE VOLUME 03 CLOSE AND AUDIT PASS — every finding, every repair, and the outline disagreements that were corrected in the outline and not in the prose

**This pass ran against `outline/volume-03.md`, the five batch summaries, the state layer, and Chapters 121 to 150 read in full. It changed no event, no motive, no refusal, no document, no custody state, no scene and no day. The batch is not to be restarted, the volume is not to be rewritten, and the audit record is `reviews/volume-03/volume-audit.md`.**

- **THE `##` HEADINGS OF CHAPTERS 0121 TO 0130 ARE GONE — TWENTY-NINE OF THEM, TWO, ONE, THREE, THREE, THREE, FOUR, FOUR, TWO, THREE AND FOUR, WITH THE `---` RULES THAT SERVED THEM.** Volume 01 has none, Volume 02 has four in three chapters, Chapters 0101 to 0120 have none and Chapters 0131 to 0150 have none, so Batch 0003 was the only place in Volume 03 where a chapter read as a beat sheet with prose between the beats. **No sentence of prose was deleted with a heading.** Two headings carried an hour and both were re-entered as ordinary prose where they belonged (0123, the seventh hour at the finding on the third board; 0130, the sixth hour at the market), one heading's duration was re-entered in 0121, one heading's count (*five things in it and four of them are printed* over a line of four) is not on the page anywhere else and was not restored, and the rest were either already in the body or were structural ledes recast as the ordinary bold sentence the volume uses everywhere else. **The four `##` headings in Volume 02 are at 0069, 0070 and 0076 and are named here and left: they are not this volume and a repair pass does not reach backwards into a closed batch.**
- **THE DAY-BOOK RE-DERIVATION DEFECT IN CHAPTERS 141 TO 150 IS REPAIRED AND RE-MEASURED.** §10h measured it at fifty-one words in Chapter 0147 with eight of the ten chapters between seventeen and thirty-nine, and named the rule. **Nine runs were broken, all of them in the day-book, none of them by cutting a sentence of prose, and none of them a motif or ritual sentence.** The measured batch longest is now **THIRTY-NINE WORDS in Chapter 0148, and that one is the fourth clause of the three-person arrangement**, which is standing. The other nine are between fourteen and twenty-seven and every one of them is the object specification, the custody formula, the fifth clause's wording, one of the four sentences of a bell's claim, the seed sentence about a route that answers, her sentence in Chapter 0146, the Office-is-right-about-the-procedure line, and two chapter findings. **The method and the table are in `state/batch-0015-summary.md`, and the certificate that stood there was written from the sweep's description and not from its output, which is the failure this project produces most often.**
- **THREE SMALL PROSE REPAIRS, each one a number that could not be true.** Chapter 0142 said he had stood at that counter *four times in forty days* and the four visits are Days 100, 101, 107 and 128, so it is **twenty-eight days**; the same sentence's *there has not been one in this room for about four days* is false in a scene where a man with an instruction was at the counter that morning, and is now **there is not one of those in this room on most of the days I come up here**. Chapter 0145 said he had *been a maker for thirty-three years* and he is thirty-two, so it is **twenty-three**. **The fifth clause's job-count was seven, then six, then seven across Chapters 0142, 0144 and 0148, and Chapter 0142's two numerals are corrected to six** — see §9.
- **THE CLOCKS OF ALL FOUR VOLUME 03 BATCHES ARE CORRECTED AGAINST THE PAGE.** See §13b. Twenty-two figures in §5a, fourteen in §5b-B, one in §5b-C, six hours that no chapter gives removed, and one parenthetical that still said *about two hundred people* in a yard the Batch 0005 review had already corrected to forty.
- **A STATE FILE CLAIMED A CLEAN SWEEP IT DID NOT HAVE, twice.** `state/current.md` and `state/open-threads.md` both said Batch 0005 uses the words *muster* and *Renewal* nowhere, and Chapter 0145 has *a renewal that has been done* in a district's trench. The claim is corrected in every file that carried it and the chapter is not touched. `state/open-threads.md` also listed the days on which *if there were three of me* is written and omitted three of them; the list is now the page's.
- **`state/character-state.md` carried Batch 0004's figures at the head of the volume close** — forty-nine days on the carrier, three hands on the crown, *if there were three of me* at Chapter 0130, a hundred and sixteen days at the last chapter, three non-uses of the slate — **and carried a whole second set of relationship paragraphs from Batch 0004 below the Batch 0005 set, with a different count of the people who are right in each.** All corrected; the duplicate set is removed, not merged.
- **`logs/batch-0005.review.log` does not exist in this repository.** §10h cites it as the reviewer's own report and the volume-close brief instructs this pass to read it. **The directory `logs/` holds `audit.log` and `models.log` and nothing else, and the reviewer's findings survive only as the §10h summary.** This is recorded as a missing artefact and is not reconstructed.

**NOTHING WAS REPAIRED IN THE PROSE THAT WAS CORRECT AND ONLY RESEMBLED A WRONG FIGURE, and the two figures checked and left alone are named here so that nobody raises them twice: the arrangement dated *the hundred and twentieth* in Chapter 0148 (§10h), and Chapter 0143's *twenty-four days* of writing *if there were three of me*, which is twenty-three days from Chapter 0120's first writing on Day 106 and is defensible on the volume's own inclusive counting, which Chapter 6 establishes.**

## 10j. THE REVIEW OF THE VOLUME 03 AUDIT — eight findings, and every one of them is an editing slip or a record that overstated itself. No prose was rewritten and no plot was touched.

**The audit of §10i was itself reviewed, and the review's report is `logs/audit.review.log`. It raised eight numbered defects and every one of the eight is fixed. Two residuals it noted separately were checked and accepted, and this section is the permanent record. The audit's method was confirmed rather than disturbed: the reviewer re-implemented the duplicate-prose measurement in `state/batch-0015-summary.md` and got the post-repair figure for all ten chapters exactly, `27, 16, 16, 19, 15, 16, 20, 39, 17, 14`, with the longest at thirty-nine in 0148; the heading count reproduces at twenty-nine removed with four left in Volume 02; and the outline disagreements were confirmed as corrected in the outline and not in the prose. The volume is not reopened.**

- **FINDING 1, THE ONE REAL PROSE REGRESSION, AND IT IS TWO MARKERS. Chapter 0149's day-book.** The audit rewrote two day-book sentences and in both rewrites it opened a new `**` span inside a span that was already open, so each line carried three markers where it had carried two. **The second column lost its emphasis onto the wrong sentence and rendered a literal `**`; the third column shifted its emphasis onto *About nine hundred forms a year come off one plate* and away from *The reason is four inches long and it is the hundred and twelfth's habit and not a rule: whenever a building shows me a way of reading a person's own sworn word, I think about hers.*** One `**` is deleted from each line, at `chapter-0149.md:107` before *Two different objects* and at `chapter-0149.md:109` after *hers.*, and the two lines carry two and six markers again, which is what each carried before the audit. **The audit's emphasis decisions are preserved as it made them and no sentence of prose was touched.**

## 10k. VOLUME 04 BATCH 0001 — CHAPTERS 151–160, DAYS 137–146 — the batch record, every fix made before the files were saved, and the sweeps

**This is the writing-time record for the first batch of Volume 04 and it is compressed. The full record, including what each of the ten chapters paid and the whole sweep position, is `state/batch-0016-summary.md`. Nothing in this section is a change of plot and nothing may be re-litigated without the chapter in front of you.**

**THE BATCH PAID EXACTLY ONE PROMISE AND REFUSED TO INFLATE IT. He walked nine miles of road in ten days and did not find a section of road that is missing, because there is not one, and a second man finds the road because he was born at the end of it and has never been asked what it is called. NO ROAD IS FOUND, RESTORED, REGISTERED OR EXPLAINED. THE RECTANGLE IS NOT EXPLAINED. THE PANEL ABOVE THE COACH INN IS NOT RESTORED. NOBODY IS ENTERED ANYWHERE AS A HOUSEHOLD. NOTHING IS RUNG, NOTHING IS BOUND, NO STAGE COMES, NO SENSE COMES, NOBODY IS THANKED, AND NO CROWN OFFICE IS DEFEATED.**

**THE SIX RESERVED MYSTERIES ARE ALL STILL OPEN AND NONE OF THEM IS NAMED IN PROSE IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS. A word-by-word sweep of `chapters/volume-04/` returns nothing for *Dain*, *First Answer*, *OFF. 411*, *Salla Keene*, *Iven Sore*, *Nell Fenn* or *Hob Kersey*, and the only occurrence of the word *Kells* in the batch is the sealed yard in all ten day-books, which is the right one.** **HOW AN OFFICE GETS INTO A BELL WAS NOT ADVANCED AND MAY NOT BE BY A LATER BATCH: a smith named a coupling, a jumper and a bracket and said on the page that he does not know what it does and neither does the man at the counter and there is a book on the counter.** **WHERE A CROWN FITTING CAME FROM IS RESTATED FROM A SMITH'S MOUTH IN A DIFFERENT COUNTY AND NOT ADVANCED: a room, a counter, a book, a man, a shelf, an instruction with a number, a district and a day, and the number is a filing reference and not a man, said for the fifth time in five counties.** **THE NAME-TRANSFER MECHANISM IS UNTOUCHED: no copied signature is explained, no transferred name is explained, and no child is found by a document.** **WHAT LIRA VALE KNEW AND WHICH PARTS OF HER CHOICE WERE COERCED IS UNTOUCHED: the eleven lines are referred to twice in the batch, once in Chapter 0155 and once in Chapter 0160, and in both it is the count and the fact of possession and not her fate, and they are not quoted out, not argued and not explained.** **IVEN SORE'S SERVICE ENTRY IS UNTOUCHED AND HE IS NOT IN THE BATCH.** **THE WORD *CLAPPER* DOES NOT APPEAR IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS, MEASURED, AND NO FITTING IS DESCRIBED AS ANYTHING BUT A COUPLING, A JUMPER AND A BRACKET, AND NO MECHANISM IS OPENED AND NO PERSON IS SHOWN ONE.** **THE SEALED FIRST ANSWER CHAMBER IS NOT APPROACHED, DESCRIBED OR NAMED, AND NO CHAPTER GOES NEAR IT.**

**THE ONE PERMITTED SEED WAS CARRIED ONCE AND IT IS NOT NAMED AND NOT ANSWERED. In Chapter 0160 a man born at the end of the road says, unasked, that his father's line came up through the bottom course of a wall in this village once and stopped, and that his father took the wall out with his own hands and put it back, and that he has never told anybody what he found and is not going to. HE SAYS OUT LOUD THAT A MAN WITH A BARROW HAS NOW TOLD HIM HE IS THE SECOND AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHAT THAT IS. Tomas Vale writes down that it is the second time in fifteen days — the first is Chapter 0145 on Day 131 and Day 146 less Day 131 is fifteen — and refuses to put two men talking about their fathers in a row, and the day-book says so in the third column. NO CHARACTER DRAWS A CONCLUSION, NO DAY-BOOK STATES, PROPOSES OR HINTS AT THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT REVERSAL, AND A WORD-BY-WORD SWEEP OF THE BATCH FOR *displac*, *administrative*, *invisible*, *registering*, *settlements*, *line of villages*, *forced answer* AND *copied answer* RETURNS NOTHING.**

**THE BATCH'S CIVIC LAW, ALL OF IT NEW, AND NONE OF IT A STAGE AND NONE OF IT A POWER:**

- **A schedule is a drawing of a road and a road is a fact about the people who walk it, and this kingdom has one word for both of them and no second word.** A drawing shows where a line runs and cannot show whose hand altered it, and a man who cannot read a run has never been able to and there is no trade in it.
- **There is no form in this kingdom on which a man is taught to read a map, and therefore there is no version of being better at one.** The way forward is four other people's eyes, one at a time, on a sheet in a coat, and **he has no standing to ask any of them for anything.**
- **Being believed by somebody in this kingdom is worth exactly nothing, and this is now said twice in one batch, in a market town and at a bench: no form on which belief is entered, and a person four days' road away cannot enter a paragraph in a column, and neither can a bench.**
- **A bench is not a court and a court is not a bench, and a room where a thing is said out loud and written down is a different object from a room where a thing is said out loud and is not.**
- **A bench of a river canton shares a river, a bridge rate and a foundry with eight others and turns up, and its book takes four kinds of thing at a sitting, and none of the four is a stranger's road.**
- **A thing said at ten feet in the open cannot be produced by anybody and dies with the people who heard it; a thing written down can be produced against you by a stranger you have never met in about forty years; a route is a person on foot and it cannot be edited and it costs a day. Neither of the first two is a route.**
- **A list of objects and a list of people are two different documents, and no form in this kingdom says which is which, and a form has four boxes and the third one is a thing and does not say whether the thing is a thing or a man.**
- **Nobody in this kingdom has ever compared notes with anybody, and this is not a coincidence in the world: it is a fact about having no form on which two people who both keep a book may put one in front of the other.**
- **A column a man rules for himself is a thing he has to be right about, and a man who keeps nine years of his own arithmetic would rather risk being wrong once in a book.**
- **A ford is not a place. It is four or five days a year, and about nine stones in it are the only fixed thing, and the rest of it moves about four feet in a fortnight, and so does the road.**
- **A road has four names on it and all four are correct and none of them is on a sheet, and words travel along a road and a word outlives the thing that made it.**
- **A man with a barrow stops people and a man on foot does not, and a receipt in five clauses is the reason a man with a barrow is not a man on foot.** That is a fact about a clause and not a stage and no chapter may write it as one.
- **A district draws the edge of its own ground and stops, and does not go and ask, and the miles of a road that are not on a sheet are not hidden — they are not anybody's business to draw. A road that is open is a fact about the people who open it; a road that is on a sheet is a fact about a piece of paper; and those are two different objects.**
- **A rectangle is not a hole in a road. It is a hole in a drawing, and the road underneath it is doing perfectly well without him.**
- **A line that has been cut has not been put back and nobody has ever asked what it was for, and when the question is asked in a room of nine people, four of them say in about nine seconds that nothing should, and one of them asks for that to be entered in a book she does not have a copy of, and nobody is going to enter it, and she said so first.**
- **A burr is a thing a tool leaves and a man leaves a mess.**
- **A mend you cannot see is a mend somebody turns up one morning with a paper in their hand, and there is nowhere in this kingdom to stand at a counter and put your own mend on the counter and say so, and a man who does that has put himself inside a column.**

**THE POWER POSITION IS UNCHANGED AND THE BATCH MAY NOT HAVE MANUFACTURED A CHANGE.** Tomas is at the first edge of **Stage 1 (Near Toll)** at the end of this batch exactly as at the end of Volume 03. He attempts no perception in any of the ten chapters, is not described as reaching for one, is not described as listening for one, and all ten day-books say that no perception was attempted and none had. He binds nothing, learns no second sense, does not identify a copied answer, **does not learn where a sound went, is not told and does not try to be told.** Nothing is rung by anybody. **The one thing he acquires is a fact about feet — four days and four miles is a mile a day with a barrow — and it is not a stage and is not written as one.**

**THIRTEEN THINGS WERE DRAFTED WRONG AND FIXED BEFORE THE FILES WERE SAVED, AND ALL THIRTEEN ARE RECORDED HERE BECAUSE A STATE FILE THAT CLAIMS A CLEAN SWEEP IT DID NOT HAVE IS THE FAILURE THIS PROJECT PRODUCES MOST OFTEN:** a weekday (*on a Sunday*, Chapter 0154, twice); an age in a stranger's mouth (*about nine years older than the joke*, Chapter 0152); **three narrator meta-leaks of the class the Volume 03 close called inward leaks, in Chapters 0153, 0154 and 0155**; a fourth meta-leak inside a day-book (*in this chapter*, Chapter 0160); **five odd-marker lines** from nested and interrupted bold spans, in Chapters 0154, 0157 and 0160; **nine day-book re-derivations** re-printing the narrator, including a forty-seven-word one; **a duration of about forty years** in Chapter 0159, which exceeds the only age on the page; an age in Chapter 0156; **three non-uses of the slate written down in three day-books**, which would have staged a fifth non-use; **two wrong intervals in Chapter 0160**, ten days where the day map gives fifteen; and **four name collisions with the existing manuscript** — *Ferrow*, *Trench*, *Callow* and the rejected *Mab Trench* — all caught by a name sweep before any state file was written; and **four odd-marker lines in the state layer itself**, the same defect as the five in the prose, in `state/current.md`, `state/continuity.md` and `state/character-state.md`, **all repaired, and the per-line count against the pre-batch commit is now zero introduced on every state file this batch touched, with nine pre-existing multi-line spans left exactly as they were.** **A FILE WAS ALSO CORRUPTED BY A BAD SCRIPTED EDIT AND A WHOLE CHAPTER WAS REWRITTEN RATHER THAN PATCHED, and the corruption was silent and the only symptom was a word count of 5,180 where 3,192 was intended.** The full list with the exact wrong wording and the exact replacement is in `state/batch-0016-summary.md`.

The duplicate-prose run inside each chapter, measured with the §10c method: 13, 13, 10, 18, 20, 24, 18, 39, 13, 17, and the batch longest is THIRTY-NINE IN CHAPTER 0158 AND IT IS THE FOURTH CLAUSE OF THE THREE-PERSON ARRANGEMENT, which is standing and is carried in that chapter's narrator and in its own day-book because the rule the day-book states IS the fourth clause. This independently reproduces the Volume 03 close figure of thirty-nine for the same standing sentence, and the project now has two batches whose measured longest is that clause.** The *about nine* figure is **SEVENTY-SEVEN ACROSS 30,849 WORDS, WHICH IS 0.25 PER HUNDRED WORDS**, against 0.28 in Volume 03's closing batch, 0.35 in the one before and 0.29 in the one before that, **and the batch does not claim a reduction, because 0.03 against a batch is a measurement and not an improvement.** The carrier's interval is **sixty on Day 137 through sixty-nine on Day 146, one figure per chapter, counted in days from the seventy-seventh, and every one of the ten verified on the page.** **The register reads thirteen in all ten day-books and *fourteen* appears ten times and every one of them is *not one of them is a fourteenth*.** **The slate is named nowhere in Volume 04 except in Tamsin Rook's own day-book in Chapter 0156, where she writes that its price side is the other way up, and the four standing non-uses at Chapters 0127, 0130, 0134 and 0143 are UNCHANGED and no fifth is staged.** **No `##` section heading in any of the ten chapters. No age for anybody, and the eleven who entered with a role all enter with none: Brida Tolliver, Row Kellow, Tib Neave, Pell Okell, Mab Sarter, Bel Tremayne, Aman Petch, Nieve Ossick, Corin Nook, Ottilie Brane and Wend Dreeve.** **No stray shell text in any file this batch wrote.**

- **MINOR 2, AN EQUATION IN §5f THAT CANNOT HOLD.** The line read **TEN DAYS is nine miles, a mile a day**, and nine miles in ten days is not a mile a day. **THE TWO FIGURES ARE NOW KEPT AS TWO FIGURES AND THE PROHIBITION IS STATED: they are not a rate and may not be multiplied, and a later writer who divides one by the other and carries the answer into a card is doing arithmetic the book does not do.** The page's own rate is given as what it is — *Four days. Four miles. A mile a day with a barrow*, Chapter 0155, on the first four days and no further — and Chapter 0156's nine-hours-a-day figure is marked as a request and not a measurement, and Chapter 0160's whole road is kept at nine miles in ten days. **NO CHAPTER OF THE BATCH NAMES WHICH OF THE TEN DAYS DID NOT MAKE A FULL MILE AND A LATER BATCH MUST NOT INVENT ONE.** The same loose figure was corrected in the next-phase prompt, where *a road is nine miles long and a mile a day is what it costs with a barrow* bound the rate to nothing.

**TWO THINGS THIS PASS FOUND THAT THE REVIEW DID NOT RAISE, BOTH BECAUSE IT ASKED FOR ONE FILE AND NOT THE WHOLE STATE LAYER, AND BOTH OF THE SAME FAMILY AS A FINDING ABOVE:**

- **A VERBATIM DUPLICATED LINE IN `state/continuity.md` ITSELF, AT THE TAIL OF §9.** The bullet *Four rows of figures that look alike and are not: the **fifty-three** wards marked on the Measure's road map; the **forty-one** L-4 schedule lines; the **four hundred and eleven** of the Low Ward; and the **four** columns. None of them is a fifth.* stood **twice, complete, two hundred and fifty-three characters each**, eleven lines apart, the second as a stray at the end of the section. **The stray second copy is deleted and the first is left exactly as it was**, because the figures it holds are Volume 03's and are correct and are not this pass's to change. **IT IS THE SAME SILENT-WRITE DEFECT AS FINDING 1 AND AS THE BATCH'S OWN ITEM 13, IN A FILE NOBODY HAD SCANNED FOR IT, AND IT IS THE PROOF THAT THE REPEAT SCAN BELONGS ON EVERY STATE FILE EVERY PASS AND NOT ONLY ON THE ONE BEING COMPLAINED ABOUT.**
- **FIVE MORE STALE CHAPTER RANGES IN `state/open-threads.md`, OF THE SAME FAMILY AS THE THREE THE REVIEWER NAMED.** The file's header says *after Chapter 160* and five claims still stopped at Chapter 150. Each was swept before it was rewritten and each sweep is recorded on the face of the claim: *Chapters 141 to 150* for the word *clapper*, extended to 141 to 160 with **a file-by-file sweep of `chapters/volume-04/` returning zero, so the word named once in Chapter 0125 has not been named a second time**; *not in any chapter from 101 to 150* for Iven Sore, extended to 101 to 160 with the Volume 04 sweep returning zero; *any of the one hundred and fifty chapters* for Tamsin Rook's hearing, corrected to **one hundred and sixty** with the Volume 04 ten swept and nobody asking her; and *Chapters 141 to 150* for what became of Lira Vale, for the ten people in Kells, and for the arrest of Hob Kersey, each extended to 141 to 160 with its sweep recorded — *lira* and *sister*, all ten Kells names, and *kersey* — **and the *muster* and *Renewal* claim extended from Chapters 121 to 150 to Chapters 121 to 160 with the Volume 04 sweep for both returning nothing.** The one letter from Kells is restated as Volume 03's closing batch's letter and **the word *letter* is now recorded as not appearing at all in Chapters 151 to 160.**

**THE CHECK THAT WAS RUN ON EVERY FILE THIS PASS TOUCHED, AND IT IS TWO CHECKS AND NOT ONE. The per-line bold-marker count must be unchanged from the pre-pass commit, because a bold span may legitimately cross a line and a file-level count cannot see that: `state/current.md` 0 and 0, `state/continuity.md` 7 and 7, `state/character-state.md` 1 and 1, `state/open-threads.md` 0 and 0, and the next-phase prompt 0 and 0 — zero introduced anywhere, and the pre-existing multi-line spans are left exactly as they were. And a whole-file repeated-run scan, which finding 1 shows the marker test cannot replace: `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md`, the next-phase prompt and `state/batch-0016-summary.md` are all clear of duplicated long lines, and the remaining repeated runs are standing clauses that these files mean more than once.**

**AND THIS SECTION BROKE ITS OWN CHECK TWICE WHILE BEING WRITTEN, WHICH IS RECORDED BECAUSE A RECORD THAT CERTIFIES A SWEEP IT DID NOT HAVE IS THE FAILURE §10k NAMES AND THIS IS THE THIRD TIME IN FOUR PASSES.** **THE DRAFT OF THIS SECTION CARRIED THE LITERAL TWO ASTERISKS INSIDE BACKTICKS TWICE, in finding 1 and in the paragraph above, and a backticked pair is one unmatched marker to a per-line count, so `state/continuity.md` went from seven odd lines to nine and the certificate in this very paragraph was false as it stood when it was written.** **Both are reworded to say *bold-marker count* in words, the count is back to seven against the pre-pass commit's seven, and the paragraph above is true now and was not true five minutes before it was read.** The lesson is §10j finding 1 exactly and it needs no restating: **a check that is written in the same file as the thing it checks will be written by the hand that is being checked, and the only defence is to run the check on the file after the sentence claiming it is in place.**

**AND THE SAME TWO TRAPS WERE ALSO STANDING IN THE OTHER FILES THAT CARRY THE SENTENCE, AND THE LINE DRAWN IS RECORDED RATHER THAN LEFT SILENT.** Finding 8's bell sentence and finding 6's volume-or-series permission are each in more than one file. **THE LIVE FILES ARE CORRECTED IN BOTH CASES: `state/current.md`, which is the file a next writer reads first and which was internally inconsistent because the wheelwright is in its own Chapter 0153, and §13c below, which is titled *What Volume 04 opens on* and is therefore live while §13a and §13b, which are scoped to the Volume 03 close and say so in their own headings, are left at one hundred and fifty because that was true inside their declared scope.** **THE HISTORICAL FILES ARE LEFT AS THE RECORD AND ARE NOT REWRITTEN: `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` is a completed phase's prompt and Chapters 151 to 160 were written against its wording, and `state/batch-0016-summary.md` is the writing-time record of that batch, and altering either would make the record of what was certified untrue. A LATER PASS THAT FINDS ONE OF THESE SENTENCES IN A COMPLETED PHASE'S PROMPT IS READING A RECORD AND NOT AN INSTRUCTION.**

**THIS PASS CHANGED NO EVENT, NO MOTIVE, NO REFUSAL, NO DOCUMENT, NO CUSTODY STATE, NO HOUR, NO DAY, NO FIGURE OF THE PLOT AND NO CHAPTER TITLE. IT EDITED NO CHAPTER FILE AT ALL — `git diff --name-only` over `chapters/` returns empty — IT DID NOT RESTART THE BATCH, IT DID NOT REOPEN ANY FINDING §10i, §10j OR §10k SETTLED, IT CHANGED NO OUTLINE, AND IT EDITED NO CONTROLLER FILE. THE PLANNED PLOT IS UNTOUCHED AND THE ONE NEXT-PHASE PROMPT IS STILL THE ONLY PHASE FILE, AND IT IS STILL `workspace/volume-04/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, CHAPTERS 161–170, DAYS 147–156.**


## 10m. VOLUME 04 BATCH 0002 — CHAPTERS 161–170, DAYS 147–156 — the batch record, every fix made before the files were saved, and the sweeps

**The ten cards for this batch were written by the batch itself and there is no `outline/batches/volume-04-batch-0002.md`, and none may be created. The cards are set out in full in `state/batch-0017-summary.md` and were checked against `outline/volume-04.md` before a word of prose was drafted.**

**WHAT THE BATCH PAID, IN THE ORDER THE OUTLINE NAMES IT.** A district's bypass is cut and a district's line is worked, and **the households above the cut are the ones that lose a voice: a bypass takes the traffic and it takes the man who used to come past twice a year, because he is paid for the road with a name on it and not for the road underneath the bank, and a man does not mend a road he cannot write down (Chapter 0163).** A man who has mended his own work once and will not do it again, **and the reason is on the page and it is not about time: a mend nobody can see is a mend somebody turns up one morning with a paper in their hand, and there is no counter in this kingdom where a man may say *I have mended my own and here it is*, so he does it twice, once properly and once with a lip you could catch a heel on (Chapter 0166).** A district that is right, **and the standing is a thing a man with a barrow has not got: the form wants a ward, a day, a number and a ground, and a reason box that a man with no standing cannot reach (Chapter 0164).** The first place that is on nobody's map and is plainly there, **and the discovery that a place can be absent from a schedule and be standing here with a fire going in it, and can be on a schedule under a name nobody at a door uses, and both are true of the same forty houses, and HE CANNOT PROVE WHICH IS WHICH BECAUSE HE CANNOT READ THE SCHEDULE (Chapter 0169).** And the group is now four and has agreed about exactly one thing, **and the one thing is a bridge, and it is the only thing on that stretch a man can check himself, which is why it is the only thing they agree about and also the least useful thing they could have agreed about (Chapter 0165, and Chapter 0167 for the question that follows it).**

**THE OUTLINE DISAGREED WITH THE PROMPT AND THE PROMPT GOVERNS, AND THE DISAGREEMENT WAS IN THE OUTLINE, NOT IN THE PROSE.** `outline/volume-04.md` line 86 reads *A man who repairs his own wire and owns nine days for it*, and **nine days, a lighter and a cart belong to Nevin Holt at a wharf in Chapter 0113 on Day 99, a different man in a different trade in a different chapter, and joining the two is a man arranging a thing, which is the one thing this book does not do. Chapter 0166 is written to the outline's shape and to none of its arithmetic, and the road-mender in it has no figure of nine days, no lighter and no cart anywhere in his mouth.** The outline is NOT corrected here, because the batch is not a volume close and the correction belongs to the audit that has not run.

**THE SWEEPS, MEASURED AND NOT CLAIMED.** No weekday and no month is named in any of the ten chapters and **NONE WAS DRAFTED INTO ONE AND REMOVED, which is a measured zero and is the first Volume 04 batch in which that is true.** Every day-time hour in the ten chapters is bare and the ninth hour of the evening is the only half-day, carried in all ten, and there is **no eighth hour anywhere in the batch**, and Chapter 0165 is the one chapter with no seventh hour. No `##` heading in any of the ten. **The word *clapper* does not appear in Chapters 151 to 170, measured.** No boundary name is on the page: *Dain*, *First Answer*, *OFF. 411*, *Salla Keene*, *Iven Sore*, *Nell Fenn*, *Hob Kersey*, *Mara Vale* and *Oren Pike* all return nothing, **and the only occurrence of *Kells* is the sealed yard, ONCE IN EACH OF THE TEN CHAPTERS AND IN EVERY CASE IN THAT CHAPTER'S OWN DAY-BOOK. THIS PARAGRAPH SAID *TWICE IN EACH DAY-BOOK* AND THE TRUE COUNT IS ONCE, and the correction is §10n finding 4.** The seed-word sweep of `chapters/volume-04/` for *displac*, *administrative*, *invisible*, *registering*, *settlements*, *line of villages*, *forced answer* and *copied answer* returns nothing. No age for anybody. No bell rung or struck by anybody.

**FIVE THINGS WERE DRAFTED WRONG AND REPAIRED BEFORE THE STATE LAYER WAS WRITTEN, and all five are in `state/batch-0017-summary.md`: three narrator instances of *in this book* (Chapters 0161, 0163, 0166); one silent corruption of a district's road surveyer's dialogue in Chapter 0164 by a scripted replacement that matched an identical sentence in a day-book two hundred lines earlier; twenty-one day-book re-derivations of the narrator's own sentences, the longest seventy words in Chapter 0170; a geography error in Chapter 0161 in which he turned round and walked back over ground he had already walked, which was removed from the prose before any state file existed; and a figure of nine days given to the road-mender, which a prompt bars from him, and which now reads *since the ford*.** THE MEASURED INTRA-FILE DUPLICATE-PROSE BATCH LONGEST IS TWENTY-SIX WORDS, IN CHAPTER 0168, AND IT IS THE BATCH'S OWN STANDING CUSTODY SENTENCE carried word for word in the opening note and in the day-book of the same file, and no other chapter is above twenty-four. **THE FIGURE IS AN INTRA-FILE FIGURE AND IT IS NOT THE BATCH'S LARGEST REPEATED TEXT: the same measurement run across pairs of files returns a maximum of one hundred and twenty-two words and a minimum of one hundred and twenty-one across all forty-five pairs, and inside it a ninety-three-word custody core is byte-identical in all ten chapters. That block is the standing custody formula, it is exempt on the rule this project already carries, it is named and measured at §10n finding 1, and it was named nowhere when this section was written. This section's claim was true as an intra-file figure and it was a disclosure gap as a statement about the batch, and both halves of that are the finding.** The per-line bold-marker parity count is zero introduced on every state file this batch touched and the seven odd-marker lines in this file are pre-existing multi-line spans that were already there at the previous commit and were left exactly as they were.

## 10n. VOLUME 04 BATCH 0002 — THE POST-BATCH REVIEW PASS, every finding, and every fix. TWO chapter files were edited, in three places between them, and the batch was not restarted.

**The reviewer read the ten finished chapters, the state layer and the next-phase prompt and returned TEN findings. Eight are fixed and two are fixed as measurements and binding instructions rather than as rewrites, and this pass found FOUR MORE on its own. THE BATCH IS NOT TO BE RESTARTED. THIS SECTION IS THE PERMANENT RECORD AND IT IS THE ONLY COMMITTED COPY: the reviewer's own report is `logs/batch-0002.review.log`, and `.gitignore` carries `logs/` and `*.log`, so a log written during a run can never be committed and never was, and the findings survive here and in `reviews/volume-04/batch-0002.md` and nowhere else — which is now the third file in this project to be cited as missing for that reason and not for any other.**

**WHAT THE REVIEW CONFIRMED AND THIS PASS DID NOT RE-SWEEP, AND A LATER WRITER MAY RELY ON: there is no `##` heading in any of the ten chapters; the day map is exact, Chapter 0161 on Day 147 through Chapter 0170 on Day 156, one chapter to a day; the strike counter is exact, ten distinct values from seventy to seventy-nine days from the seventy-seventh, one per chapter, and no chapter says the carrier was rung twice; `ninth hour of the evening` is the only half-day, is carried in all ten, there is no eighth hour anywhere, and Chapter 0165 has no seventh hour; the boundary sweep returns nothing and the seed sweep returns nothing and every hit on the loose month pattern is the modal *may*; `clapper`, `Lira`, `sister`, `Kersey`, `Mara`, `Oren Pike`, `letter`, `slate` and `Vale Bellhouse` all return zero across the ten; `stage` appears only as the banned-sense negation or as the `landing stage` noun in Chapter 0167; there is no stray shell text; the per-line bold-marker parity count is zero introduced; and *nine days* is genuinely gone from Chapter 0166, where it reads *since the ford*.**

- **FINDING 1, THE BATCH'S OWN DUPLICATE-PROSE METHOD IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO THE BATCH'S LARGEST REPEATED TEXT, AND THE TEXT WAS NAMED NOWHERE.** The summary defined the measurement as a longest-common-run over every ordered pair of paragraphs **in the same file.** A paragraph that appears once in each file has no same-file partner, so the largest text the batch carries is exactly the text the metric cannot see. **Run across pairs of files, the word-level longest-common-run is a maximum of ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-TWO WORDS and a minimum of one hundred and twenty-one across all forty-five pairs, and inside it a NINETY-THREE-WORD CUSTODY CORE — *All five clauses stand. …and the warrant stands.* — IS BYTE-IDENTICAL IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS**, the only variation anywhere in the block being the day number, the strike count and the four nouns each chapter gives for the ground he slept on. **IT IS EXEMPT AND IT IS EXEMPT ON THE RULE THIS PROJECT ALREADY CARRIES, which lists the custody formula and the fifth clause's wording as sentences that may be carried forward word for word; re-deriving a receipt's own wording in a man's own book would make the receipt a different receipt on ten different days, and it is not re-derived.** What was wrong was not the sentence and it was not the exemption: **it was that the summary named exactly one exempt carried sentence at twenty-six words and §10m said *MEASURED DUPLICATE-PROSE BATCH LONGEST IS TWENTY-SIX WORDS* with nothing to say that the figure was an intra-file figure, so a later pass reading the state layer would have believed the batch's largest repeated text was twenty-six words when it is one hundred and twenty-two.** **THE METHOD NOW RUNS THREE TESTS WHERE IT RAN ONE, and the third is the cross-file pair: the same state files now carry a three-row table naming the intra-file figure, the line-level figure and the cross-file figure separately, and the one-line honest statement of this batch's repetition is that the largest text carried twice in one chapter is twenty-six words and is a standing sentence, and the largest text carried in all ten chapters is the one-hundred-and-twenty-two-word custody block and is also a standing sentence.** Recorded because §10l finding 1 is the same blindness in a state file and this is the second time the project's certifying metric has been unable to see the thing it was certifying.
- **FINDING 2, CHAPTER 0170'S ONE DECLARATION TO THE GROUP WAS RE-PRINTED IN ITS OWN DAY-BOOK, AT TWENTY-FIVE WORDS, AND THE REPAIR LIST COULD NOT HOLD IT.** The speech he makes on the flat at the fifth hour closed *if you walk it, that is yours and I will not say thank you for it, and if you go home, that is yours and I will not ask you why*, and the second column of the day-book set down both halves word for word. **It went unrepaired in the writing-time pass for one reason and one reason only: twenty-five is under a threshold of twenty-six, and the threshold is what the list was.** **RE-DERIVED, and the day-book now reaches the fact as a ruling rather than a quotation — the two halves given one under the other and not joined, and written that way because they are not one thing and he will not have them read as one thing.** The speech in the prose is untouched. **THE THRESHOLD IS CORRECTED ON THE FACE OF THE RECORD: a run goes on the repair list if it is at or over the standing figure OR if it re-prints the beat the chapter is built on, at any length, and a central beat is not measured by its length.**
- **FINDING 3, EVERY CHAPTER STATES ITS FINDING THREE TIMES, AND THE FIGURES BEHIND IT ARE WORSE THAN THE FINDING.** Prose finding, third column of the day's own book, and a restatement in that same book's closing paragraph, in nine or ten of the ten chapters. **The day-book is 12,599 words of the batch's 26,948, which is FORTY-SEVEN PER CENT, and seventy-one per cent of the batch's words sit inside bold markers.** The sentence-level re-derivation rule is satisfied — **zero verbatim sentence re-prints across three hundred and twenty-one day-book sentences** — and that is exactly the shape of the problem: the rule is satisfied at the level of the sentence and the repetition happens at the level of the chapter. **THIS IS NOT RETRO-FITTED AND THE SECTION SAYS SO RATHER THAN LETTING A FIX IMPLY A FIXED CHAPTER.** Two of the three statements are re-derived where they were actual re-prints — the 0170 pair above and the 0168 pair at finding 4 — **and the third, the chapter-level restatement, is left in all ten chapters, because removing it is a rewrite of ten finished chapters and that is the restart this pass was told not to do.** It is turned into a binding instruction on the next batch instead: **the third column of a day-book is a RULING ON THE DAY and may not be a paraphrase of the prose's finding in the man's own words, and a batch states its figure the required number of times and not once more.** The prompt also carries the day-book share as a figure to be brought down and stated.
- **FINDING 4, A FALSE COUNT IN THIS FILE AND IN THE BATCH SUMMARY, IN THE PRECISE FAILURE CLASS THIS PROJECT PRODUCES MOST OFTEN.** §10m asserted **the only occurrence of *Kells* is the sealed yard, twice in each day-book**, and `state/batch-0017-summary.md` asserted the same. **Measured: EXACTLY ONCE in each of the ten chapters, and in every case inside that chapter's own day-book.** **BOTH ARE CORRECTED AND BOTH NOW SAY ON THEIR FACE THAT THE EARLIER FIGURE WAS TWICE AND WAS WRONG.** Nothing else depended on the count — the sealed yard is the right place and the bar is on the bell and not on the licence and the bell does not travel — **but a state file that doubles a count is a state file a later writer will quote, and this one would have been quoted as a measurement.**
- **FINDING 5, THE *about nine* FIGURE IS ONE MEMBER OF A FAMILY AND THE BATCH TRACKED THAT ONE MEMBER.** Forty across 26,948 words, 0.15 per hundred, against 0.25 in the previous batch — **and the batch was honest that a figure is a measurement and not an improvement, which was the right instinct applied to too narrow a measurement.** The family across the ten chapters, counted after this pass's re-derivations: *about the* 71, *about a* 52, **about nine* 40, *about forty* 18, *about four* 18, *about it* 18, *about two* 15**, *about sixty* 5 — **two hundred and thirty-seven in those eight and three hundred and six instances of the word before anything in the ten chapters.** **A decline in one member of a family is not a decline in the tic; it is a change of which member the batch happened to be counting, and *about forty* and *about two* are the same tic wearing different numbers and were never swept.** The summary now carries the family and the rule that **a later pass quoting the *about nine* figure without the family is quoting half a measurement.** The load-bearing hedges are untouched and no chapter adds one to another or writes a fifth.
- **FINDING 6, ONE VOICE FOR EVERY CHARACTER, AND THE ONE CHARACTER WITH AN ESTABLISHED INTERIORITY OFF THE PAGE IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS.** A carter, a girl counting fences, a woman with a shovel and a district's road surveyor all speak in the same aphoristic sub-clause-heavy register as Tomas, **and the effect the reviewer names is the one AGENTS.md's *show information through character action, conversation, work and consequences* exists to prevent: characters explaining the theme to each other instead of acting.** **PART OF THIS IS A CONSEQUENCE OF A RULE AND IS NOT A DEFECT, AND IT IS RECORDED AS SUCH RATHER THAN FIXED: the fourth clause of the arrangement made out loud in a room he paid for on Day 120 bars him from asking her for a reason at any hour, and that bar is why Tamsin Rook has no dialogue in the batch, and no chapter may lift it to give her a line.** **THE PART THAT IS A DEFECT IS THE OTHER ONE: a man and a woman standing nine feet apart for ten days is a scene, and the batch wrote her one entry in her own hand and no scene, and the next prompt now requires her to be on the page in at least one of Batch 0003's ten chapters in her own presence and not in a clause of a custody recital.** A character's speech is in his trade's grammar and not in the narrator's: a carter counts loads, a woman with a shovel names a surface, a clerk names a form and a procedure, and **the theme is not explained aloud by a second mouth.**
- **FINDING 7, THE TEN TITLES RUN FROM FIFTEEN TO THIRTY-THREE WORDS AND ONE OF THEM STATES AN OUTCOME.** Chapter 0167's is thirty-three words and ends in a clause about what a man *may not put in a column*, which is a finding and not a title. **THE TITLES ARE NOT CHANGED, AND THE REASON IS RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS NOT THE REVIEWER'S REASON AND IT IS THE RIGHT ONE: the ten titles are cited 10 to 28 times each across `state/batch-0017-summary.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/character-state.md` and `state/current.md`, and §10l already set the precedent that a completed batch's titles are the record. Retitling ten chapters would put a hundred and seventy citations out of date to improve a heading.** The fix is forward: **the next prompt caps a title at about twenty words and bars a title that states the finding, the price or the outcome of the chapter it sits over.**
- **FINDING 8, THE NEXT PROMPT'S READ LIST IS ROUGHLY A HUNDRED AND TWENTY-SEVEN THOUSAND WORDS BEFORE THE BIBLE AND THE OUTLINES.** The continuity sections alone are 18,147 words in a file of 80,400 that grows about seventy-five lines a batch, and the prompt then restates some two thousand six hundred words of Day 156 object-and-person state that is already in `state/current.md` and in §10m, so the same custody facts exist in three places and each needs maintaining. **THE READ LIST IS TRIMMED BY EXACTLY ONE SECTION AND THE TRIM IS REPORTED HONESTLY RATHER THAN AS A SAVING, because a state file claiming a reduction it did not achieve is the failure this project produces most often: §5a, the Volume 03 closing batch's clock, is dropped, because §13b consolidates the clock for Days 87 to 136 and §5g and §5f carry the live clocks, and a batch about Days 157 to 166 has no use for a chapter-by-chapter clock for Days 127 to 136. That is 2,656 words out. §10n went in at 3,815 and §10m grew by 155, so the list is 19,461 words where it was 18,147, and the removal puts back less than the addition took.** What the pass did do is put a number on a file that has been growing unmeasured: **`state/continuity.md` was 80,400 words at the pre-pass commit and is 84,370 now, and `state/batch-0017-summary.md` grew by 1,978, so this project's state layer is the growth problem and a later pass has to answer it with something other than a trim.** The prompt now says that `state/continuity.md` is eighty thousand words and is not to be read whole and names the twelve sections it means, and **the duplicated custody block in the prompt is now labelled a carry-forward copy and not the authority, with `state/current.md` named as the live reading, so that a later writer extends one of the two and not both.** **The prompt is not shortened below what a batch needs to inherit a volume's state, because a thinner prompt is how a batch loses custody of a bell, and the finding is recorded as a growth rate and not as a licence to drop the state.**
- **FINDING 9, THE PROMPT PRE-STATED THE REVERSAL IN FULL, INCLUDING ITS MECHANISM, AND TOLD THE BATCH TO PLAN ITSELF.** Line 26 gave the midpoint reversal in about two hundred and thirty words with its shape, its reason and the keeper clause written out, **which removes the discovery from the writer and turns Chapter 0176 into the execution of a specification.** **THE MECHANISM IS OUT OF THE PROMPT AND THE FENCES STAY IN IT.** What remains is the position, the shape `outline/volume-04.md` gives it, the instruction to read that paragraph twice, and the eight constraints that are fences and not plot — not a forgotten town, not a burned village, nobody wicked, no new enemy, no door, the First Answer chamber untouched, the Chapter 0145 seed not re-used to smuggle it in early, and no document produced by him. **THE KEEPER CHAIN IS NOT DELETED FROM THE BOOK; IT IS DELETED FROM THE PROMPT, and the reason it has to arrive on the page is that a reversal whose reason is in the brief is a reversal the reader has already read.** The prompt now says that the reason must arrive **in a clerk's hand or a surveyor's hand or a district man's mouth and not in a narrator's summary**, which is the form the outline already asks for. Separately, **the instruction that the batch writes its own ten cards is KEPT, because that is this project's settled practice and §10m records the same batch doing it, and what is added is that the cards are checked against the outline and that the reversal's card fixes its position and its fences and not its mechanism.**
- **FINDING 10, `state/phase-ledger.json` IS STALE AND IS NOT THIS PASS'S TO FIX.** It reads `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap`, `status: planned`, `attempts: 0`, after seventeen completed batches. **AGENTS.md tells the writer to update it and the operating instructions make the ledger controller-owned, and the ledger has not advanced in seventeen batches, and this pass DID NOT EDIT IT AND MUST NOT.** It is recorded here as an observed gap in the controller layer so that it is not rediscovered as a mystery later, and the correct place for it is the phase system, not a fiction state file.

**FOUR MORE THIS PASS FOUND THAT THE REVIEW DID NOT RAISE, AND THE FIRST OF THEM IS THE MOST SERIOUS DEFECT IN THE BATCH:**

- **A DAY-BOOK BLOCK PRINTED TWICE IN CHAPTER 0168, ONE HUNDRED AND TEN WORDS IDENTICAL, ON ADJACENT LINES INSIDE ONE ENTRY.** The third column appeared twice. The two copies were not identical — the second carried one extra clause about the bank at the seventh hour and used a different pronoun for the rule — **and that is the only reason it survived, and the reason is structural: a duplicated block that came out byte-identical would have been caught instantly by the per-line bold-marker parity test, because a duplicated bold span is an odd marker count, and a copy that differs by one clause is even on every line and passes every test this project had.** **The paragraph-pair method is blind to it by construction, because a blockquote entry is one paragraph and both copies were inside it.** **THE REDUNDANT COPY IS DELETED AND THE SUPERSET IS KEPT WHOLE, so nothing is lost, and the day-book now has one third column and its column sequence is intact.** **THIS IS THE THIRD DUPLICATED-BLOCK DEFECT IN THIS PROJECT AND THE THIRD THAT NO STANDING TEST COULD SEE — §10l finding 1 was a hundred and ten duplicated words in `state/current.md` and this is one hundred and ten duplicated words in a chapter — and THE LINE-LEVEL NEAR-DUPLICATE SCAN IS NOW A STANDING TEST ON EVERY CHAPTER AND EVERY STATE FILE, AT TWENTY-FIVE WORDS A LINE AND A TWENTY-WORD SHARED RUN, and it is the test that finds this and the paragraph-pair test never will.** A whole-file scan of all twenty Volume 04 chapters and every state file for a twenty-five-word line-level repeat returns **Chapter 0168 and nothing else in the prose**, and Chapter 0158's thirty-nine words, which is the standing fourth clause already audited at §10h, §10i and §10l.
- **A SECOND TWENTY-SIX-WORD RUN IN CHAPTER 0168 THAT THE FILE'S OWN HEADLINE FIGURE COVERED.** The day-book's third column opened by re-printing the prose's inventory of the ground. **The file's stated maximum was already twenty-six, so a second twenty-six was on no list, and the list is built from maxima.** It is re-derived as a count and a judgement rather than a description. **THE GENERAL LESSON IS FINDING 2'S THRESHOLD AGAIN FROM THE OTHER SIDE: a headline figure conceals every run that ties it, and a pass that reports one number per file has not reported the file.**
- **A FIFTH WORD IN THE *about* FAMILY IS UNMEASURED ANYWHERE** and is now named at finding 5.
- **THE PROMPT'S OWN HOUSE-RULE PARAGRAPH RESTATED THE DAY-BOOK THRESHOLD AS THE BATCH'S FIGURE**, that the measured batch longest for Chapters 161 to 170 is twenty-six in Chapter 0168 and that nothing else is above twenty-four, **which was the intra-file figure presented to a next writer as the batch's figure.** It now carries all three figures and the cross-file one, and names the ninety-three-word custody core as a standing sentence that may be carried word for word, so that the next batch inherits the exemption with the measurement attached instead of the measurement alone.

**THE CHECK THAT WAS RUN ON EVERY FILE THIS PASS TOUCHED, AND IT IS THREE CHECKS AND NOT ONE. One: the per-line bold-marker count must be unchanged from the pre-pass commit, because a bold span may legitimately cross a line and a file-level count cannot see that. `state/current.md` 0, `state/continuity.md` 7, `state/character-state.md` 1, `state/open-threads.md` 0, `state/volume-index.md` 1, `state/batch-0017-summary.md` 0, the next-phase prompt 0, the two edited chapters 0 each, and the new review file 0 — ZERO INTRODUCED ANYWHERE.** **AND THE TEST CAUGHT THREE OF THIS PASS'S OWN LINES ACROSS TWO FILES, WHICH IS THE THIRD TIME IN THIS PROJECT THAT IT HAS AND IT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY FIXED. A line in §10m above was carrying a backticked pair of bold markers in the middle of a bold span, which is one unmatched marker to a per-line count and took this file from seven odd lines to eight. A line of the new review record quoted the same markers inside a sentence and did the same thing in a file that had no odd lines at all.** **And then the third, and the third is the one worth keeping: the sentence in this paragraph that reported the first two introduced a third, because it opened a bold span at the top of the line and then opened a second one inside it without closing the first, and a nested span is five markers on one line where a flat one is four.** All three were reworded, this file is back to seven odd lines against the pre-pass commit's seven, the new review file is at zero, and **the lesson is §10l's and §10j's exactly and it has now been demonstrated rather than asserted: a check written in the same file as the thing it checks is written by the hand being checked, the only defence is to run the check after the sentence claiming it is in place, and the sentence claiming it is in place is itself part of what has to be checked.** Two: the whole-file repeated-run scan of §10l finding 1, on the state layer and not only on the prose, and **run before and after, which is what makes it evidence: Chapter 0168's line-level maximum is 110 at the pre-pass commit and 25 now, and every other file in the list is unchanged to the word — `state/continuity.md` 62, `state/batch-0017-summary.md` 24, `state/current.md` 14, `state/volume-index.md` 10, `state/open-threads.md` 24, `state/character-state.md` 42, `state/chapter-summaries.md` 37, the next-phase prompt 15 — and those figures are the standing clauses those files mean more than once.** Three, and it is the new one: the line-level near-duplicate scan described in the fourth bullet above, on every chapter and every state file this pass touched, at twenty-five words a line and a twenty-word shared run, before and after. And the reverse sweep, in the direction the error has gone more than a dozen times: every string removed from the prose by the three repairs was searched for across every state file, every outline, the bible and the next-phase prompt, and each returns only inside its own repair record, with one correct exception — `state/batch-0017-summary.md`'s Chapter 0170 card quotes the speech at the fifth hour, which was not changed. No exact duplicated line of twelve words or more exists in any file this pass wrote.

**THIS PASS CHANGED NO EVENT, NO MOTIVE, NO REFUSAL, NO DOCUMENT, NO CUSTODY STATE, NO HOUR, NO DAY, NO FIGURE OF THE PLOT AND NO CHAPTER TITLE. IT EDITED TWO CHAPTER FILES IN THREE PLACES AND NOTHING ELSE IN `chapters/`, ALL THREE EDITS ARE DAY-BOOK RE-DERIVATIONS AND ONE IS THE DELETION OF A REDUNDANT DUPLICATE, IT DID NOT RESTART THE BATCH, IT DID NOT REOPEN ANY FINDING §10i, §10j, §10k OR §10l SETTLED, IT CHANGED NO OUTLINE, IT EDITED NO CONTROLLER FILE, AND IT DID NOT CREATE, MOVE OR DELETE ANY PHASE FILE — THE ONE NEXT-PHASE PROMPT IS STILL `workspace/volume-04/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` AND IT WAS REPAIRED IN PLACE, CHAPTERS 171–180, DAYS 157–166.**

The immediate state of every object and person is in `state/current.md` and `state/character-state.md`; the live threads are in `state/open-threads.md`; the per-chapter archive is `state/chapter-summaries.md`, which is not in any batch prompt's read list.
