### 13a. The weekday and month rule, as it now stands across all one hundred and fifty chapters

**NO WEEKDAY IS NAMED IN ANY CHAPTER OF THE NOVEL. NO MONTH IS NAMED IN ANY CHAPTER OF VOLUME 02 OR VOLUME 03.** The four month names in five places are all in Volume 01 — **March** at `ch12:73` and `ch15:87`, **June** at `ch15:89`, **February** at `ch17:49` — and no batch after Volume 01 has added one. Weekdays drafted and removed before the files were saved: Volume 02 Batch 0002 ten, Batch 0003 seven, Batch 0004 zero, Batch 0005 two; Volume 03 Batch 0001 one (the word *Tuesday*, in Tamsin Rook's day-book, Chapter 0106); Volume 03 Batch 0003 one (*Two hundred is a Tuesday in a market of about nine hundred*, Chapter 0130); **Volume 03 Batch 0005 one (*the cart goes on Thursday or a day near it*, Chapter 0150)**. The sweep run at the volume close across `chapters/volume-03/` returns nothing for either, and that is a measured zero and not a certificate.

### 13b. The clock, consolidated for Days 87 to 136

**There is one clock and it is §5. The batch clocks are §5a (Days 127 to 136, the last batch), §5b-C (Days 117 to 126), §5b-B (Days 107 to 116) and §5b (Days 87 to 96), with §5e (Days 97 to 106) and §5c (the Volume 02 close) below them. EVERY DAY-TIME HOUR IN EVERY CHAPTER OF VOLUME 03 IS BARE. THE NINTH HOUR OF THE EVENING IS THE ONLY HALF-DAY CARRIED, it is carried in all fifty chapters, and it is the opening hour of every day-book. THE ONLY TWO OTHER HALF-DAYS IN THE WHOLE VOLUME ARE CLOSED RETROSPECTIVES AND BOTH BELONG TO DAYS THEY NAME: the fifth hour of the morning of the thirty-third (the receipt's signing hour, quoted in Chapters 0108, 0117, 0124 and elsewhere) and the second hour of the morning of Day 103 (the hearing, quoted at 0118). NO STATE FILE MAY ATTACH A HALF-DAY TO AN HOUR THAT NO CHAPTER ATTACHES ONE TO, AND THE VOLUME-CLOSE SWEEP OF `state/` FOUND NONE.**

**THE OCCURRENCE COUNTS IN ALL FOUR VOLUME 03 CLOCKS WERE RE-MEASURED AT THE AUDIT AGAINST THE HOUR PHRASE ON THE PAGE, AND EVERY ONE OF THE FOUR WAS WRONG IN THE SAME WAY: the counts were scene beats, not the hour.** §5a was corrected on twenty-two of its forty-four figures, **three hours it named are not on the page at all** (the fifth hour of Day 134, the fifth hour of Day 135, and the seventh hour of Day 133), **one of its parentheticals still carried *about two hundred people* in the yard after the crowd had been corrected to forty**, and **it named six retrospective hours belonging to other days where the batch carries one.** §5b-B was corrected on fourteen figures and **two of its hours are not on the page** (the fifth hour of Day 108 and the seventh hour of Day 112). §5b-C needed one correction, and nine of its ten day entries were already right. **THE MEASUREMENT IS THE HOUR PHRASE AND NOT THE SCENE, and that is the Batch 0004 rule applied a second time, and a state file that says a count without naming what each occurrence rests on is not a count.**

### 13c. What Volume 04 opens on, in one place

**Days 137 onward. A step by the river about a mile out of Sennet at about the ninth hour of the evening on Day 136, a barrow, on foot, and he is not going back to that city. **VOLUME 04 HAS NOW RUN DAY 137 TO DAY 156, CHAPTERS 161 TO 170, and the position at the close of Day 156 is a bank above a road about seven miles above the yard of the coach inn, with a barrow, four people on the road and a bridge three miles back that nobody is going to be told about in a column; the record of how Batch 0002 went is `state/batch-0017-summary.md` and its batch record is `state/continuity.md` §10m and its clock is §5g.** A warrant that stands and cannot be lifted because there is no form on which a man asks for one to be. Eleven lines in his own hand on a leaf of his own book. A physical route map he cannot read with a rectangle gone out of it in the same place. A woman beside him whose hearing is worse than it was ten days ago and who may ring her bell at most once more in the whole series, on her own terms, and never for him. Three people, one of the three answers, and no paper in it. And two questions: *why a route that answers is a route that does not have to be argued with*, seeded once in Chapter 0145 by a man talking about his father and not concluded and not named; and *what a route map with a section cut out of every public copy can be used for*, which is the same object seen from the other end.**

**THE AUDIT DIRECTORY IS LEFT UNMARKED ON PURPOSE. `workspace/volume-03/audit/` still holds its `PROMPT.md` and it holds no `.done` and no `.blocked` marker, because the marker is the controller's and not this phase's, and this phase is instructed not to create one. The runner selects with `find workspace -name PROMPT.md | sort` and skips any directory holding a marker, and `volume-03/audit` sorts before `volume-04/batch-0001`, so until the controller marks the audit directory the audit phase is the first unmarked prompt in sort order. That is the correct state for a phase that has just run and has not yet been acknowledged, and it is recorded here so that a later phase does not mistake it for a second handoff. `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned, is stale by design, is not read by the runner, and is not described as authoritative in any file written here.**

**The six reserved mysteries are all still open and none of them is named in prose, and so is the seventh, Tamsin Rook's hearing, which is worse at the end of the volume than at the start of it and is not restored, and nobody asks her for a reason at any hour in any of the ONE HUNDRED AND SEVENTY CHAPTERS, including the chapter she loses it in and including him, **and the ten chapters of Volume 04 Batch 0002 are swept and nobody does, and she is not further reduced in them, and her one entry in that batch is in her own hand in Chapter 0169.** Dain is not named in any chapter. The First Answer chamber is not opened, described or approached in any chapter. Salla Keene is not found and no chapter may find her by a document. The Reckoning 619 bench-book and the Vale Bellhouse archive are not restored and no copy of either is invented. No bell he has ever made is unbarred and no Crown office is defeated and no officer is relieved of anything.**

### 14d. What Volume 05 inherits, in one place

**Day 186, at about the ninth hour of the evening, on a flat of beaten earth above the water above a staging, on foot, with a barrow, four bolts made fast, above a town of about six hundred people called Hallowmere whose low quarter of about two hundred and forty people is not on the district's schedule. He is not going back to Sennet and has no standing in any county. The register is thirteen. The frame has not been in a building since the thirty-third and all fifty chapters say so in their own day-books, a hundred and fifty-three days at Day 186. The plain carrier is in the sealed yard at Kells with the bar on the bell and not on the licence and it does not travel. The certified plain carrier is in a sack against the cradle, fourteen pounds two ounces, not rung for a hundred and nine days and with a hand laid flat on its crown four times and no more. A schedule folded in three in the inside pocket of his coat with a rectangle about two hands together in it that four men looked at in a bench room and did not explain. Eleven lines in his own hand on a leaf of his own book. A warrant that stands. A district's own form in a district's own book nine miles away with nine lines in it and no name on it anywhere. A woman on a step with her hand on an oak jamb, her hearing worse than it was at the start of Volume 04 and not restored, who has not rung her bell in this series since Chapter 0106 and may ring it at most once more in the whole series, on her own terms and never for him.**

**AND THE THREE THREADS VOLUME 05 INHERITS, ALL THREE STILL OPEN, IN THE ORDER THEY ARE CARRIED IN `state/open-threads.md`:** *why the old network goes on working a line that has a Crown fitting on it*, at two seeds and no conclusion, in Chapter 0145 by a man talking about his father and in Chapter 0160 by a man born at the end of the road who said on the page that he knows he is the second and is not going to say what that is; *what a route map with a rectangle gone out of it in the same place in every copy can be used for*, narrowed at the volume's close to **a bay nothing was run into**, not explained, and nobody asked the mould maker and there is no form in this kingdom on which a man in that room could have asked him and written down afterwards that he had; and *whether a line can be refused and what a person who holds a line is obliged to do about it*, which has three partial answers that do not agree with one another and no conclusion — a reason off a clerk's mouth, a price off a man with a scraper, and an account off a man coiling a rope — and **he asked the same question at two benches on purpose and carried neither answer to the other man, and that is not a failure of the method, it is the method.**

**THE AUDIT DIRECTORY IS LEFT UNMARKED ON PURPOSE AND `workspace/volume-04/audit/` HOLDS NO `.done` AND NO `.blocked` MARKER, because the marker is the controller's and not this phase's.** The marker on that directory is the controller's to write. `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned, is stale by design, is not read by the runner, and is not described as authoritative in any file written here.

**The six reserved mysteries are all still open and none of them is named in prose, and so is the seventh, Tamsin Rook's hearing. Dain is not named in any chapter of Volume 04. The First Answer chamber is not opened, described, approached or named in any of the fifty chapters, no person is sent to it, and no one asked the mould maker.** Salla Keene is not found. The Reckoning 619 bench-book and the Vale Bellhouse archive are not restored and no copy of either is invented. No bell he has ever made is unbarred, no Crown office is defeated, no officer is relieved of anything, and the Crown notice about an originating workshop is still nailed to the ward board in Kells.


## 10o. VOLUME 04 BATCH 0003 — CHAPTERS 171–180, DAYS 157–166 — THE BATCH RECORD AND THE POST-BATCH REVIEW PASS. THE REVERSAL IS EARNED AND PAID, THE BATCH WAS NOT RESTARTED, AND FOUR FINDINGS NO EDITING PASS CAN REPAIR ARE LEFT ON THE PAGE WITH THEIR FIGURES.

**The ten cards for this batch were written by the batch itself and there is no `outline/batches/volume-04-batch-0003.md`, and none may be created. The cards are set out in full in `state/batch-0018-summary.md` and were checked against `outline/volume-04.md` and its midpoint-reversal paragraph before a word of prose was drafted. The writing-time record is that same file; §10o is the permanent record of what the batch did and of what was done to it afterwards, and the reviewer's own report is `logs/batch-0003.review.log`, which `.gitignore` carries and which can therefore never be committed — this is now the fourth file in this project cited as missing for that reason and not for any other. The findings survive here and at `reviews/volume-04/batch-0003.md` and nowhere else.**

**WHAT THE BATCH PAID, IN THE ORDER THE OUTLINE NAMES IT. A bridge, agreed about by four people in Batch 0002, PAID BY BEING SAYED NOT TO BE PAYABLE: two stringers of twelve foot and fourteen deck boards, cut nine years ago and left in a yard past the turning, and the man will not sell one board and wants the lot gone together or none, and no district will buy timber it cannot set against a new line (Chapter 0171).** The first of the line of settlements entered as work rather than as a place people live: a ford gang of six shifting gravel for a heap the district pays for, and a man who beds an edge losing the edge because a return wants feet of ditch and feet can be counted and edges cannot (Chapter 0172). A market crew of about fifty under canvas at forty stalls, a village that packs up twice a week and moves a mile, and a book that takes coin for a row (Chapter 0173). A landing gang of eight and food for forty, and **Tamsin Rook at a landing post with her hand flat on it, reading the gang's work through the wood, asked nothing — the best writing in the batch (Chapter 0174)**. A district's clerk mending a shelf badly in twenty light nails and a carter doing it in four strokes, and the return seen for the first time with nine places entered as work with a figure of mouths against each (Chapter 0175). **THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL, AT THE POSITION THE OUTLINE FIXES: nine places up the water, each with a market or a ford or a landing, each with people living at it, each entered in a district's fair hand in a return about a thing in a ward, paid coin and meal, and no box anywhere in four years of that clerk's book that says a household — and the reason arriving on the page in the clerk's own mouth WHILE HE RULED THE DAY'S LINE, which is that a house that is answered for is a house with a keeper, and a keeper is a man who can be asked to hold a line at the hour, and he cannot get keepers up that water (Chapter 0176).** The instrument proved unequal to its job: one true thing put once to four people in the same words and refused four times in four trades' grammars, because a document can be carried by a district for forty years and cannot be produced by a person, and a sentence dies with the last ear that heard it (Chapter 0177). **The volume's relationship movement, paid once: a question asked about the road and not about her, one word back, a no accepted and not improved, and nobody asked her for a reason at any hour including him (Chapter 0178).** A fifth walker for one day, who walks because the next cut lies that way and not because she was asked (Chapter 0179). And the close, with the third column holding a ruling and not a sentence, and the road going on whether he can read the drawing of it or not (Chapter 0180).

**THE OUTLINE WAS KEPT AND NOT CORRECTED IN THIS PASS, BECAUSE A BATCH IS NOT A VOLUME CLOSE: `outline/volume-04.md` line 88 gives this volume's fifth batch a Crown clapper fitted to a gate and the volume does not open the fitting, and the reconciliation with §8's standing bar on the word is written into the Batch 0004 prompt rather than into the outline, and no outline line was touched.**

**THE WRITING-TIME SWEEPS, MEASURED AND NOT CLAIMED, AND TWO OF THE FIGURES IN THEM WERE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED BELOW.** No weekday and no month is named in any of the ten chapters and none was drafted and removed. Every day-time hour in the ten chapters is bare and the ninth hour of the evening is the only half-day, carried twice in each of the ten and opening every day-book, and there is no eighth hour anywhere. No `##` heading in any of the ten. **The word *clapper* does not appear in Chapters 151 to 180, measured.** No boundary name is on the page: *Dain*, *First Answer*, *OFF. 411*, *Salla Keene*, *Iven Sore*, *Nell Fenn*, *Hob Kersey*, *Mara Vale* and *Oren Pike* all return nothing, and *Kells* occurs exactly **once in each of the ten chapters and in every case inside that chapter's own day-book**, in the sealed-yard line. The seed-word sweep of `chapters/volume-04/` for *displac*, *administrative*, *invisible*, *registering*, *settlement*, *line of villages*, *forced answer* and *copied answer* returns nothing. No age for anybody. No bell rung or struck by anybody. Two things were drafted wrong and repaired before the state layer was written: *first answer* in Chapter 0178, which hit the First Answer sweep and became *first plain word*, and *settlement* in Chapter 0173, which became *village*. The per-line bold-marker parity count is zero introduced on every state file the batch touched.

**THE THREE DUPLICATE-PROSE TESTS, RE-MEASURED BY THIS PASS, AND THE WRITING-TIME FIGURES WERE WRONG IN TWO OF THE THREE COLUMNS:**

| test | what it can see | the writing-time figure said | **measured** | what the measured text is |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1, intra-file paragraph pairs | a block that appears twice inside one file | 11, in all ten | **12, in all ten** | *the frame has not been in a building since the thirty third* — the refrain sentence, not the custody block, which is what the summary named |
| 2, line-level, lines of 25 words or more, shared run of 20 | a block printed twice inside one blockquote entry | clean | **clean in all ten** | 11 to 18 long lines a file and no shared run of twenty words in any of them |
| 3, cross-file, all forty-five pairs | a sentence the batch carries on purpose in all ten | about 95 words, maximum | **109 words maximum, 99 minimum, and no pair below 99** | the invariant core of the custody block, between the strike count that varies and the register block that follows it |

## 10p. VOLUME 04 BATCH 0004 — CHAPTERS 181–190, DAYS 167–176 — THE BATCH RECORD, THE TEN CARDS, EVERY FIGURE MEASURED, AND THE SIX THINGS THAT WERE DRAFTED WRONG AND FIXED BEFORE THE STATE LAYER WAS WRITTEN.

**The ten cards for this batch were written by the batch itself and there is no `outline/batches/volume-04-batch-0004.md`, and none may be created. They are set out in full in `state/batch-0019-summary.md` and were checked against `outline/volume-04.md` line 88 and read twice before a word of prose was drafted. THE REVERSAL CARD LESSON CARRIED: every card fixes a position, a fence and a cost and NOT a reason, and in four of the ten days the reason refused to arrive at all, which is the batch's shape and not a failure of the plan. THE WRITING-TIME RECORD IS THAT SAME FILE; §10p is the permanent record of what the batch did. THE OUTLINE WAS NOT CORRECTED AND NO OUTLINE LINE WAS TOUCHED.**

**WHAT THE BATCH PAID, IN THE ORDER THE OUTLINE'S ENTRY FOR CHAPTERS 181 TO 190 NAMES IT, AND ALL FIVE BEATS ARE ON THE PAGE.** **THE MAP LAID FLAT ON FOUR TABLES BY FOUR PEOPLE WHO CANNOT READ IT EITHER.** A carter on a weighbeam (0181), a weighmaster at a canton bench (0182), a draughtsman in a mould shed (0184) and a bench clerk at a lock (0185). **HE ASKED NOBODY TO LOOK AT IT: he put it down, stepped back about four feet, and said nothing for as long as it took each of them to do whatever they wanted with it, and he told one room out loud that he had not asked, and he did not tell any of them what he wanted off it, and he thanked nobody.** What came off the four tables is a crease that has not been folded, a corner heavier than a crease, a price with no ledger under it, and a figure at the head of a column — **a fact about four men, and the only form this instrument has ever had.** **HE STILL CANNOT READ A RUN AND THIS VOLUME DID NOT TEACH HIM**, and the paper itself gave him two new things: a draughtsman in this kingdom cannot see a place where the drawing stops, because his trade is a place where the drawing stops; and the figure nearest the top of the sheet is the sheet's own number in the same hand, and a clerk has had that figure in the corner of a rate book for six years and will not join the two because joining them is not his work. **THE RECTANGLE IS NOT EXPLAINED, THE TWO QUESTIONS WRITTEN DOWN IN CHAPTER 0160 ARE NARROWED BY THREE LINES AND ANSWERED IN NEITHER HALF, THE ROAD SURVEYOR'S FINDING THAT THE TWO BLACKS ARE ONE INK WET AND DRIED STANDS AND IS NOT OVERTURNED, AND THE MAP IS NOT READ WHOLE: THAT IS BATCH 0005'S RESOLUTION.** **A RIVER CANTON THAT WILL SHARE A REPAIR AND WILL NOT TAKE A COMMAND** (0182, 0185, 0186): nine benches, one river, one rate set by weight, a bell-foundry tradition surviving as eleven foundry weights in a press in a shed that nobody has entered in a book in ten years, and a civic life that is a habit of turning up. **They will share a repair and they will refuse a command, and the refusal is not politics: a gate nobody owns gets mended and a gate a man owns does not, and those are the same gate in about nine years, and every gate on this river has gone two hundred years without anybody standing over it. The price is not the money, it is that the rate goes up for the whole reach for everybody and goes up again if the sill fails with no name at the top of the bill. HE IS NOT THE CLERK OF IT, HE KEEPS NO TERMS, AND HE ASKED THE SAME QUESTION AT TWO BENCHES AND GOT FOUR DAYS AND NINE DAYS, BOTH TRUE, AND CARRIED NEITHER TO THE OTHER MAN, because a man working to a figure he has been given comes at eight instead of at the second hour, and that is the whole of what a command is.** **THE INHERITED QUESTION FROM 0146 BECOMES CIVIC AND EXPENSIVE AND THE BATCH PAYS THE COST AND NOT THE ANSWER** (0187, 0189): a keeper is a person who can be asked to hold a line, and a person who is asked has to be able to say what it would be to refuse, and no form in this kingdom anywhere asks a man that, and the asking was in words with no form, which is a protection and is also why he has not slept properly since the spring, and the only way to find out what refusing would be is to go up four hundred yards to four doors, and the going is the damage. **IT IS NOT ANSWERED IN THIS BATCH AND IT IS NOT ANSWERED IN THE VOLUME, AND CHAPTER 0190'S DAY-BOOK SAYS SO IN THE MAN'S OWN HAND.** **A CROWN FITTING IS FITTED TO A GATE SOMEWHERE AND THE VOLUME DOES NOT OPEN IT** (0188, and referred to twice afterwards and never explained): a foot of box on the end of a gate frame at the hinge, a bracket, a coupling with a jumper across it, and a wire going down inside the post, under the deck and off towards the bank, put there in the spring by a man with a sheet with a number and a day who said it was for the gate and went away with the sheet. **NO MECHANISM OPENED, NO PERSON SHOWN INSIDE ONE, NO PART NAMED AS A PART, THE WORD BARRED FROM EVERY LIVING SPEAKER, AND THE BATCH DOES NOT FIND OUT WHO PUT IT THERE.** The fitting's only consequence in this batch is that it made a gate man's job harder, which is a thing a gate has the way a gate has a hinge. **THE OLD NETWORK'S ACCOUNT OF A LINE THAT WAS WORKED AND THEN WAS NOT, AS THREE PARTIAL ANSWERS AND NO CONCLUSION** (0190): a rope-maker coiling hawser says there were four places on that reach where a sheet was read out at a landing and he laid the rope for all four, and the rope is ordered by whoever is working the water, and there were four and there is one and the one left is the worst because it is the one nobody wants; a byre that is a byre, a gate that was a stake and a hut, a wall that is not old, and then he stops. **He did not name a line, did not say when it stopped and did not say why, and Tomas did not ask him the rest, and the boy stopped turning coils, which is how a boy knows.**

**THE THREE PARTIAL ANSWERS ARE COUNTED ON THE PAGE IN CHAPTER 0190'S DAY-BOOK AND THEY ARE A REASON, A PRICE AND AN ACCOUNT, AND NONE OF THE THREE IS AN ANSWER.** The reason came off a clerk's mouth in Chapter 0176 while he ruled a line. The price came off a man with a scraper on a lock step in Chapter 0187 and off the same man at a form in Chapter 0189. The account came off a man coiling a rope in Chapter 0190, out loud, while he was doing something else. **THE CHAPTER 0145 SEED IS AT TWO AND STAYS AT TWO: no character in Chapters 181 to 190 talks about his father's line, his father's work, a line that came up through a wall, or a day a line stopped answering, no character calls himself the second man, and no day-book in the batch refers to either seed. THE SEED SWEEP OF `chapters/volume-04/` FOR *displac*, *administrative*, *invisible*, *registering*, *settlement*, *line of villages*, *forced answer* AND *copied answer* RETURNS NOTHING, and *settlement* was drafted into Chapter 0189 twice and removed before the file was saved.**

**THE FIGURES, AND THEY ARE THE HEADLINE OF THIS PASS.** **Batch 0001 mean 3,085 words a chapter. Batch 0002 mean 2,695. BATCH 0003 MEAN 829, TOTAL 8,289, A THIRD OF THE BATCH BEFORE IT. BATCH 0004: 2,933 / 2,999 / 2,516 / 2,819 / 2,797 / 2,827 / 2,936 / 2,913 / 2,950 / 2,637, TOTAL 28,327, MEAN 2,832.7, MINIMUM 2,516, MAXIMUM 2,999. NO CHAPTER IS UNDER THE 1,800 FLOOR AND EVERY CHAPTER IS INSIDE THE 2,000 TO 3,000 BAND.** The day-book is 8,497 words, **THIRTY PER CENT of the batch, down from thirty-four in Batch 0003 and forty-seven in Batch 0002, and no chapter's day-book exceeds thirty-five per cent of that chapter, the two that come closest being Chapter 0189 at 34.9% and Chapter 0186 at 33.9%, and the batch figure is AT the thirty per cent line and not under it.** The shape rules are met and measured: **the frame-and-four-bolts opening opens three of the ten (0182, 0185, 0186) and in the other seven the fact is on the page or in the day-book in different words each time; the scene counts are 3 / 5 / 3 / 4 / 4 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 4, so five chapters run on a single break and three run on three or more; four chapters run most of their length on one sustained exchange (0181, 0184, 0187, 0189) with the work visible and the theme nowhere stated aloud; five chapters open on a person who is not Tomas inside that person's own work (0181, 0183, 0184, 0188, 0190); and eight of the ten have at least one of the four off the page entirely.**

**THE FIVE NEW NAMED PEOPLE OF THIS BATCH ALL ENTER WITH A ROLE AND NO AGE, AND NONE MAY BE GIVEN ONE: Merrit Sallow, a weighmaster of one of nine benches with a beam and a set of foundry weights; Perry Nunn, a bench clerk of eleven years who has never filled a fourth column; Harlan Loxley, a mould maker who has read drawings for nineteen years; Cobb Arley, a lock keeper with a small bell on a bracket and four doors up four hundred yards of bank; and Rafe Mear, a rope-maker who laid the rope for four places and lays one now. The man who shuts the cart bridge gate is UNNAMED ON THE PAGE AND MAY NOT BE GIVEN A NAME. A MAN'S YEARS IN A TRADE ARE A DURATION AND ARE NOT AN AGE AND NO DURATION IN THIS BATCH EXCEEDS THIRTY-TWO, WHICH IS THE ONLY AGE ON THE PAGE AND IT IS IN SABIN FELL'S MOUTH IN VOLUME 01.**

**AND THE STANDING FORM OF A CLOCK SECTION AND OF A BATCH RECORD IN THIS FILE IS NAMED AND MEASURED RATHER THAN LEFT AS A HIDDEN REPETITION, ON THE §10o PRECEDENT, AND THE FIGURE IS EIGHT. §5i shares THREE twenty-word runs with the other clock sections and §10p shares SIX, AND BETWEEN THEM THAT IS EIGHT DISTINCT RUNS, and every one of the eight is the standing wording of a clock entry or of a record in this ledger: the word-by-word hour-phrase search, which §5g, §5h and §5i all carry, the hand counter's standing sentence, which §5h and §5i both carry, the *about* family's first seven members in a row, the three-test duplicate table's test-two row, the per-line bold-marker parity sentence with its seven file figures, the seed sweep's list of eight words, the line-level near-duplicate scan's standing sentence, and the seed's four nouns. That is deliberate — a file that keeps one ledger of twenty batches is going to have one way of recording a sweep, and the alternative is ten batches each inventing a vocabulary, which is a worse defect than a repeated noun — and it is named here with its figure so that the next pass can see it rather than discover it. MEASURED HONESTLY AND NOT OPTIMISTICALLY: `state/continuity.md` carried SIXTY-FIVE twenty-word runs of this kind before this pass and carries SEVENTY-THREE now, and the eight are the pass's own. On the state layer as a whole this pass introduced eight and removed four, and the other five state files introduced none at all.**

**THE CHECKS RUN ON EVERY FILE THIS PASS TOUCHED ARE THREE.** One: the per-line bold-marker count, unchanged from the pre-pass commit — `state/current.md` 0, `state/continuity.md` 7, `state/character-state.md` 1, `state/open-threads.md` 0, `state/volume-index.md` 1, `state/chapter-summaries.md` 5, `state/batch-0019-summary.md` 0, the new clock section 0, the batch record 0, the ten chapters 0 each, and the next-phase prompt 0. **AND THE CHECK WAS RUN AFTER THE SENTENCE CLAIMING IT WAS IN PLACE, which is §10l's rule and the fourth time in this project it has been done deliberately.** Two: the line-level near-duplicate scan at twenty-five words a line and a twenty-word shared run, run on the state layer as well as on the prose, and **it found the same shape on the state layer that it found on the prose and it is recorded in the batch summary's repair list.** Three: the reverse sweep, searching this pass's new state text for every word and figure removed from the prose.

**THIS PASS CHANGED NO EVENT, NO MOTIVE, NO REFUSAL, NO DOCUMENT, NO CUSTODY STATE, NO HOUR, NO DAY, NO FIGURE OF THE PLOT AND NO CHAPTER TITLE. IT DID NOT RESTART ANYTHING, IT DID NOT REOPEN ANY FINDING §10i, §10j, §10k, §10l, §10m, §10n OR §10o SETTLED, IT CHANGED NO OUTLINE, IT EDITED NO CONTROLLER FILE, AND IT DID NOT CREATE, MOVE OR DELETE ANY PHASE FILE — THE ONE NEXT-PHASE PROMPT IS `workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, CHAPTERS 191–200, DAYS 177–186, THE FLOOD AND THE INCOMPLETE WARNING AND THE RESOLUTION. THE BATCH THAT FOLLOWS IT IS THE FIFTH AND LAST OF VOLUME 04, AND ITS CARDS ARE NOT WRITTEN HERE.**

## 10q. VOLUME 04 BATCH 0004 — THE POST-BATCH REVIEW PASS. EIGHT FINDINGS, ALL REPAIRED, NO CHAPTER FILE TOUCHED, NO PROSE REWRITTEN, AND THE TWO INHERITED OUTLINE DISAGREEMENTS CORRECTED IN THE OUTLINE AND NOT IN THE PROSE.

**The reviewer's own findings survive as `reviews/volume-04/batch-0004.md`, which is the committed disposition. `logs/batch-0004.review.log` cannot be in the repository — `.gitignore` carries `logs/` and `*.log` — so this section and that file are the record, on §10h's and §10i's rule that a later pass cites the §10 sections and does not wait for a log.** The finding in one sentence: **the ten chapters are sound and every defect is in the state layer, the index or the outline.** The reviewer's own verdict was that the chapters are publishable-quality and internally consistent and that no finding required touching prose. That verdict was tested and not merely accepted, and the four measurements it rests on were re-measured here rather than taken on trust.

### What was measured before anything was changed, and the four figures that held

- **LENGTH: 2,516 / 2,999 / 2,516 / 2,819 / 2,797 / 2,827 / 2,936 / 2,913 / 2,950 / 2,637 is not what this pass measured; the measured figures are 2,933 / 2,999 / 2,516 / 2,819 / 2,797 / 2,827 / 2,936 / 2,913 / 2,950 / 2,637, total 28,327, mean 2,832.7, minimum 2,516, maximum 2,999, and every one is inside the 2,000 to 3,000 band with no chapter under 1,800.** The first figure in that sentence is the reviewer's own transcription slip and it is named here so no later pass goes looking for a 2,516 in Chapter 0181: the 2,516 is Chapter 0183, which is the minimum. **Batch 0003's 829-word mean has not been repeated.**
- **THE DAY MAP HOLDS: one chapter to a day, 167 to 176, no gap and no duplicate, verified against the day-book header and the prose in each of the ten.**
- **THE TWO COUNTERS HOLD. The not-asking counter reads thirty-one on Day 167 through forty on Day 176, one figure per chapter, every one of them on the page. The strike counter reads ninety on Day 167 through ninety-nine on Day 176. The frame arithmetic holds: Day 167 minus 33 is 134, Day 176 minus 33 is 143, and Day 176 minus 77 is 99.**
- **THE REPEATED PROSE HOLDS, and the review's own figure for it was half a measurement, which is the fault this batch's own summary warns about two hundred words earlier. THE LONGEST BLOCK IS TWENTY-FIVE WORDS, NOT TWENTY-FOUR, AND IT IS IN SIX FILES AND NOT TEN**: it is the day-book's opening register line, *The register is thirteen. I have produced nothing and asked for nothing and been given nothing and taken nothing, and nobody was thanked today*, in Chapters 0182, 0183, 0185, 0186, 0188 and 0190. The **twenty-four-word** block the review quoted — *Her bell is in her bag and it is not rung and nobody asked her for a reason at any hour today, including me* — is the widest-spread of the three and is in all ten. The third is twenty words, also the register, in six files. **So: longest twenty-five in six, widest twenty-four in ten, and both are standing custody sentences on the exemption §10n named. Both are under the thirty-nine-word figure the Volume 03 audit established, and neither is a refrain.** The review reached twenty-four by measuring a sentence where the block is a sentence and a lead-in, which is a defensible instrument and the wrong one for the question it was answering.

### The eight findings, and what each one was

1. **`state/open-threads.md` thread 7 was factually wrong and contradicted the six threads the same file's own commit added below it: it said the map is read in Batch 0004.** Batch 0004 is spent and the map was not read. **REPAIRED IN PLACE.** The thread now says what the batch did with the sheet, which is that it went onto four tables and came off in his own hands four times, names the four surfaces and the four chapters they are in, and states that the reading is Batch 0005's. Nothing was closed and nothing was added.
2. **`state/open-threads.md` thread 8 was stale in two places: it said Batch 0004 owed the volume a third partial answer, and it said nothing in Batch 0004 would close either of the two threads.** The third partial answer was paid on Day 176 in Chapter 0190, as an account and not as a finding. **REPAIRED IN PLACE**, with the three named in the order they were earned, the Chapter 0145 seed recorded as still at two, and the two threads left open and re-dated to Batch 0005.
3. **The intro block of `state/open-threads.md` was not updated: the title said Chapter 190 while the mapping paragraph still stopped at Batch 0002 and still reported the manuscript at Chapter 170.** **REPAIRED.** Batches 0003 and 0004 and their two summaries are now named, the manuscript position and the Day 176 close are stated, what Batch 0004 opened and did not close is stated, and the three reviewed dispositions are listed.
4. **A FALSE CHAPTER ATTRIBUTION AND A FIGURE THAT WAS RIGHT, IN TWO FILES.** `state/continuity.md` §5i and `state/batch-0019-summary.md` both said *Chapter 0190's day-book says he has not put his hand on the frame's own crown in a hundred and forty-nine days*. **Chapter 0190 contains neither *crown* nor *forty-nine*; *crown* is on the page in this batch only in Chapter 0187, about a bell's crown.** The figure is right and is not on any page: forty-nine is Day 176 minus Day 127. The reviewer's note claimed the arithmetic was also wrong; it is not, and that part of the finding is declined on the measurement. **THE ATTRIBUTION WAS FALSE AND IT WAS THE WORTHLESS PART.** The last page that states the hands is Chapter 0150's own day-book at Day 136, **— A SUPERLATIVE THAT WAS ALSO FALSE, AND THE VOLUME 04 AUDIT CORRECTED IT IN PLACE HERE AND IN THE OTHER FIVE PLACES IT APPEARS: CHAPTER 0155 AND THEN CHAPTERS 0160 TO 0180 — TWENTY-TWO FILES — ALSO STATE THE HAND IN THEIR OWN DAY-BOOKS, AND CHAPTERS 0156 TO 0159 DO NOT, AND THE LAST PAGE IN THE NOVEL THAT STATES IT IS CHAPTER 0180 AT DAY 166. WHAT IS TRUE OF CHAPTER 0150 IS ONLY THAT IT STATES IT,** where he writes that he put his hand flat on its crown four times and that the fourth was the last one and he knew it was the last one while he was doing it, and the four days are listed in §9. **BOTH FILES NOW CARRY THE RIGHT CHAPTER AND SAY PLAINLY THAT FORTY-NINE IS THE SECTION'S OWN ARITHMETIC AND MAY NOT BE CITED TO A CHAPTER.**
5. **THE *about* FAMILY TOTAL WAS WRONG BY ONE AND CONTRADICTED ITS OWN CORRECT PARTS.** Both files said 231. All ten per-file figures in the same sentence were right and they sum to 230, which is what the ten chapters hold. **CORRECTED TO 230 IN ALL THREE PLACES IT APPEARED** — §5i, the batch summary, and a third copy inside §10p that the review did not raise and that this pass found. **The derived rate was corrected with it: 230 over 28,327 words is 0.81 of the word in a hundred and not 0.82**, and 0.82 was the rate the wrong 231 produced.
6. ***About six* WAS COUNTED TWICE AND OCCURS ONCE**, in Chapter 0187 at line 5. Every other sub-phrase figure was verified and is right: *about the* 70, *about a* 54, *about nine* 29, *about four* 16, *about it* 14, *about two* 12, bare 7, *about that* 6, *about me* 3, *about him* 2, *about paper* 2, *about this* 2, *about thirty* 2, and the ten singles including *about six*. **CORRECTED IN ALL THREE PLACES.**
7. **TWO INHERITED OUTLINE DISAGREEMENTS WERE LEFT STANDING, ON THE §10h AND §10i PRECEDENT THAT A DISAGREEMENT BETWEEN A VOLUME AND ITS OUTLINE IS FIXED IN THE OUTLINE AND NOT IN THE PROSE.** `outline/volume-04.md` line 88 said **The map read** and **and the thing it says about the capital** for Chapters 181 to 190, and the batch explicitly did not read the map; and line 89 said **the map shows the First Answer**, which reads as an instruction to go to a building no chapter of this volume may approach. **BOTH CORRECTED IN THE OUTLINE, WITH THE PROSE UNTOUCHED AND THE PLOT UNCHANGED.** Line 88 now says the map goes onto four tables and is not read on them, names what came off them, and states that the reading is Batch 0005's; line 89 now says the map, read at last and in a bench room, shows that obligations drain from the edges to sustain the capital, and bars opening, describing or approaching the chamber. **The volume's resolution is unchanged: the cantons still agree to share physical repairs and refuse a central command, and Volume 05 still finds Lira alive in the chamber and not before.**
8. **`state/volume-index.md` labelled Batch 0004 *SPENT AND REVIEWED-READY*, which is not a state the project has and which sits confusingly beside the *SPENT AND REVIEWED* entries.** **REPAIRED to SPENT, REVIEWED AND REPAIRED**, with the §10q citation and the disposition, and the closing sentence of that file's standing paragraph corrected to match.

### Two things this pass found on its own, which are the same shape as findings 5 and 6

- **THE CLAIM *THE BATCH THE MAP IS READ IN* WAS NOT ONLY IN `state/open-threads.md`. IT WAS IN FOUR MORE PLACES, IN THREE OF WHICH IT WAS A FALSE STATEMENT ABOUT WHAT HAPPENED** — the head of `state/batch-0019-summary.md`, the Batch 0004 line in `state/character-state.md`, the one-volume-arc line in `state/volume-index.md`, and the head of `workspace/volume-04/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`. **The first three are state and are corrected. THE FOURTH IS A SPENT PHASE'S PROMPT AND IS LEFT AS IT IS, ON THE RULE THAT A PROMPT IS A RECORD OF WHAT A PHASE WAS TOLD AND IS NOT REWRITTEN AFTER THE PHASE HAS RUN**; §10p is the record of what the batch did instead, and this section, the outline amendment and the index now say so in three more places, so the plan language in a spent prompt cannot mislead a reader who is following the state layer. **A plan and an outcome are allowed to differ and the outcome is what the state layer records.**
- **THE FOURTH PLACE IS `state/chapter-summaries.md`, WHICH WAS NOT RAISED BY EITHER THE REVIEW OR THE PROSE, AND IT IS CLEAN:** the ten chapter summaries for 181 to 190 are present, one per chapter, and they carry no *crown*, no *forty-nine*, no 231 and no *about six*.
- **A NINTH FINDING, FOUND BY READING AROUND THE FIGURE AND NOT BY A SWEEP, AND IT IS OLD.** One garbled word, **WHATSOEVER, which is not a word, in a sentence that says a record says nothing whatever about what became of Lira Vale** — and it was in **THREE PLACES AND NOT ONE**: §9 of this file, `state/batch-0013-summary.md` line 21 and `state/batch-0014-summary.md` line 60, the two batch summaries being the two passes that wrote the sentence in the first place. All three are present in the pre-pass commit, none is Batch 0004's, and all three are corrected to *whatever*. **An earlier draft of this finding said four and named the tail of §13, and §13 does not carry the sentence; the figure is three and the wrong one was caught in this file before the file was saved, which is §10l's rule for the fourth time in this project.** It sits in the rule about the one person who is not found, which is a line nobody re-reads, and a figure can be checked and a word cannot: the whole-file check is one string and it is now zero, the two occurrences in this file and in the review disposition being the sentences that name the repair.

### The two self-referential figures in this file, re-measured after the edits and not before

**Editing a file that quotes its own measurements invalidates them, and this pass found that twice and repaired both.**

- **THE PER-LINE BOLD-MARKER PARITY FIGURE. §10p records `state/continuity.md` at 7 and `state/batch-0019-summary.md` at 0. MY FIRST EDITS PUT THE CONTINUITY FILE AT 8 AND THE BATCH SUMMARY AT 1, because in both I removed a closing bold marker and left the opening one.** Both were found by the parity check and repaired, and the seven state files are back at the figures §10p records: current 0, continuity 7, character-state 1, open-threads 0, volume-index 1, chapter-summaries 5, batch-0019-summary 0. **The outline file, which is not in §10p's list, went to 1 on the same slip and is back at 0.**
- **THE SHARED-TWENTY-WORD-RUN FIGURE. §10p names eight standing blocks shared between §5i and §10p and the rest of this file. MY FIRST INSERTION OF THE CHAPTER 0150 QUOTE INTO §5i REPEATED A TWENTY-WORD RUN THAT §9 ALREADY CARRIED, WHICH WOULD HAVE MADE §10p's EIGHT INTO NINE.** That was caught by re-running the cross-section scan and the quote was shortened to the clause §9 does not use, and the scan now returns §5i at eighty-seven shared runs and §10p at fifty, and the file-wide figure at six hundred and nine — **all three identical to the pre-pass measurement, so §10p's eight stands and is not restated.** **The eight named blocks are unchanged, and the hand counter's standing sentence is still the block shared between §5h and §5i, because that sentence's opening was left word for word.**

### What this pass did not do, listed so the next writer does not look for it

**NO CHAPTER FILE WAS OPENED FOR EDITING. NO EVENT, MOTIVE, REFUSAL, DOCUMENT, CUSTODY STATE, HOUR, DAY, TITLE, WORD COUNT OR FIGURE OF THE PLOT CHANGED. NO CARD CHANGED, NO POSITION CHANGED, NO FENCE CHANGED, NO COST CHANGED, AND NO PROSE WAS REWRITTEN, RESTARTED OR REGENERATED.** The good prose of Chapters 0181 to 0190 was read by this pass and left alone, which is the outcome the reviewer's verdict predicted and this pass confirmed by measurement. No thread was closed. No document was added and the register is thirteen. No bell was rung, named or unbarred. No name was entered anywhere. No fitting was opened and no mechanism was named. No clock was rebuilt. **§5i IS THE LIVE CLOCK AND IT WAS EDITED IN PLACE, BESIDE ITSELF, AND NOT APPENDED, ON THE §10o PRECEDENT.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and was not touched, and it still reads `phase-000-bootstrap`, which is consistent with every earlier batch in this repository and is **not** a finding. **NO NEW PHASE FILE WAS CREATED: the one next phase is `workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, Chapters 191 to 200, Days 177 to 186, and it was corrected in place and not rebuilt, because the only thing wrong with it was a figure this pass has now fixed and a divergence this pass has now recorded in the outline.**

**AND THE ONE THING A LATER PASS SHOULD KNOW ABOUT §5i AFTER THIS EDIT: the hand counter is four, the fourth hand was on Day 127, the last page that says so is Chapter 0180 at Day 166 and not Chapter 0150, because Chapter 0155 and then Chapters 0160 to 0180 carry it in their own day-books, twenty-two files, and Chapters 0156 to 0159 do not (**the Chapter 0150 attribution in this sentence was false and is corrected by the Volume 04 audit**), and forty-nine is this file's subtraction and not a quotation. NO CHAPTER MAY BE CITED FOR IT, and no chapter of the batch says it.**

## 10r. VOLUME 04 BATCH 0005 — THE POST-BATCH REVIEW PASS. EIGHT CHAPTER FILES OPENED, TWENTY-NINE PLACES, THIRTEEN OF THEM AGES THAT SHOULD NEVER HAVE BEEN DRAFTED, AND THE VOLUME'S CLOSE PROMPT WRITTEN AT LAST. THE BATCH WAS NOT RESTARTED AND NO PLOT WAS TOUCHED.

**This is the sixth batch in this project whose own summary claimed its sweeps were clean and was not, and the first one where the failure was not a figure.** The batch's writing-time record is `state/batch-0020-summary.md`; the clock is §5j; the batch record is that file and this section; the reviewer's disposition is at `reviews/volume-04/batch-0005.md`. **The batch's reviewer found six things. This pass found three more, and the third of those is the only finding in the whole record that is not mechanical.**

### What the reviewer found, and what was done

1. **THE VOLUME-CLOSE PROMPT WAS REFUSED AND DID NOT EXIST.** `outline/volume-04.md` runs Chapters 151 to 200 on Days 137 to 186 in five batches of ten, this batch wrote 191 to 200, and the batch's summary stated in terms that it was *not a volume-close phase and no volume-close prompt was created*. That is a refusal of a standing rule and not an observation about it, and its effect was that self-dispatch had nothing to select. Volumes 01 and 03 both carry `workspace/<volume>/audit/PROMPT.md`. **`workspace/volume-04/audit/PROMPT.md` IS NOW WRITTEN AND IS THE ONE NEXT PHASE, and the claim in the batch summary is corrected on its face. It was not written by the batch and is not claimed as the batch's work.**
2. **THE STATE LAYER WAS TWO FILES OUT OF SIX.** `state/current.md`, `state/volume-index.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and `state/character-state.md` were all still describing Chapter 190 as the last chapter written, and **the people who entered in the ten days were in no state file at all, so the binding do-not-age list of §4 did not cover one of them.** All five are now written at Chapter 200. **AND THE TWO BAD NAMES HAVE A FINDABLE ORIGIN, WHICH IS THE POINT: they came out of THIS PROJECT'S OWN PLANNING DOCUMENT. The Chapter 0192 card in `state/batch-0020-summary.md` planned the woman at the fourth door as *Sena Ludlow* and the lighterman as *Amon Trayle*, and the prose named NEITHER — the woman is *the woman at the fourth door* and the page says *his name was Trayle* and gives him no forename. The same card also planned that Trayle's family was on the flat, and the chapter did not deliver that; what is on the page is *no house here* and *his boy*. THE CARD HAS NOW BEEN CORRECTED IN PLACE AND THE DIVERGENCE RECORDED, ON THE §10h AND §10i PRECEDENT THAT A PLAN MAY DIFFER FROM AN OUTCOME AND THE STATE LAYER RECORDS THE OUTCOME. THE LESSON IS THE ONE THIS SECTION KEEPS ARRIVING AT: A FINDING IS ONLY AS GOOD AS THE DOCUMENT IT CAME FROM, AND A PLANNING DOCUMENT THAT NAMES SOMEBODY THE PROSE DOES NOT WILL GENERATE A FINDING ABOUT A CHARACTER WHO DOES NOT EXIST, AND THE ERROR PROPAGATES OUTWARD UNTIL SOMEBODY READS THE MANUSCRIPT.** AND THE REVIEWER'S LIST OF NINE NEW PEOPLE CONTAINED TWO NAMES THAT ARE NOT ON ANY PAGE — *Amon Trayle* and *Sena Ludlow*. THE PAGE HAS *Trayle*, A LIGHTERMAN, GIVEN AS *his name was Trayle* AND WITH NO FORENAME, AND THE WOMAN AT THE FOURTH DOOR IN CHAPTER 0192 IS UNNAMED AND WAS NEVER NAMED. NEITHER FORENAME WAS ADOPTED, because adopting them would add canon to a manuscript that does not have it, and a repair pass that invents names to satisfy a reviewer's list commits the error it is repairing. **Two further names that recur in these ten chapters are not new and were not on the reviewer's list either: Isa Trevail and Pell Okell both entered in earlier Volume 04 batches and both already in `state/character-state.md`, and so are Mab Sarter and Ned Adley. THE PEOPLE GENUINELY NEW IN THESE TEN DAYS, AND IN NO STATE FILE UNTIL THIS PASS, ARE: **Abel Trope, Obin Farrow, Trayle with no forename, the woman at the fourth door with no name at all, Wick, Retha Vask, Garret Poole, Averill Crome and Rennick Dole** — eight names on the page, one name and one forename missing, and one place, **Hallowmere**, a town of about six hundred people on the reach above the low quarter, which is now registered in `state/current.md` and `state/volume-index.md`.**
3. **FIVE PLACES WHERE THE *ABOUT* SWEEP HAD DESTROYED A SENTENCE.** Chapter 0192, a lime carrier's speech where a clause-ending object had been eaten and two sentences spliced into one — *I have never once said so out loud to* ran straight into *And if the water is over those steps*; **anybody** was supplied. Chapter 0194, a doubled opening quotation mark, removed. Chapter 0196, a stray hyphen in *the other -odd of them*, where the word is **half-odd** and always was. Chapter 0197's day-book opening line and Chapter 0199's bench clerk's mouth, each an orphaned comma with nothing in front of it, each repaired by **deleting the comma** rather than by supplying the missing phrase, because the sweep's missing words are unrecoverable and a day-book list and a plain sentence are both complete without them. **The batch's own file claimed the orphan-mark sweep *returns zero in all ten files* and it returned two. That claim is corrected on the face of the file that made it.**
4. **FOUR FIGURES THAT DID NOT RECONCILE, and one more found while correcting them.** The per-file word list summed to 27,876 against a stated 27,878; the day-book list summed to 8,128 against a stated 8,291; the *about* family was published at 225 and measured at 232, wrong in four of ten files, and the 0.81 rate derived from it was 0.83, **so the batch was congratulating itself on matching Batch 0004's rate when it had not matched it**; *about nine* was published at 12 and is 10. **And the margin over the 1,800-word floor was published as *three hundred and twenty words to spare on the worst file* and the worst file is 2,564, so the margin is seven hundred and sixty-four and three hundred and twenty follows from nothing.** All are corrected on the face of `state/batch-0020-summary.md` and in §5j. **THE FIGURE 225 IS THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE ITEM IN THE RECORD: after the age repair below the family is 225 again, by coincidence, from a different per-file distribution, and the agreement with Batch 0004's 0.81 is a false count in one file cancelling four unlawful *about* figures in another. IT IS PUBLISHED AS A COINCIDENCE AND NOT AS A REVERSAL. A TOTAL THAT LANDS ON A PUBLISHED FIGURE IS NOT CONFIRMATION; A TOTAL IS CONFIRMED BY ITS PER-FILE FIGURES ADDING UP.**
5. **A CHAPTER THAT STRADDLES THREE DAYS.** Chapter 0197 opened on Day 183, re-staged the water coming over the bank at its seventh hour — which Chapter 0196 had already done at the sixth hour of Day 182, and which Trope's forecast in that chapter says goes out on the day after — and then ran the low-quarter accounting *on the day after it went out* before returning to a Day 183 evening, so one chapter held Day 183 twice and Day 184 once. **The repair keeps every event and moves nothing: Day 183 is now the night the water is coming off the grass, which is what Trope's forecast requires, the accounting walk is at the seventh hour of Day 183 with the front row still wet and the water still in the rooms, and the ninth hour of the evening on the flat follows it. ONE CHAPTER, ONE DAY, DAYS 177 TO 186 IN TEN CONSECUTIVE DAYS, UNCHANGED, and §5j's hour list for Day 183 is still correct without amendment.**
6. **A NINE-YEAR FLASH-FORWARD, AND THE TWO SENTENCES THAT STOOD IN A ROW WITH IT.** A mould maker said he would like to be asked about the bay in about nine years, and the narrator then said out loud that nobody asked him then or in nine years and that it was not explained and was not going to be. **That is a promise about a thread this series requires to keep, in a voice this project does not use, and Volume 05 opens the chamber it gestures at.** Loxley now says he has said so out loud in a room with nine benches' people in it and not one of them has asked him; the narrator's sentence is now that nobody asked him and that there is no form in this kingdom on which a man in that room could have asked him and written down afterwards that he had; a habit about gates that was projected nine years forward is now stated as a habit and not a law; a line that had Tomas writing two more pairs of figures *in nine years* now has him writing them at the next bench he stands in; and Chapter 0200's day-book and its closing paragraph were changed with them, because both quoted the line that no longer exists. **THE RETROSPECTIVE *NINE YEARS* — A MAN'S TENURE, A BOOK, A WET SPRING — IS RETROSPECTIVE AND WAS LEFT ALONE IN ALL TEN FILES, and the fix is a fix of tense and not of a number.** **The frame reading itself was CHECKED AND CLEARED AGAINST `outline/ending.md` and stands: *answer frames* is ending-outline vocabulary, Loxley names nothing inside the bay, says he will not guess, and no chamber is opened, described, approached or named in any chapter of this volume.**

### What this pass found that the reviewer did not

7. **A DUPLICATED DAY-BOOK COLUMN IN CHAPTER 0197, WHICH ALL THREE DUPLICATE-PROSE TESTS IN THE BATCH'S OWN FILE PASSED.** The *Second column* entry and the *Second column, the sheet* entry were the same passage of Tomas's account printed twice with differences of wording, so the longest exact shared run was twenty-three words and every threshold in the file is set at twenty. **The sheet material was struck from the *Second column* entry and the labelled *Second column, the sheet* entry kept, so the day-book still carries three distinct things in its second column — the count in the margin, the sheet, the sentence — each once. THE LONGEST EXACT SHARED RUN IN THAT FILE FELL FROM TWENTY-THREE WORDS TO ELEVEN. This is the fourth time in this project a duplicate has got past a sweep, and it is the same failure every time: the duplicate was of another paragraph in the same file, which is exactly what test one exists to catch, and test one compares whole paragraphs, and these two differ by a handful of words. A duplicate test that counts only exact word runs is blind to a passage reprinted with its connectives altered, and a re-derived day-book is exactly where that happens, because the temptation is to reach for the sentence already written.**
8. **THE MARGIN OVER THE WORD FLOOR**, recorded at item 4 because it was found while correcting the other figures and is the same class of error: a number on the face of a file that does not follow from the number beside it.
9. **THIRTEEN AGES GIVEN TO NEW PEOPLE, IN TWO CHAPTERS, AGAINST §4, IN A BATCH WHOSE OWN SWEEP REPORTED CLEAN — AND THIS IS THE ONLY FINDING IN THE RECORD THAT IS NOT MECHANICAL.** §4 is binding: **a new person enters with no age, and a further recorded exception is a canon change no batch may make**, and on the Volume 02 precedent an age drafted into a chapter *was still removed*. **Chapter 0193 gave Wick, new in that chapter and new to the project, the age thirteen EIGHT TIMES** — *was a boy of thirteen called Wick*, *I am not going to send a boy of thirteen into three foot of moving water*, and six more including two in his own day-book. **Chapter 0195 gave an unnamed boy fifteen THREE TIMES, changed the same boy into *a man of fifteen* ONCE, and gave an unnamed woman *about seventy* FOUR TIMES.** Thirteen instances, all on new people, in a batch whose summary asserted **NO AGE IS GIVEN FOR ANYBODY, INCLUDING EVERY NEW PERSON IN THIS BATCH.** **EVERY FIGURE IS OUT: Wick is *a boy*; the boy in the river is *a boy*; the woman who got ninety foot up a bank and would not go the last fifty is ***an old woman***, which keeps the entire reason she sat down and reuses the word the chapter already applies to the old man who went up the steps first, because *old* is a description and not a number. NOTHING ELSE IN EITHER CHAPTER MOVES — Pell still will not send the leg, the boy still can swim and says so and it is not a question, the cold chisel still takes four minutes, and the reason Tomas does not follow him up the steps is now *because a boy who has just done a thing he has never done before does not need a stranger to come and talk to him about it*, which was always that sentence's point.** ONE AMBIGUITY WAS REPAIRED RATHER THAN REMOVED: Chapter 0194's *a woman of forty*, in a carter's mouth, read as forty years old and meant one of the forty people in that yard, and is now *a woman of forty people*, the yard having been a yard of about forty people since the chapter's title. **THE ONLY *YEARS OLD* LEFT IN THE TEN CHAPTERS IS *it is eleven years old*, in Chapter 0200, and it is about a clerk's sentence and not about a person. NO EXCEPTION WAS ADDED TO §4 AND NONE IS NEEDED. THE FIGURES THIRTEEN, FIFTEEN AND SEVENTY NO LONGER APPEAR AS AGES ANYWHERE IN CHAPTERS 191 TO 200.** The *about* family fell by four as a result, which is why item 4's figure is where it is.

### What this pass changed, and what it did not

**EIGHT CHAPTER FILES WERE OPENED — 0192, 0193, 0194, 0195, 0196, 0197, 0199 and 0200 — IN TWENTY-NINE PLACES, and the largest single change in any of them is one phrase and the smallest is one comma. The batch was not restarted. NO PLOT, EVENT, MOTIVE, REFUSAL, DOCUMENT, CUSTODY STATE, HOUR, DAY, TITLE, CARD, POSITION OR FIGURE OF THE PLOT CHANGED.** The reversal at 0176 was not re-earned and may not be. The 0145 seed stayed at two. The four nines were not added to. The register of non-bell documents was and is **thirteen**, and the state layer now says so at Chapter 200. No bell was rung, struck or bound by anybody in this batch and none is rung in the repairs. The First Answer chamber is not opened, described, approached or named anywhere, no person is sent to it, and **Volume 05 still finds Lira alive in it and not before.** The fitting on the cart bridge gate is still not opened, not followed and not named as a part. **The plot as planned is untouched, and the volume's resolution as planned stands: THE CANTONS STILL AGREE TO SHARE PHYSICAL REPAIRS AND REFUSE A CENTRAL COMMAND, THE NINE PLACES ARE STILL ENTERED AS PLACES PEOPLE LIVE ON A DISTRICT'S OWN FORM WITH NO NAME ON IT ANYWHERE, AND NOBODY IS THANKED.**

**AND THE STANDING LESSON, WHICH IS THE REVIEWER'S OWN LAST SENTENCE AND IS NOW THE INSTRUCTION FOR THE VOLUME 04 AUDIT: *the measurement apparatus is more confident than it is accurate.* This batch spent a hundred and forty lines measuring itself, published three headline numbers that were wrong, published a prose-cleanliness claim that was false on the page in five places, published an age claim that was false on the page thirteen times, and every sweep in its own file reported zero. A sweep that returns zero tells you what it looked for and nothing else. §10j found the same thing in the Volume 03 audit, §10o and §10q found it twice more, and this is the sixth time, and the finding that would have caught four of the six is the cheapest one available and was not run: a person reading the finished file.**

**THE ONE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-04/audit/PROMPT.md`, and it is the only phase file this pass created. It carries the Volume 04 close and audit, the Volume 03 precedent at `workspace/volume-03/audit/PROMPT.md`, and the six lessons above as required method rather than as encouragement. NO VOLUME 05 FILE WAS CREATED AND NO OUTLINE WAS REWRITTEN, and `state/phase-ledger.json` was not touched, and `outline/volume-04.md` needed no amendment in this pass because the batch did not disagree with it.** **AND THAT SENTENCE IS TRUE OF §10r AND OF NOTHING LATER, AND IT IS CORRECTED HERE SO THAT NO LATER PASS CAN MISREAD IT AS A STATEMENT ABOUT THE TREE: THE VOLUME-CLOSE PHASE HAS NOW RUN, `reviews/volume-04/volume-audit.md` EXISTS AND THE AUDIT PASSED, `outline/volume-05.md` AND `outline/batches/volume-05-batch-0001.md` EXIST, `outline/volume-04.md` HAS BEEN AMENDED IN FOUR PLACES BY THE AUDIT AND NOT BY THIS PASS, AND THE ONE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-05/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`. THE CLOSE IS §14.**
